#### V07-111 Concordance and Quotation: plain explanations under generated column names (#205 item 3)

- Commits: a2c7bd14
- Area: Concordance tables (preview, Run, dispersion), Quotation table, Add to Project dialog column list
- Type: wording
- Problem / reason: headers showed internal names like "CONC_left_context" and "QUOTE_extraction". Agreed decision: keep the stored names (they are the column names in created Data Blocks and exports) and put a small grey plain explanation under each; same in the Add to Project column list. Stored names and prefixes stay as they are in v0.7.
- What changed: new map `GENERATED_COLUMN_EXPLANATIONS` (exact):
  - CONC_left_context "Text before the match"; CONC_matched_text "The match"; CONC_right_context "Text after the match"; CONC_start_idx "Where the match starts (character)"; CONC_end_idx "Where the match ends (character)"; CONC_l1 "Word just before (L1)"; CONC_r1 "Word just after (R1)"; CONC_l1_freq "How often that L1 word occurs"; CONC_r1_freq "How often that R1 word occurs"; CONC_dispersion "Where the matches occur in the document"; CONC_extraction "The matches found in this document".
  - QUOTE_extraction "Document, with quotes highlighted"; QUOTE_speaker "Who is quoted"; QUOTE_speaker_start_idx "Where the speaker starts (character)"; QUOTE_speaker_end_idx "Where the speaker ends (character)"; QUOTE_quote "The quote"; QUOTE_quote_start_idx "Where the quote starts (character)"; QUOTE_quote_end_idx "Where the quote ends (character)"; QUOTE_verb "Reporting verb, such as said"; QUOTE_verb_start_idx "Where the verb starts (character)"; QUOTE_verb_end_idx "Where the verb ends (character)"; QUOTE_quote_type "How the quote was found"; QUOTE_quote_token_count "Words in the quote"; QUOTE_is_floating_quote "Continues the previous quote"; QUOTE_quote_row_idx "Quote number in the document".
  - `GeneratedColumnLabel` renders the stored name with the explanation beneath in small grey text (`aria-hidden`, so the accessible name stays the stored column name). Other columns show just their name. In the Add to Project dialog the explanation appears beside the column name.
- Expected behaviour (testable): the Concordance header "CONC_left_context" has "Text before the match" beneath it; screen readers read only "CONC_left_context"; a user column without an entry shows no second line.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/common/generatedColumns.ts`, `common/components/GeneratedColumnLabel.tsx` (new), `common/components/AddToWorkspaceDialog.tsx`, `concordance/components/ConcordanceRowsTable.tsx`, `ConcordanceDispersionRowsTable.tsx`, `SortableHeader.tsx`, `quotation/components/QuotationNodeBlock.tsx`.
- Tests: none added.
- v0.8 notes: if v0.8 keeps the `CONC_`/`QUOTE_` column names, reuse this map; if it renames them, keep a plain explanation per generated column.

#### V07-112 One plain vocabulary for data types (#205 item 4)

- Commits: a1c5d17e (builds on #206 in 3fda1f28)
- Area: Data Editor type button and menu, Filter column picker, Aggregate, Join errors, help
- Type: wording
- Problem / reason: raw Arrow/Polars spellings leaked ("Int32", "Float32", "List<Utf8View>", "Struct<...>", "Timestamp<MICROSECOND>", "org.ldaca.wordflow.topic_coverage.v1"), and three vocabularies were in use (Data Editor, Aggregate's "text/number/date/boolean/other", type menu's "categorical"/"datetime").
- What changed:
  - `arrowTypeDisplayName(field)` now always returns one of: "text", "category", "whole number", "decimal", "date and time", "date", "time of day", "length of time", "true / false", "list of text", "list", "topic coverage", "other" (dictionary to category; any Int to whole number; Float or Decimal to decimal; Timestamp to date and time; Date to date; Time to time of day; Duration/Interval to length of time; Bool; list of strings to list of text; other lists to list; the topic coverage extension; else other). No raw fallback.
  - New `arrowTypeTooltip(field)`: capitalised plain name, plus the exact spelling in brackets when the type is not one of Wordflow's standard storage types (`Utf8View`, `Dictionary<Uint32, Utf8View>`, `Int64`, `Float64`, `Timestamp<MICROSECOND, UTC>`, `Date32<DAY>`, `Bool`), for example "Whole number (Int32)". Used for the Data Editor type button tooltip.
  - Type menu label "categorical" to "category" (value stays `categorical`).
  - Aggregate lists each column with the same plain type name (`typeLabel`) instead of its kind.
  - Backend join errors (`_join_dtype_label`): text, category, whole number, decimal, true / false, date, date and time, time, list, other. Message examples: '"tweet_id" is a whole number column in the first Data Block and "party" is a text column in the second. Choose columns of the same type, or change one column's type in the Data Editor first.'; same-label case '"a" and "b" are both {label} columns, but they are stored differently.'; "a column of another type" for other. Missing columns: 'The first Data Block has no column "{left_on}".' / 'The second Data Block has no column "{right_on}".' (old 'Join left column "x" was not found').
  - Help pages (preprocessing, sequential-analysis, ui) and type menu screenshot updated.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - An Int32 column's type tooltip reads "Whole number (Int32)"; an Int64 column reads "Whole number".
  - A struct column reads "other"; a topic coverage column reads "topic coverage".
  - The type menu shows "Category".
  - Joining a whole number column to a text column shows the new sentence.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/lib/arrow/arrowTable.ts` (`plainTypeName`, `arrowTypeTooltip`, `EXTENSION_DISPLAY_NAMES`, `STANDARD_TYPE_SPELLINGS`), `WorkspaceColumnHeader.tsx` (`typeTooltip` prop), `WorkspaceTable.tsx`, `schemaMutations.ts`, `preprocessing/DataPreprocessingFeature.tsx`, `builder/builderTypes.ts`, `summarise/GroupSummarySubTab.tsx`; backend `services/node_operations.py` (`_join_dtype_label`, `_with_article`).
- Tests: `arrowTable.test.ts`, `WorkspaceTable.test.tsx`, `SequentialAnalysisParameterPanel.test.tsx`, backend `test_canonical_node_api.py`.
- v0.8 notes: in v0.8 the vocabulary must be mapped from DuckDB types (VARCHAR to text, ENUM to category, TINYINT..HUGEINT to whole number, FLOAT/DOUBLE/DECIMAL to decimal, TIMESTAMP(TZ) to date and time, DATE, TIME to time of day, INTERVAL to length of time, BOOLEAN, LIST/ARRAY of VARCHAR to list of text, STRUCT/MAP to other); keep exact type spellings only in tooltips.

#### V07-115 Say "Data Block", not "node", wherever users read it (#205 item 7)

### Frequency and Keyword Analysis

#### V07-008 Frequency corpus roles as "Use as Study Corpus" toggles (#118, docs for #115)

- Commits: 74a0e7f3, a0c444f7
- Area: Frequency, Docs
- Type: behaviour change (wording/UI) + docs
- Problem / reason: In two-corpus comparison, each selected-node card showed "Study Corpus / switch / Reference Corpus", leaving users to work out which side was on.
- What changed: Each card shows one switch labelled "Use as Study Corpus" (a clickable `<label htmlFor>`; accessible name `Use <corpus label> as Study Corpus`). The two toggles are linked through the single `studyNodeId`, so exactly one is on: the on corpus is the Study Corpus, the other the Reference Corpus. Turning a toggle on makes that block the study corpus; turning the active toggle off hands the study role to the other block (a comparison always has a study/reference pair). The first corpus is the default study corpus. Label text is foreground when on, description colour when off. The bundled tutorial `frontend/public/tutorials/token-frequency.md` (Step 2 and the walkthrough steps 3 and 4) now describes the toggles and that right-clicking a cloud word switches the stop words filter on automatically. Publication to ldaca-wordflow-docs deferred to the v0.7 docs release (#119).
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - With two blocks selected, the first card's "Use as Study Corpus" is on and the second is off.
  - Turning on the second card turns off the first.
  - Turning off the active card turns on the other.
  - Clicking the label text toggles the switch.
  - Statistics: the reference block is O1/%1 and the study block O2/%2 (unchanged semantics).
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/token-frequency/components/panels/TokenFrequencyParameterPanel.tsx` (`CorpusRoleSwitch`).
- Tests: `TokenFrequencyParameterPanel.test.tsx` (default, linking, turn-off hands role to other, clickable label).
- v0.8 notes: Keep one "Use as Study Corpus" toggle per card with the linked exactly-one semantics.

#### V07-053 Tokenizer list: language and project link; hide ja-ipadic-neologd (#167)

- Commits: 17b67e9c
- Area: Token Frequency (and every tool using TokenizerModelSelector)
- Type: bug fix / feature
- Problem / reason: `lindera:ja-ipadic-neologd` always failed ("Failed to read Lindera dictionary response") because its 305 MB dictionary archive exceeds the polars-text 128 MB download cap (`MAX_ARCHIVE_BYTES`); raising it is Rust work out of scope for v0.7. Users also could not tell which language each tokenizer was for.
- What changed: The catalogue endpoint hides `lindera:ja-ipadic-neologd` and adds `docs_url` per tokenizer. Each option shows its label, a language chip (English names via `Intl.DisplayNames`), and the model id; the trigger shows "Label · Language"; an external-link icon beside the dropdown ("About <label> (opens in a new tab)") opens the selected tokenizer's page.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - The list has no ja-ipadic-neologd; `lindera:ja-ipadic` and `lindera:ja-unidic` remain for Japanese.
  - Selecting BERT shows the link to https://huggingface.co/google-bert/bert-base-uncased.
  - Docs URLs: native:plain_words_en to the polars-text GitHub repo; lindera:jieba to github.com/fxsjy/jieba; lindera:cc-cedict to the MDBG CC-CEDICT page; lindera:ja-ipadic to taku910.github.io/mecab/; lindera:ja-unidic to clrd.ninjal.ac.jp/unidic/; lindera:ko-dic to bitbucket.org/eunjeon/mecab-ko-dic.
- Implementation: `HIDDEN_TOKENIZER_MODELS`, `TOKENIZER_DOCS_URLS` in `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/api/tokenizers.py`; `TokenizerModelResource.docs_url` in `models/tokenizer.py`; `TokenizerModelSelector.tsx` (`TokenizerOptionText`, `languageNames`); `frontendModels.ts`.
- Tests: `backend/tests/unit/test_tokenizer_catalogue.py::test_catalogue_hides_unloadable_models_and_links_each_to_its_docs`; `TokenizerModelSelector.behaviour.test.tsx`.
- v0.8 notes: The hide is a v0.7 workaround for the polars-text download cap; v0.8 can re-enable NEologd if the cap is raised. Note (from #171 follow-up 3fa0e130, outside this range): the desktop app needed `opener:allow-open-url` for these `target="_blank"` links to work.

#### V07-054 Keyness: measure Study against the Reference baseline (#168)

- Commits: 7630163c
- Area: Token Frequency (Keyword Analysis)
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Two user reports. The worker passed Reference as corpus_0 and Study as corpus_1 to `polars_text.token_frequency_stats`, so %DIFF, relative risk, log ratio and odds ratio treated Study as the baseline, contrary to convention (Gabrielatos %DIFF, Hardie Log Ratio). Overuse and Signed LL compared raw counts, so with corpora of different sizes the direction could be wrong. The LogRatio tooltip said natural log (it is log2); the %DIFF tooltip omitted the division; the tutorial described old O1/O2 columns and gave no formulas.
- What changed: Study is corpus_0 and Reference corpus_1, with the result columns mapped back to `*_study` / `*_reference`. The requested block order is kept when one block is tokenized and the other is raw text (a merged-dict ordering could swap them). Overuse = %S > %R; Signed LL = +|LL| for overuse else -|LL|. Tooltips rewritten (quoted exactly): Overuse "Which Data Block uses the token more, relative to its size: Study (overuse) or Reference (underuse)."; Signed LL "The log-likelihood score, positive when the token is relatively more frequent in Study and negative when it is relatively more frequent in Reference."; %DIFF "How much more (or less) frequent the token is in Study than in Reference: (Study − Reference) ÷ Reference relative frequency × 100. 0 means equal."; RRisk "Study relative frequency divided by Reference relative frequency; 1 means equal relative frequency."; LogRatio "Binary logarithm (log2) of the Study-to-Reference relative-frequency ratio; 0 means equal, 1 means twice as frequent in Study."; OddsRatio "Study token odds divided by Reference token odds; 1 means equal odds." The tutorial gains a column table and a "How the statistics are calculated" section (LL per Rayson and Garside 2000 with critical values 3.84, 6.63, 10.83, 15.13; %DIFF per Gabrielatos and Marchi 2012; Bayes BIC = LL - ln(N) per Wilson 2013; ELL per Johnston et al. 2006; LogRatio with zero frequency counted as 0.5 per Hardie 2014; OddsRatio formula), and notes that results created before 0.7.8 have reversed ratio columns and should be rerun.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - A token relatively more frequent in Study has %DIFF > 0, RRisk > 1, LogRatio > 0, OddsRatio > 1, Overuse "Study", Signed LL > 0.
  - A token with more raw hits in a larger Reference block but a higher %S is marked Study overuse.
  - Mixed tokenized/raw pair keeps [Reference, Study] roles as selected.
- Implementation: column map and `node_order` in `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/workers/token_frequency.py` (`_compute_token_frequencies`, `run_token_frequency_analysis`); `enhanceRows` and `STATISTICS_COLUMN_TOOLTIPS` in `TokenFrequencyStatisticsTable.tsx`; `tutorials/token-frequency.md`.
- Tests: `backend/tests/unit/test_token_frequency_worker.py::test_keyness_measures_study_against_the_reference_baseline`; `TokenFrequencyResultsLayout.test.tsx` (tooltips, "labels frequency direction by relative frequency, not raw counts (issue 168)").
- v0.8 notes: No polars-text change; the fix is argument order. Stored results from before 0.7.8 keep the old direction for the four ratio columns. Reuse the tutorial's formula section as the spec for any DuckDB reimplementation.

#### V07-058 Token Frequency: frequency per million words (#172)

- Commits: 159b2e10, d1f2a1b3 (follow-up 39d93a3d rounds to whole numbers, outside this range)
- Area: Token Frequency
- Type: feature, then bug fix
- Problem / reason: User feedback asked for normalised frequency per million; the docs told users to compute it in Excel. After release, Per million showed 0 for every token because Arrow Int64 counts reach the frontend as strings and summing them concatenated text into a huge number.
- What changed: The frequency list has a header row "Token", "Count", "Per million" (title "Occurrences per million tokens (N tokens in this block)"); Count uses thousands separators; Per million = count / total tokens in the block x 1,000,000, shown with up to 2 decimals at this commit. Total tokens is the sum over all tokens in the block before stop-word and token-filter filtering, so neither changes it, and existing results show it without rerun. CSV and Markdown downloads add `per_million` / "Per million". d1f2a1b3 converts `frequency` to a number when Result rows are read.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - A token with count 5 in a block of 1,000 tokens shows Per million 5,000.
  - Hiding stop words does not change any Per million value.
  - CSV header is `word,count,per_million` for the frequency list download.
  - String counts ("3") are summed numerically.
- Implementation: `perMillion`, `totalTokens`, `withNumericFrequencies` in `tokenFrequencyAdapters.ts`; `TokenFrequencySingleTokenSection.tsx` (`listGridColumns`, `countFormat`, `perMillionFormat`); `tokenFrequencyExport.ts` (`PER_MILLION_COLUMN`); docs `information/token-frequency.md`, `tutorials/token-frequency.md`.
- Tests: `tokenFrequencyExport.test.ts`, `TokenFrequencyResultsLayout.test.tsx`, `useTokenFrequencyListLimit.test.tsx`, `tokenFrequencyAdapters.test.ts` (string counts regression).
- v0.8 notes: The Int64-as-string behaviour comes from the Arrow JS decoder (bigint serialised as text); any v0.8 frontend summing Arrow Int64 must coerce. Final rounding is whole numbers (39d93a3d).

#### V07-076 Token Frequency: Per million as whole numbers (#172 follow-up)

- Commits: 39d93a3d
- Area: Frequency
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: After the string-count bug (Arrow Int64 counts arriving as strings, fixed in d1f2a1b3) was fixed, per million values were shown with up to two decimals; whole numbers are clearer.
- What changed: `perMillion(frequency, totalTokens)` returns `Math.round(count / total × 1,000,000)`; the list uses the integer count formatter; the frequency download uses the same rounded value. Docs: "rounded to a whole number".
- Expected behaviour (testable): A token with 642,571.43 per million shows "642,571" in the list and `642571` in the download.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/token-frequency/tokenFrequencyAdapters.ts` (`perMillion`), `components/results/TokenFrequencySingleTokenSection.tsx`, `public/tutorials/token-frequency.md`.
- Tests: `tokenFrequencyAdapters.test.ts`, `TokenFrequencyResultsLayout.test.tsx`.
- v0.8 notes: Per million is computed from all tokens in the block (stop words and the token filter do not change it; see #172). Convert Int64 counts to numbers before summing.

#### V07-086 Frequency: results and cards keep the parameter-card order, also after a reload (#193, #198)

- Commits: 7676af86, 388115f4
- Area: Frequency
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: (#193) With two blocks, word clouds and other per-corpus sections showed in reverse card order, because the result model received the comparison order sent to the backend, always [Reference, Study] (regression from 43be3676 after a55bf5f3 added card order). (#198) After a reload or new browser session, the cards themselves swapped to Reference first, because card order lived only in memory and hydration fell back to the saved request's [Reference, Study] order.
- What changed:
  - The result model gets the parameter-card selections (`nodeColumnSelections`), so result sections follow the cards; `deriveResultDisplayNodeIds` still shows any result node whose card was removed. Keyness statistics and downloads keep the comparison order.
  - The card order is stored as a tab setting `tokenFrequency.cardOrder` (JSON array of node ids) whenever there are at least two cards, and restored on hydration. If the stored order does not cover every request node (e.g. nothing remembered), Study goes first (the reverse of [Reference, Study]), where a new comparison starts it.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Add A then B (A is Study), run: cards, word clouds and lists show A then B.
  - Reload: still A then B, Study toggle still on A.
  - Same with Study on the second card.
  - With no remembered order, Study is first.
  - `parseTokenFrequencyCardOrder('not json')` is `[]`; `'["a",2,"b"]'` gives `['a','b']`.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/token-frequency/TokenFrequencyFeature.tsx` (`useTokenFrequencyResultModel` call; hydration; `useEffect` writing the setting via `host.setSetting`), `tokenFrequencyUtils.ts` (`TOKEN_FREQUENCY_CARD_ORDER_SETTING`, `parseTokenFrequencyCardOrder`, `orderHydratedTokenFrequencyNodeIds`). Tab settings are stored on the workspace Tab resource (`setTabSetting` in `AnalysisTabsHost`).
- Tests: `tokenFrequencyUtils.test.ts` ("follows the remembered card order", "puts Study first when no card order is remembered"); none for 7676af86.
- v0.8 notes: The request's comparison order and the display order are different concepts; persist display order with the tab. Checked in WebKit.

#### V07-090 Frequency: %DIFF as a percentage; N/A without Reference hits (#197)

- Commits: 6101fb8f
- Area: Frequency (Keyword Analysis table)
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: polars-text already returns `percent_diff` as a percentage (e.g. -32.4), but the table multiplied by 100 again (showing -3240%), since the 26 August frontend refactor. When a token never occurs in the Reference, polars-text divides by 1e-18 instead of 0, giving values like 2796491674081606656.00%.
- What changed: %DIFF shows the value as returned with 2 decimals and "%"; when `freq_reference` is 0 it shows "N/A" (same as LogRatio). The `multiplier` option was removed from `formatNumber`.
- Expected behaviour (testable): A token at 0.38% in Study and 0.56% in Reference shows "-32.40%"; a token with Reference count 0 shows "N/A".
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/token-frequency/components/results/TokenFrequencyStatisticsTable.tsx` (`formatNumber`, `enhanceRows` sets `sort_percent_diff` to NaN when Reference count is 0; the column cell now reads `sort_percent_diff`).
- Tests: `TokenFrequencyResultsLayout.test.tsx` ("shows %DIFF as a percentage and N/A without Reference hits").
- v0.8 notes: If v0.8 computes keyness itself (e.g. in SQL), define %DIFF = (normStudy − normRef) / normRef × 100 and return NULL when the Reference count is 0 rather than dividing by a tiny epsilon. Unclear from the diff: sort order of NaN rows in the column, and whether the keyness download also replaces the huge value (only the table was changed).

#### V07-118 Keyword Analysis: plain tooltips, a significance legend, CSV headers that match (#205 item 12)

- Commits: abdfe9e6
- Area: Frequency, Keyword Analysis statistics table and CSV download
- Type: wording
- Problem / reason: "OR" clashed with "OddsRatio"; tooltips were technical; thresholds and "n.s." were unexplained on screen; CSV headers ("E_<name>", raw keys like `log_likelihood_llv`) did not match the screen and "E_" was never explained. Agreed: keep the abbreviations, improve tooltips.
- What changed:
  - Header tooltips (each followed by " Click to sort."):
    - token "The word (token) being compared."
    - OR "OR: how many times the token occurs in the Reference corpus."; %R "%R: the token's share of all tokens in the Reference corpus."; OS "OS: how many times the token occurs in the Study corpus."; %S "%S: the token's share of all tokens in the Study corpus."
    - LL "LL (log-likelihood): how sure we can be that the difference is real. Above 3.84 is significant (p < 0.05); above 15.13, p < 0.0001."
    - Overuse "Overuse: which corpus uses the token more, for its size: Study or Reference."
    - Signed LL "Signed LL: LL with a sign, positive when the token is more frequent in Study and negative when it is more frequent in Reference."
    - %DIFF "%DIFF: how much more (or less) frequent the token is in Study than in Reference, as a percentage. 0 means the same; N/A means the token never occurs in Reference."
    - Bayes "Bayes (Bayes factor, BIC): how strong the evidence for a difference is. Above 2 is positive, above 6 strong, above 10 very strong."
    - ELL "ELL (effect size for log-likelihood): how big the difference is, allowing for the corpus sizes. Unlike LL, it does not grow just because the corpora are larger."
    - RRisk "RRisk (relative risk): Study relative frequency divided by Reference. 1 means the same; 2 means twice as frequent in Study."
    - LogRatio "LogRatio: RRisk on a log2 scale. 0 means the same; 1 twice as frequent in Study; -1 half as frequent."
    - OddsRatio "OddsRatio: the odds of the token in Study divided by its odds in Reference. 1 means the same."
    - Significance "Significance, from LL: **** p < 0.0001, *** p < 0.001, ** p < 0.01, * p < 0.05, n.s. not significant."
  - Legend below the table: "Significance, from LL: **** p < 0.0001 (LL above 15.13), *** p < 0.001 (above 10.83), ** p < 0.01 (above 6.63), * p < 0.05 (above 3.84), n.s. not significant."
  - CSV headers: `OR_{ref}`, `OS_{study}`, `%R_{ref}`, `%S_{study}`, `Expected_{ref}` and `Expected_{study}` (was `E_…`), `Total_{ref}`, `Total_{study}`, `Token`, `LL`, `Overuse`, `Signed_LL`, `%DIFF`, `Bayes`, `ELL`, `RRisk`, `LogRatio`, `OddsRatio`, `Significance`; internal `sort_*` columns are left out.
  - Help token-frequency.md: LL row, Significance row with **n.s.**, and a paragraph explaining CSV names, `Expected_…` ("the count the token would have in that corpus if both corpora used it equally") and `Total_…`.
  - "Study corpus" and "Reference corpus" are kept as terms of art.
- Expected behaviour (testable): hovering "LL" shows the LL sentence ending "Click to sort."; the legend line is under the table; a downloaded CSV has `Expected_<name>` and no `sort_` columns.
- Implementation: `token-frequency/components/results/TokenFrequencyStatisticsTable.tsx` (`STATISTICS_COLUMN_TOOLTIPS`), `token-frequency/hooks/useTokenFrequencyDownloads.ts` (`renameStatisticsKeysForExport`).
- Tests: `TokenFrequencyResultsLayout.test.tsx`, `useTokenFrequencyDownloads.test.tsx`.
- v0.8 notes: the statistics are computed in Polars in v0.7; if v0.8 computes them in DuckDB SQL, keep the same column names, formulas (Rayson's calculator) and thresholds.

#### V07-259 Tokeniser picker: Plain words is for any language with spaces, not only English (#339)

- Commits: 7c8626f2
- Area: Frequency and Keyword Analysis
- Type: interface change
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-08: "Plain words (English)" is not English-specific: it splits at spaces and punctuation (BERT's basic pre-tokeniser) with no trained vocabulary, unlike BERT base uncased, which is trained on English. Tagged English only, it was never recommended or picked automatically for French, Indonesian and other space-separated languages.
- What changed:
  - `api/tokenizers.py`: `TOKENIZER_CATALOGUE_OVERRIDES` gives `native:plain_words_en` the label "Plain words" and languages `["*"]`; polars-text's registry is unchanged until its next release.
  - `lib/languages.ts`: `ANY_SPACED_LANGUAGE` (`*`) models are recommended for every detected language not in `LANGUAGES_WITHOUT_SPACES` (zh, ja, ko, th, lo, km, my, bo); catalogue order is kept, so Plain words stays the first (automatic) choice for English and becomes it for other spaced languages.
  - `TokenizerModelSelector.tsx`: `*` shows as "Any language with spaces". Help `token-frequency.md` explains Plain words and BERT.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Picker: "Plain words · Any language with spaces".
  - Recommended: English Plain words then BERT; French and Indonesian Plain words; Chinese CC-CEDICT and Jieba; Thai none.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/api/tokenizers.py`; `frontend/src/lib/languages.ts`, `TokenizerModelSelector.tsx`; `public/tutorials/token-frequency.md`.
- Tests: `test_tokenizer_catalogue.py`; `languages.test.ts` (any-spaced recommendation).
- v0.8 notes: Name and tag the plain tokeniser as language-neutral for spaced scripts; consider imported language-specific tokenisers (#340).

### Stop words and word cloud

#### V07-004 Word cloud right-click opened the native context menu (#116)

- Commits: 939d0e02
- Area: Word cloud (shared `ResponsiveWordCloud`, used by Frequency and Topic Modelling)
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Right-clicking a word to add a stop word also opened the browser/webview context menu. zrender's native listener runs first and the handler adds the stop word; React flushes the update between listeners and the cloud effect's `chart.clear()` detaches the SVG target, so React's delegated `onContextMenu` could no longer resolve the target and its `preventDefault` never ran. With stop words disabled the handler was a no-op, which is why only enabled clouds showed the menu.
- What changed: The cloud cancels the native menu in a native capture-phase `contextmenu` listener on the chart element (runs before zrender), only when a word context-menu handler is supplied. The React `onContextMenu` prop was removed. Clouds without a right-click action (e.g. Topic Modelling) still show the native menu.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Right-clicking a word in the Frequency cloud adds the stop word and no native context menu appears (browser and desktop app).
  - On a cloud with no right-click action, the native menu still appears.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/common/components/ResponsiveWordCloud.tsx` (`element.addEventListener('contextmenu', suppressNativeContextMenu, true)`, removed in cleanup; reads `wordContextMenuRef.current`).
- Tests: `common/components/__tests__/ResponsiveWordCloud.test.tsx` (detaches the word during dispatch; fails on old code).
- v0.8 notes: If the chart library re-renders synchronously on right-click, prevent the native menu in a capture-phase native listener, not a React synthetic handler.

#### V07-005 Frequency "Enable stop words" toggle remembered in tab settings; right-click enables it (#117, #115)

- Commits: a4ba8dc9, 684d4ff0
- Area: Frequency, Stop words
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: (#117) The "Enable stop words" toggle lived in local `useState` keyed to the current result, so switching to another analysis tool unmounted the feature and turned the filter off. (#115) Right-clicking a word in the cloud or token list did nothing while the toggle was off, because a no-op handler was passed in that state.
- What changed:
  - The toggle is stored in tab presentation settings as `tokenFrequency.stopWordsEnabled` (string `'true'`/`'false'`; missing or malformed means disabled), the same store as the token limit (`tokenFrequency.tokenLimit`), persisted per user/workspace/tab on the device. It survives tool switches, reloads and new runs until the user turns it off. Still gated on a result existing (`frequencyResultKey !== null`).
  - Right-clicking a word (cloud or token list) now switches the toggle on (in tab settings) if it was off, then adds the token as a stop word. The handler is memoised (`useCallback`) so the word cloud sections are not re-laid out every render.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Turn on "Enable stop words", switch to another tool and back: still on. Reload: still on. Run again: still on.
  - With the toggle off, right-clicking a word turns the toggle on and the word appears in the stop-word list.
- Implementation: new `frontend/src/features/views/token-frequency/hooks/stopWordsToggle.ts` (`STOP_WORDS_ENABLED_SETTING`, `readStopWordsEnabled`, `addStopWordEnablingFilter`); `TokenFrequencyFeature.tsx` (`host.setSetting`, `handleStopWordRightClick`). The helper is later shared with Topic Modelling (465b4c57).
- Tests: `token-frequency/hooks/__tests__/stopWordsToggle.test.ts`.
- v0.8 notes: The stop words enabled state is a per-tab persisted setting, not per-result state. Right-click must never be silently ignored.

#### V07-006 Word cloud sizing fills the pane, frequent words compressed, circle mask kept (#114, #123)

- Commits: 74d66e7b, bbc4d7ce, 93338b63, ec9d72e0
- Area: Word cloud (shared `ResponsiveWordCloud`; Frequency and Topic Modelling bubble clouds)
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: After the move to echarts-wordcloud the cloud looked small: the series set no `sizeRange`, so words used the extension's fixed 12-60px range at any size and the circle left corners empty (#114). Iterations from review: height-only scaling still left margins for fewer words; then the most frequent words ("the", "and") were too large and large neighbours touched ("toof"); then #123 asked to restore the rounded circle mask.
- What changed (final state after ec9d72e0):
  - Series values given to echarts are `sqrt(count)` (`wordCloudSizingValue`), the usual word-cloud compression. Displayed counts, aria labels and exports keep exact counts.
  - `wordCloudLayoutSize({width, height, words})` returns `{sizeRange, gridSize}`, solved from the actual words: estimate each word's padded box (CJK/Hangul/fullwidth `[、-鿿가-힯＀-￯]` = 1em per char, others 0.6em; line height 1.1em; padding = gridSize each side) and binary-search (20 steps) for the range whose total area reaches `TARGET_FILL = 1.5` times the canvas area (exceeds 1 because layout packs at pixel level).
  - Largest font capped at height / 3 (floor 24px). Default smallest font = largest / 6 (floor 10px). When the cap binds (words fit at the cap), the smallest font grows instead, up to largest / 3, so short lists still fill the pane without flattening the hierarchy. Otherwise the largest font is searched between 24px and the cap.
  - Word spacing (`gridSize`) = largest font / 10, at least 4px.
  - Mask: `shape: 'circle'` with `keepAspect: false` (circle stretched to the pane). 74d66e7b briefly switched to `'square'`; ec9d72e0 restored the circle.
  - Range recomputed on every layout pass, so words grow and shrink with the pane when resized. `shrinkToFit: true` still guards words that would not fit; `drawOutOfBound: false`; no rotation.
  - Intermediate states (not final): 74d66e7b used height/5 max, max/6 min, square mask; bbc4d7ce used a 1.4 fill target and cap height/2 with linear raw counts.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - For 10 to 100 words at 1000x600 (Zipf-like counts), every word is placed and the cloud spans about 88-97% of the pane at its widest and tallest (measured in headless Chromium with the real layout).
  - The largest word is at most one third of the canvas height.
  - Resizing the pane rescales words.
  - The cloud outline is rounded (ellipse stretched to the pane).
  - Topic Modelling bubble clouds get the same sizing (small clouds use the 24px/10px floors).
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/common/components/wordCloudLayoutSize.ts` (replaces `wordCloudSizeRange.ts`, deleted in 93338b63); `ResponsiveWordCloud.tsx` series options.
- Tests: `common/components/__tests__/ResponsiveWordCloud.test.tsx` (size range, grid size, sqrt values, circle shape).
- v0.8 notes: The constants were tuned by screenshot comparison in headless Chromium; if v0.8 uses a different cloud library, re-tune to the same observable targets (fills about 90% of the pane, all words placed, cap H/3, sqrt compression, round outline).

#### V07-007 Word cloud stops flashing when stop words change (#124)

- Commits: 82edd69f
- Area: Word cloud
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Every relayout clears the chart and waits for echarts-wordcloud's deferred layout pass, leaving the pane blank. A single stop-word right-click caused several relayouts: (1) the genuine change; (2) the saved list echoing back through `savedStopWordsJson`, which re-applies the same words as a new Set so the section rebuilds an identical words array; (3) any re-render of the per-node wrapper, whose inline `svgRef` was a layout effect dependency.
- What changed: The cloud relays out only when word content (text, count, colour), canvas size or colour changes: words are keyed by a JSON content signature (`wordsSignature` -> `stableWords` via `useMemo`), and `svgRef` is read through a ref (`svgRefRef`). For a genuine change, a clone of the previous SVG frame is held in an absolutely positioned, `aria-hidden`, pointer-events-none overlay (`data-testid="word-cloud-previous-frame"`) until the new layout draws its words (a `MutationObserver` waiting for `svg text`), with a 1.5 s fallback (`OVERLAY_MAX_HOLD_MS = 1500`).
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Right-clicking a word to add it as a stop word never shows a blank cloud pane.
  - An identical words array (same content, new identity) does not trigger a relayout.
  - Re-rendering the parent with a new inline `svgRef` callback does not trigger a relayout.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/common/components/ResponsiveWordCloud.tsx`.
- Tests: `ResponsiveWordCloud.test.tsx` (no relayout for identical content; previous frame held until redraw), both fail on old code.
- v0.8 notes: Keep relayouts content-driven and keep a previous-frame overlay (or equivalent double buffering) if the chart library clears before an async layout.

#### V07-009 Shared stop-words dropdown: append languages, other tabs' lists, Wordflow classic lists, detected language (#120, #121)

- Commits: 1e1d0afe, 958b222b, ccc52a2f, a04a742d
- Area: Stop words (Frequency and Topic Modelling), Docs
- Type: feature + behaviour change
- Problem / reason: (#120) Frequency's stop-word box had an "Apply Stop Words" button that repeated what leaving (blurring) the text box already does, and an "Add Default" dialog whose only job was picking a language; Topic Modelling already had an inline language dropdown, so the two tools differed. (#121) Users wanted to reuse a stop-word list built in another tab, and review asked to restore the classic Wordflow lists that the backend used to serve, and to shorten the ~60-language list.
- What changed (final state after a04a742d):
  - New shared `StopWordsLanguageSelect` used by both Frequency and Topic Modelling (trigger `aria-label="Stop words language"`, placeholder "Select language", width `w-56`).
  - Frequency: "Apply Stop Words" button removed (edits still apply on blur); "Add Default" button and its `FillDefaultStopWordsDialog` removed; the dropdown and the "Sort" button now sit beside the "Enable stop words" switch. Picking a list that adds words switches the stop words filter on (same as right-click). Frequency help tooltip text: "Words entered here are removed from the displayed token tables and comparison views after a run completes. Pick a language to append its default stop words (the detected language is marked Recommended), or right-click a word to add it. Edits apply when you leave the text box." (Note: this tooltip still says "Recommended" although the dropdown now says "(Detected)"; a wording inconsistency as of a04a742d, possibly fixed later.)
  - Every pick appends its words to the current list with duplicates skipped (`mergeStopWordsText`); Topic Modelling changes from replace to append. Custom words are kept.
  - Menu contents, in order:
    1. Action rows: "Clear stop words" (empties the list) and "Saved list (N words)" when a list exists; a disabled "Select language" row when the list is empty.
    2. "From other tabs" (hidden when empty): every other available Frequency or Topic Modeling tab with a non-empty saved list (`settings.stop_words.words`), labelled "<Tool> · <Tab name> (N words)" where Tool is "Frequency" or "Topic Modeling", sorted by label. Picking one appends a copy; tabs stay independent afterwards. No new storage: derived from the workspace tabs query, so entries appear/disappear with the tab's list and follow renames.
    3. "Wordflow classic lists": seven built-in lists restored verbatim from the backend's last version (old repo Australian-Text-Analytics-Platform/ldaca-wordflow-backend: commit 91278ed2 `src/ldaca_wordflow/resources/stopwords_*.txt`, removed in 9e9dbfc1 on 2026-05-28, previously served at `/api/text/default-stop-words`), displayed as "<Name> (N words)" in this order: Chinese 746, English 231 (manually revised by Monika, ldaca-wordflow-backend d3784a49), French 146 (NLTK subset), German 131 (largely NLTK, source not recorded), Japanese 310 (Kaggle lazon282), Korean 679 (stopwords-iso, MIT), Spanish 151 (NLTK subset). Loaded lazily (~25 KB chunk). (Later, per #119, the classic Chinese list was removed for licence reasons; outside this range.)
    4. "Languages (stopword library)": the `stopword` npm package (v3.1.5, about 60 languages). By default only the language detected from the column is shown, labelled "<Language> (Detected)" (was "(Recommended)"), followed by "Show all languages (N)" which expands the remaining languages (alphabetical) in place while the menu stays open; the list collapses again whenever the menu closes. While detection runs a disabled "Detecting language…" row is shown. Detection runs only while the menu is open.
  - Load failures show toast "Failed to load stop words." with the error as description.
  - Tutorials `frontend/public/tutorials/token-frequency.md` and `topic-modeling.md` updated for all of the above.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Frequency has no "Apply Stop Words" or "Add Default" buttons; "Sort" is next to the dropdown.
  - Picking "English (Detected)" twice does not duplicate words; custom words already in the list stay.
  - In Topic Modelling, picking a language appends rather than replaces.
  - With the Frequency filter off, picking a language turns "Enable stop words" on.
  - A second Frequency tab with a saved list appears under "From other tabs" as "Frequency · <tab name> (N words)"; clearing that tab's list removes the entry; the current tab never lists itself.
  - "Wordflow classic lists" shows the seven counts above and each pick appends exactly that many new words to an empty list.
  - Clicking "Show all languages (N)" keeps the menu open and shows all languages; closing and reopening shows the collapsed view again.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/common/components/StopWordsLanguageSelect.tsx` (Radix Select; `keepOpenRef` ignores the one close Radix fires after the expander pick; value prefixes `tab:` and `classic:`); `common/utils/stopWordListSources.ts` `buildStopWordListSources`; `common/hooks/useStopWordListSources.ts` `useStopWordListSources(workspaceId, tabId)`; `frontend/src/lib/wordflowClassicStopwords.ts` + `wordflowClassicStopwordsData.ts` (dynamic import); `frontend/src/lib/loadMergedStopwords.ts`; `common/hooks/useDetectedColumnLanguage.ts`; `TokenFrequencyResultsPanel.tsx`, `TokenFrequencyFeature.tsx` (`handleStopWordsListChange`), `TopicModelingStopWordsControl.tsx`, `TopicModelingResultsPanel.tsx`, `TopicModelingFeature.tsx`. Removed `FillDefaultStopWordsDialog.tsx` and the Add Default handler/loading state in `useTokenFrequencyPreferences.ts` / `tokenFrequencyPreferenceState.ts`.

#### V07-262 Clicks on a word inside another word reach that word (#343)

- Commits: ebdda3a9
- Area: Stop words and word cloud
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Monika (feedback, 2026-10-08, 0.7.10): words sit inside others in the word cloud ("it" inside the "h" of a large word). Chao: the layout is fine, as long as clicks are not caught by the wrong word, which made Concordance and Add stop word act on the wrong word. Cause: echarts-wordcloud gives every word ECharts' hover emphasis, which lifts the hovered word by z2; crossing a large word on the way to a small word inside its box left the large word on top, taking the click.
- What changed:
  - pnpm patch `patches/echarts-wordcloud@2.1.0.patch` (registered in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` patchedDependencies) sets `textEl.z2EmphasisLift = 0` on each word in `WordCloudView.js`. The cloud has no visible hover style, so nothing else changes; the layout is unchanged.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Clicking (or right-clicking) any word picks that word, also a small word inside a large word's box, whatever path the pointer took.
- Implementation: `patches/echarts-wordcloud@2.1.0.patch`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`.
- Tests: `ResponsiveWordCloud.test.tsx` guards the patch; WebKit harness with a real cloud (30-100 words): one wrong word per layout before, none after.
- v0.8 notes: Whatever draws the word cloud must hit-test the word under the pointer: no hover lift that puts a large word above smaller words placed inside its box.

### Concordance

#### V07-082 Concordance: dispersion markers line up with the selected range (#96)

- Commits: cd3df6f3
- Area: Concordance (Dispersion view)
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Selecting 30 to 70% in the dispersion chart, the per-document match markers did not line up vertically with the selection, worst for short texts such as tweets. Two causes: (1) the combined view passed no text column, so each row's length fell back to its last visible match; after a selection every row was rescaled to that match and markers drifted to about 85 to 100%; (2) lengths used JS UTF-16 `length` while match offsets and backend bins count code points, so each emoji shifted markers left (up to 22% of the bar in the QLD tweets sample).
- What changed:
  - Combined view rows resolve their own source's document column (from `__source_node` of the first hit or the row, mapped to that node's column selection).
  - Text length counts Unicode code points (`codePointLength`).
  - Each document row shades the selected bins behind its markers (`bg-focus/15`), in both combined and per-source views.
  - Tutorial: "Each document row shades the selected range, so its match markers line up with the selection (positions count characters, so emoji and other symbols count once)."
  - Not changed: the summary chart's plot area and the rows have slightly different horizontal insets (a few px).
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - In the combined view with two corpora, after selecting 30 to 70% a marker for a match at 50% of a tweet sits at 50% of its bar.
  - A text with emoji places markers at the same relative position as the backend bins.
  - Selected bins appear as a shaded range on every row.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/concordance/concordanceDispersionDomain.ts` (`DispersionTextColumn` = string or per-row function, `codePointLength`, `getDispersionTextLength`, `getDispersionBarWidthPercent`), `components/ConcordanceDispersionNodeBlock.tsx` (`combinedTextColumn`), `ConcordanceDispersionRowsTable.tsx`, `ConcordanceDispersionCell.tsx` (`selectedRuns`), `concordanceDispersionTableModel.ts`.
- Tests: `concordanceDispersionLength.test.ts`, `ConcordanceDispersionCell.test.tsx` ("shades the selected bins behind the markers"), `ConcordanceDispersionNodeBlock.test.tsx` ("resolves each combined row to its own document column and passes the selected bins").
- v0.8 notes: Offsets and lengths must use the same unit end to end. DuckDB `length()` on VARCHAR counts code points (good); JS must also count code points. Checked in WebKit.

  - Title `${mode} dispersion` to "Where matches occur in the documents".

#### V07-140 Concordance: Whole word works on Japanese and Chinese text (#220)

- Commits: fb08efec
- Area: Concordance
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Feedback from a Japanese-language researcher (June 2026), checked on v0.7. Whole word wrapped the term in `\b…\b`. Kana, kanji and hanzi are word characters in Rust regex, and these languages put no spaces between words, so there was almost never a boundary: `いただ` matched 発表させていただきます without Whole word and found nothing with it. The Troubleshooting table even advised ticking Whole word.
- What changed:
  - `build_concordance_search_pattern` (backend `analysis/concordance_core.py`): for a literal term, the `\b` is kept only at an edge whose character is not in a script written without spaces (`_UNSPACED_SCRIPT_RE`: Thai, Lao, Myanmar, Khmer, kana with 々〆〇 and halfwidth katakana, CJK ideographs including extensions). Hangul keeps its boundary because Korean uses spaces. Regex terms are unchanged (the UI turns Whole word off with regular expressions).
  - Rust regex has no lookbehind, so a mixed term such as "AI技術" still needs a space or punctuation before its Latin edge.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Text mode, Whole word on: `いただ` finds 発表させていただきます; `经济` finds 中国经济发展很快.
  - `cat` finds "the cat sat" but not "concatenate"; `학교` does not match 학교에.
  - "AI技術" matches 「AI技術」を紹介 but not BRAI技術 or 最新のAI技術.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/analysis/concordance_core.py`.
- Tests: `tests/unit/test_concordance_helpers.py::test_whole_word_skips_boundaries_next_to_unspaced_scripts` (runs each pattern through Polars' Rust regex); manual run through the polars-text concordance engine.
- v0.8 notes: any "whole word" option must not rely on `\b` alone for scripts without spaces; with DuckDB regex, check the same cases.

#### V07-141 Concordance: Tokens mode explained plainly; help for text without spaces (#221)

- Commits: 7652cb2c
- Area: Concordance
- Type: wording
- Problem / reason: Same feedback. Tokens mode matches one whole token, exactly as written, and a space means "any of", so a Japanese expression typed as one term (させていただく, which UniDic splits into さ / せ / て / いただく) found nothing, and "させて いただく" found every させて and every いただく anywhere; conjugated forms (いただき) are different tokens. The researcher found Text mode with a regular expression listing kana and kanji variants reliable. A backend comment also wrongly said Text-mode context counts characters for CJK.
- What changed:
  - Search box placeholder in Tokens mode: "Whole tokens; a space, comma or | means any of them". Tokens tooltip: "Finds each term as one whole token, exactly as written. A space, comma or | means any of them, not a phrase: 猫|犬 or cat dog finds every hit of either. For a phrase or other word forms, use Text with a regular expression."
  - Help (`tutorials/concordance.md`): Tokens mode bullet with examples; Whole word does not apply to text without spaces; new section `help-concordance-unspaced-text` (UniDic splitting example, the variant pattern `せていただ|せて頂|していただ|して頂`, Text-mode context counts stretches between spaces or punctuation); Troubleshooting rows for Whole word and for phrases in Tokens mode.
  - Comment in `analysis/concordance_tokens.py` corrected.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - The Tokens placeholder and tooltip read as above.
  - The help page has the new section and the two Troubleshooting rows.
- Implementation: `features/views/concordance/components/ConcordanceParameterPanel.tsx`; `public/tutorials/concordance.md`.
- Tests: docs drift check; Concordance frontend suite.
- v0.8 notes: keep this wording until phrase search (#222) and dictionary-form matching (#223) exist, then update it.

#### V07-157 Concordance: context headers sort by L1/R1 when Highlight L1/R1 for sorting is on (#241)

- Commits: 2ccb5dce
- Area: Concordance
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: Corpus linguists sort a concordance by the word next to the match: clicking Left context should order by L1 and Right context by R1. The context headers were not sortable. Only L1 and R1 are stored, so sorting by L2 and further words is out of scope. Included in v0.7 at Chao's request as an expected function of the stable version.
- What changed:
  - The checkbox "Highlight L1/R1 in context" is renamed **Highlight L1/R1 for sorting** (`ConcordanceResultsPanel.tsx`); it keeps the inline L1/R1 tints and now also sets how the context headers sort.
  - `concordanceHeaderMode` (`concordanceTablePresentation.ts`): the left and right contexts are sortable in separated Review and show "Run to enable sorting" in Preview; the document column and the combined table stay plain.
  - New `concordanceSortColumn(columnKey, highlightL1R1)`: on, the left context sorts by `CONC_l1` and the right context by `CONC_r1`; off, each context sorts by its own text. `concordanceSortHint` gives the header tooltip ("Sorts by L1, the word before the match. Turn off Highlight L1/R1 for sorting to sort by the text.").
  - `SortableHeader` takes `sortKey` and `hint`, shows the arrow when the sort is its `sortKey` or its own column, and keeps the column's alignment.
  - No backend change: the Review sort already accepts any scalar Result column.
  - Help: `tutorials/concordance.md` (Result panel, Table view, sorting, settings table) and `information/concordance.md`.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - After Run, with the checkbox on, clicking Left context orders rows by L1 and both headers show the arrow; clicking Right context orders by R1. The tooltip explains the redirect.
  - With the checkbox off, the context headers sort alphabetically by their text; a second click reverses the order.
  - With Ignore punctuation on, R1 can differ from the first word of the right context (for example ", ate end" has R1 "ate").
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/concordance/concordanceTablePresentation.ts`, `components/SortableHeader.tsx`, `components/ConcordanceTableNodeBlock.tsx`, `components/ConcordanceResultsPanel.tsx`.
- Tests: `concordanceTablePresentation.test.ts` (modes, sort column, hint), `ConcordanceTableNodeBlock.test.tsx` (on, off, sorted arrow), `ConcordanceFeature.test.tsx` (label); manual WebKit run on the isolated instance.
- v0.8 notes: keep one switch for the L1/R1 highlighting and the context-header sort, labelled Highlight L1/R1 for sorting. If v0.8 stores L2 and further words, the left-context sort can extend to L1, L2, and so on.

#### V07-158 Concordance: show L1/R1 in the contexts as coloured text, not a background tint (#242)

- Commits: 91834c82
- Area: Concordance
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: The L1 and R1 words inside the contexts had a pale background tint in the source colour, which read like a highlighter and competed with the matched-text cell. Chao asked for a font colour instead.
- What changed:
  - `renderContextWithAnchor` (`components/ConcordanceRowsTable.tsx`): the `<mark>` gets the class `concordance-context-anchor` and `font-semibold`, and sets the CSS variable `--concordance-anchor-color` to the source colour; no inline background or text colour.
  - `index.css`: `.concordance-context-anchor { background-color: transparent; color: color-mix(in srgb, var(--concordance-anchor-color) 55%, currentColor); }`. Mixing with the surrounding text colour darkens the word in the light theme and lightens it in the dark theme; 55% keeps the darkest palette colour (#92400e) at about 4.2:1 in the dark theme.
  - The matched-text cell keeps its background tint. Help: `tutorials/concordance.md`, `information/concordance.md`; screenshot `tutorials/assets/concordance/table_view.png` retaken.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - With Highlight L1/R1 for sorting on, L1 and R1 in the contexts are bold and coloured, with a transparent background, in both themes; off, the contexts are plain text.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/concordance/components/ConcordanceRowsTable.tsx`, `frontend/src/index.css`.
- Tests: `ConcordanceTableNodeBlock.test.tsx` (class, CSS variable, no background, combined-table fallback colour); manual WebKit run in the light and dark themes on the isolated instance.
- v0.8 notes: show L1/R1 in the contexts as source-coloured bold text mixed with the text colour, not a background tint.

#### V07-173 A generated column used as the text is renamed, not refused (#244, #245, #246)

- Commits: da795fd9
- Area: Concordance
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: Grilled with Chao on 2026-10-02 after the #244 to #246 fixes. The #244 workflow searches a stacked `CONC_extraction`, which the earlier fix refused ("rename it in the Data Editor"). Decisions: old generated columns are replaced; generated columns are recognised by exact reserved names; a generated column used as the text is renamed automatically; Add to Project dialogs do not offer old generated columns.
- What changed:
  - `source_text_column_name` and `_without_previous_generated_columns` (`analysis/generated_columns.py`) keep a generated-named text column and rename it to `CONC_source` or `QUOTE_source` (then `_2`, `_3` when taken); the other old generated columns are still dropped. The `*_source` names are ordinary columns, so later runs keep them.
  - Run All descriptors record the new name, so Review, Add to Project and the created Data Blocks follow it (document column `CONC_source`). Previews apply the same rule in the frontend (`sourceTextColumnName` in `common/generatedColumns.ts`).
  - Topic Modelling refuses a `TOPIC_` text column in plain words ("… holds topics from an earlier Topic Modelling run, not documents. Choose the column with the text to model."), because none of those columns is document text.
  - Help: `tutorials/concordance.md`, `quotation.md`, `topic-modeling.md` list each tool's reserved names.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Searching a Data Block whose text column is `CONC_extraction` works; the Result and the Data Blocks made from it show the text as `CONC_source`, with one set of `CONC_` columns and no duplicates; old `CONC_`/`QUOTE_` columns are not offered in Add to Project.
  - Quotation on `QUOTE_extraction` gives `QUOTE_source`; a `CONC_source` already present gives `CONC_source_2`.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/analysis/generated_columns.py`, `analysis/concordance_core.py`, `analysis/quotation_core.py`, `workers/concordance.py`, `workers/quotation.py`, `workers/topic_modeling.py`; `frontend/src/features/views/common/generatedColumns.ts`, `concordance/ConcordanceFeature.tsx`, `quotation/QuotationFeature.tsx`.
- Tests: `test_workspace_analysis_contract.py::test_concordance_on_a_stacked_extraction_keeps_its_text_as_conc_source`, `test_concordance_helpers.py`, `test_quotation_run_all_worker.py`, `test_topic_modeling_worker.py`; `sourceTextColumnName.test.ts`.
- v0.8 notes: the backend and frontend copies of the naming rule must agree; better, have the Result report the text column's name.

#### V07-184 Concordance: rows with equal sort values stay in Data Block order (#266)

- Commits: 0bc99716
- Area: Concordance
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: Sorting a Run result by L1, R1 or any other column gave no order among rows with the same value: Polars promises nothing about ties, so matches of one word from one document could appear out of reading order, and a later Polars could reorder them between pages.
- What changed:
  - `_projected_artifact_page` (`services/analysis_results.py`) sorts match rows by the chosen column, then `__wordflow_source_row_id`, then `CONC_start_idx`; the two tie keys are always ascending, also when the column sorts descending.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Sorting by L1 across many pages shows every match exactly once.
  - Rows sharing a value are in Data Block order: by document, then position in it.
  - Descending reverses the values only; rows sharing a value stay in reading order.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/services/analysis_results.py`.
- Tests: `test_analysis_results.py`: `test_concordance_l1_sort_pages_show_every_match_once_in_reading_order`, `test_concordance_l1_sort_breaks_ties_by_source_then_position` (checks the sort keys themselves), `test_concordance_l1_sort_descending_reverses_words_not_reading_order`.
- v0.8 notes: give every result sort explicit tie keys (source row, then position); never rely on the engine's tie order.

#### V07-185 Concordance: Case sensitive also governs L1/R1 counts and sorting, and the matched-text sort (#267, #274)

- Commits: 0bc99716, b9e98b48
- Area: Concordance
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: With Case sensitive off, a search found "The" and "the" together, but L1/R1 counted and sorted them as different words, and sorting by matched text put all "The" rows before all "the" rows. Agreed with Chao on 2026-10-03: the one option decides both the search and how its neighbours are grouped.
- What changed:
  - Worker `_context_frequency(column, case_sensitive)` (`workers/concordance.py`) counts `CONC_l1_freq`/`CONC_r1_freq` over the lowercased word when the search was not case-sensitive.
  - `_projected_table_snapshot` returns the request's `case_sensitive`; `_projected_artifact_page` lowercases the sort key for `CONC_l1`, `CONC_r1` and `CONC_matched_text` when it is off. Displayed text keeps its capitals.
  - A ? help icon beside Case sensitive (target `analysis.concordance.regex-toggle`); help and the generated-column help for the frequency columns say so.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Case sensitive off: an L1 of "The" and one of "the" show the same frequency; sorting by L1, R1 or matched text mixes them in Data Block order, each keeping its own capitals.
  - Case sensitive on: they are separate words; capitals sort first.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/workers/concordance.py`, `services/analysis_results.py`; `frontend/src/features/views/concordance/components/ConcordanceParameterPanel.tsx`, `common/generatedColumns.ts`; `public/tutorials/concordance.md`.
- Tests: `test_concordance_run_all_worker.py`; `test_analysis_results.py`: `test_concordance_l1_sort_ignores_case_when_the_search_did`, `test_concordance_matched_text_sort_ignores_case_when_the_search_did`.
- v0.8 notes: case-fold only the sort and count keys, never the values shown or exported.

#### V07-186 Concordance: result rows banded by source document (#268)

- Type: interface change

- Expected behaviour (testable):

- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/services/analysis_results.py`; `frontend/src/features/views/concordance/concordanceTablePresentation.ts`, `components/ConcordanceTableNodeBlock.tsx`.
- Tests: `test_analysis_results.py::test_concordance_match_rows_band_by_source_document_across_pages`; `concordanceTablePresentation.test.ts`.

Chao asked whether Ignore punctuation (on for new tabs) conflicts with regular expressions: it does not, because it changes only how context words and L1/R1 are counted, after matching.

#### V07-191 Concordance Tokens mode: always case-insensitive; L1/R1 marks capitalised context words (#272)

- Commits: 1d237fc0
- Area: Concordance
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Tokenisation lowercases tokens (except case-free tokenisers such as jieba and the Japanese IPADIC/UniDic models) and removes punctuation, so Case sensitive and Ignore punctuation mean nothing in Tokens mode: a case-sensitive Tokens search could only miss capitalised terms. Tokens-mode L1/R1 are lowercased tokens while the contexts show the original text, so the exact-match L1/R1 mark missed "Australian" for "australian" (reported by Chao on 2026-10-03).
- What changed:
  - Case sensitive and Ignore punctuation stay Text-mode only; Text-mode order is Case sensitive, Ignore punctuation, Whole word, Use regular expression.
  - Tokens runs always send `case_sensitive: false` (`requestCaseSensitive` in `ConcordanceFeature.tsx`, also used by the parameter-change check); the Text-mode checkbox value is kept for switching back. `ConcordanceAnalysisRequest`'s validator forces `case_sensitive=False` when `search_mode == "tokens"`, so counts and sorting follow.
  - `findContextAnchor` (`concordanceTablePresentation.ts`) marks L1/R1 by exact match when there is one, else by a literal case-insensitive match, keeping the context's own capitals.
  - Help: Tokens mode lowercases and drops punctuation, so it always ignores capitals.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Tokens mode shows no Case sensitive or Ignore punctuation option and finds "Australian" for `australian`.
  - A Tokens request submitted with `case_sensitive: true` is stored as false.
  - In Tokens results, L1 "australian" marks "Australian" in the left context; an exact match is still preferred when present.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/domain/workspace/analysis.py`; `frontend/src/features/views/concordance/ConcordanceFeature.tsx`, `components/ConcordanceParameterPanel.tsx`, `components/ConcordanceRowsTable.tsx`, `concordanceTablePresentation.ts`; `public/tutorials/concordance.md`.
- Tests: `test_analysis_domain.py::test_concordance_tokens_mode_is_always_case_insensitive`; `concordanceTablePresentation.test.ts` (`findContextAnchor`); `ConcordanceTableNodeBlock.test.tsx` (case-mismatched anchors are marked in their original case).
- v0.8 notes: whatever the tokeniser does to case, the mark must locate L1/R1 in the original context text.

#### V07-229 Text mode: no L1 for a match at the start of a text (#312)

- Commits: 678c1283 (polars-text 43fc460, 0.6.2)
- Area: Concordance
- Type: bug fix

R1 used a plain lookup and was right; Tokens mode (Python) was right; v0.8's ldaca-rs already uses `checked_sub`.
- What changed:

  - Wordflow v0.7: `polars-text>=0.6.2`, both uv locks, submodule pointer.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Search "no" in "No matter what": L1 empty, R1 "matter"; in "I have no": L1 "have", R1 empty.
  - L1 counts, sort and tint no longer include the matched word for such rows.
- Implementation: polars-text `src/concordance.rs`; Wordflow `backend/pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `backend/uv.lock`.
- Tests: polars-text `tests/test_concordance.py::test_concordance_match_at_text_start_has_no_l1`; Wordflow `test_concordance_helpers.py::test_a_match_at_the_start_of_a_text_has_no_l1` (fails on 0.6.1).

#### V07-079 Trends and Concordance density chart: clearer selection style (#190, #191)

- Commits: 6aa1947c, a354a9f8
- Area: Trends, Concordance (dispersion density/summary chart)
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: In Trends, selected periods were solid dots and unselected ones hollow circles, but the solid dots were small and the same colour as the line, so the selection was hard to see (Chao's screenshot, #190). #191 asked for the same style in the Concordance dispersion density chart.
- What changed:
  - Trends line and area charts with a selection: a soft amber band (`rgba(245, 158, 11, 0.16)`) is drawn behind selected periods; adjacent selected periods merge into one band. On a category axis the band is a translucent full-width bar series on a hidden second y axis (0 to 1), silent, no tooltip, no decal. On a numeric axis it is an ECharts `markArea` on the first series, reaching halfway to the neighbouring points.
  - Selected points: solid circle, 11 px, white 2 px border; unselected: hollow circle, 4 px (applied only when `emphasizeSelection` is set). Without a selection, symbol size stays 6.
  - Bar charts keep the existing dimming of unselected bars to 25% opacity.
  - Concordance dispersion summary chart: same amber band over exact bin edges on the 0 to 100% axis (e.g. 30 to 70%) for line, area and cumulative modes (any mode except `density-bar`); same large ringed selected dots; bar mode keeps dimming. The per-document dispersion rows keep same-size markers.
  - ECharts `MarkAreaComponent` registered in `EChartsView`.
  - Trends tutorial "Period selection" text updated.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Selecting two adjacent periods in a Trends line chart draws one amber band over both and enlarges their points with a white ring.
  - On a numeric Trends axis, a selected point's band spans from halfway to the previous point to halfway to the next.
  - In the dispersion density chart, selecting bins 30 to 70% shades exactly that range.
  - In bar mode, no band; unselected bars dim.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/common/components/MultiSeriesChart.tsx` (`buildMultiSeriesChartOption`, `categorySelectionBand`, `numericSelectionAreas`, `SELECTION_BAND_*`), `views/common/echartsSeriesStates.ts` (`buildEChartsSeriesStates` with `emphasizeSelection`, `SELECTED_SYMBOL_SIZE`, `UNSELECTED_SYMBOL_SIZE`), `views/common/components/EChartsView.tsx`, `views/concordance/components/ConcordanceDispersionSummary.tsx` (`selectedBinRanges`).
- Tests: `MultiSeriesChart.test.ts` ("shades selected runs on a numeric axis halfway to the neighbouring points"), `EChartsView.test.tsx`, `ConcordanceDispersionSummary.test.tsx` ("shows selected bins in the Trends selection style").
- v0.8 notes: ECharts-specific techniques (a bar series on a hidden second y axis for category bands; markArea for numeric axes). Keep the same colour and sizes for consistency across Trends and Concordance. Checked in WebKit for both axis types.

#### V07-130 Trends: a Group By column can be chosen only once (#212)

- What changed:

  - Backend: `SequentialAnalysisRequest` gets a `field_validator("group_by_columns")` raising "choose each column only once" for duplicates. Through the #205 validation formatter the user sees "Group by columns: choose each column only once." (the issue had proposed "Choose each Group By column only once.").
- Expected behaviour (testable): with "count" chosen in the first Group By row, the second row's list does not offer "count (whole number)"; a direct API request with `group_by_columns=["gender","gender"]` returns 422 with that message.

- v0.8 notes: Polars-specific failure (duplicate projection names); DuckDB `GROUP BY a, a` may not fail but would produce duplicate output columns; keep the request-level validation regardless.

#### V07-134 Charts: Safari repaints clipped lines; no wheel zoom; clearer slider; one chart date format (#213)

- Commits: 5f062d2c (first attempt, replaced), 46e1c6e9, 915ed42d
- Area: Trends (shared `EChartsView`, so every ECharts chart)
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: In Safari, Trends lines and areas vanished and only their dots stayed: after changing Minimum group count, on a fresh chart, and while hovering (bars were fine). WebKit only; Chromium drew every line. The DOM was correct, and removing the `clip-path` attributes in the broken state brought every line back: WebKit keeps painting a clipped element with a stale clip when the ECharts SVG renderer (zrender) changes a `<clipPath>` in place (entry animation, hover, clip ids reused when series come and go). Resetting the attribute, turning animation off, or clearing the chart when the series set changed (the first attempt, 5f062d2c) did not cover every case. Chao also asked to stop wheel zoom (scrolling the page kept zooming), to make the slider easier to see, and to unify the date labels: Even spacing showed the backend label "2020-10-18", To scale and the tooltip showed the browser locale's date in local time ("18 Oct 2020" in Australian Safari, "Oct 18, 2020" in US browsers).
- What changed:
  - `EChartsView` re-inserts each `<clipPath>` after every `rendered` event (detach and insert at the same place), so WebKit rebuilds it from its current shape. The first attempt's clear-on-series-change was removed.
  - The inside dataZoom has `zoomOnMouseWheel: false` (this also stops trackpad pinch, which arrives as a wheel event). The slider and the Zoom in / Zoom out / Reset zoom buttons still zoom.
  - The slider is 20 px high and uses theme tokens: `fillerColor` 22% of `--vscode-focusBorder`, handles with a focus-coloured border, data outline and area in the focus colour (brighter for the selected range).
  - Chart dates (Chao's decision, 2026-09-28): one fixed, readable format for every user: "18 Oct 2020" (days; a week shows its Monday), "18 Oct 2020 14:05" (hours and minutes), "18 Oct 2020 14:05:09" (seconds), "Oct 2020", "2020 Q4", "2020". Tables, filters and downloads keep year-first ISO ("2020-10-18"). Labels are parsed from the backend's `time_period_formatted`, which is in the time column's own zone; To scale ticks (UTC instants) are shifted by the offset between a label and its instant, so both spacings and the tooltip agree.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - In Safari/WebKit, every visible Line or Area series draws its line after a new Run, after Minimum group count changes (10 to 100 and back), while hovering across the lines, after hiding a legend entry, and after zooming.
  - Scrolling the mouse wheel or pinching over the chart does not zoom; the slider and buttons do.
  - The zoom slider is visible in light and dark themes.
  - For a Brisbane (UTC+10) daily result whose 18 October bucket starts at 14:00 UTC on the 17th, both spacings and the tooltip read "18 Oct 2020".
  - Period labels parse as: "2020-W42" gives Monday 19 Oct 2020; "2020-Q4" gives 1 Oct 2020.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/common/components/EChartsView.tsx` (`refreshClipPaths` on `rendered`, dataZoom options); new `frontend/src/lib/chartDates.ts` (`formatChartDate`, `parsePeriodLabel`); `features/views/sequential-analysis/hooks/sequentialChartModel.ts` (`chartDateUnitFor`, `formatInstant`, zone offset); help `tutorials/sequential-analysis.md`, `tutorials/ui.md`.
- Tests: `EChartsView.test.tsx` ("re-inserts clip paths after each render ...", "does not zoom on the mouse wheel and styles the zoom slider ..."); `lib/__tests__/chartDates.test.ts`; `sequentialChartModel.test.ts` ("labels dates the same way in both spacings, in the time column's zone"); manual Playwright WebKit and Chromium runs.
- v0.8 notes: any ECharts chart on the SVG renderer in WebKit needs the clipPath re-insertion (or the canvas renderer; v0.7 keeps SVG because chart export clones the live SVG). Keep wheel zoom off and one fixed chart date format; do not use `toLocaleString` for chart labels. Format dates in the time column's zone, not the browser's.

#### V07-139 Trends: Normalise to 100% (#219, ported from v0.8)

- Commits: b9e8e95e
- Area: Trends
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: Periods hold very different amounts of data, so raw counts hide how the mix of groups changes over time. v0.8 already has "Normalize to 100%" in its Trends plot (`frontend/src/features/tools/plots/plotModel.ts` on main); Chao asked for it in v0.7 from a v0.8 handoff plan. Chao's decisions (2026-09-28): (1) the legend share also counts hidden groups and does not change when groups are hidden; (2) the toggle shows only when at least two groups meet Minimum group count; (3) Australian spelling and plain words (R5).
- What changed:
  - `buildSequentialChartModel` takes `normalise`. `canNormalise` is true when at least two groups meet the minimum group count (hidden groups count). When on, each period's total is summed from the canonical rows of every group that meets the minimum (hidden ones included, merged case variants summed); each chart value becomes `100 × count / total`, or `null` when the total is 0. The raw count is kept as `__count__:<group id>` for the tooltip. `tooltip.valueFormatter` gives "412 (37.5%)", "0 (no rows in this period)", or the plain count when off.
  - Legend share: now over every group that meets the minimum, hidden included, and hidden entries show it too: "B (3 · 37.5% · Hidden)" (was "B (3 · Hidden)", with the others recalculated over visible groups).
  - `MultiSeriesChart` accepts `tooltip.valueFormatter(seriesKey, row)`. `SequentialChart` passes a y-axis of `min 0`, `{value}%` labels and `max 100` for Area, shows a note under the chart, and the legend help text says shares include hidden groups.
  - `SequentialAnalysisResultsPanel` shows a "Normalise to 100%" checkbox after Spacing (tooltip: "Show each group as a percentage of all rows in the same period, so periods with very different amounts of data can be compared. The counts do not change."). State is the per-tab setting `sequential.normalise` (in memory like `sequential.chartType`), off by default. Nothing is added to the saved Project.
  - Chart download header adds "Values: Percentage of all rows in each period" when on.
  - Help: `tutorials/sequential-analysis.md` "Normalise to 100%" section, legend paragraph, Run paragraph, download paragraph.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Groups A and B with 3 and 1 rows in a period show 75% and 25%; hiding B leaves A at 75%.
  - A group below the minimum group count is left out of the totals; changing the minimum can change percentages.
  - A period where the listed groups have no rows has no percentage (the line breaks, no bar).
  - The checkbox is absent with no Group by or when fewer than two groups meet the minimum.
  - The tooltip shows the count and its percentage; the y axis reads in %.
  - Legend shares include hidden groups and do not change when a group is hidden, whether or not normalisation is on.
  - Add to Project, selected periods and legend counts are unchanged by the toggle.
- Implementation: `features/views/sequential-analysis/hooks/sequentialChartModel.ts`, `sequentialChartExport.ts`, `components/SequentialChart.tsx`, `components/panels/SequentialAnalysisResultsPanel.tsx`, `SequentialAnalysisFeature.tsx`; `features/views/common/components/MultiSeriesChart.tsx`.
- Tests: `sequentialChartModel.test.ts` ("Normalise to 100% (issue 219)": shares, hiding, minimum group count, empty period, tooltip, toggle availability; legend expectation updated); `sequentialChartExport.test.ts`; `MultiSeriesChart.test.ts` (value formatter); manual Playwright WebKit run on a party × gender result.
- v0.8 notes: v0.8 already normalises. Align v0.8 with the v0.7 decisions that differ: the label "Normalise to 100%" (Australian spelling), the toggle only with two or more eligible groups (v0.8 shows it whenever the measure allows), and legend shares that include hidden groups. v0.8 stacks Bars for counts; v0.7 keeps them side by side.

It was redundant with click (toggle a point) and Shift-click (range from the last clicked point). Chao's decision: remove it and remind users about Shift-click in the interface.

#### V07-143 Trends bars: stacked when groups don't fit; zoom capped when bars would be too thin (#225)

- Commits: 5dcfc81b
- Area: Trends (shared `EChartsView`, opt-in)
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: With many groups and periods, side-by-side bars became unreadably thin, and with many periods even one bar per period was too thin. Chao asked to stack bars when groups don't fit side by side, and, when there are too many periods even for stacked bars, to cap the zoom so bars stay readable and tell the user.
- What changed:
  - New `lib/barFit.ts` `fitBars({ plotWidth, pointCount, seriesCount, visiblePercent })`: side by side needs at least 6 px per bar (`visible periods × groups × 6 ≤ plot width`), otherwise stacked; stacked needs at least 4 px per period, otherwise `maxVisiblePoints = floor(width / 4)` and `maxSpanPercent = maxVisiblePoints / periods × 100`.
  - `EChartsView` prop `fitBarsLabel` (Trends passes "periods"): plot width is the element width less 90 px; bar series get `stack: 'wordflow-bars'` and square corners when stacked; both dataZooms get `maxSpan`; a wider window is cut to the earliest periods that fit; Zoom out and Reset zoom stop at the cap. Recomputed on zoom and resize.
  - Messages under the chart: "The groups are stacked because they don't fit side by side at this width. Zoom in to see them side by side." and "Too many periods to show as bars at once, so the chart shows up to N of M. Drag the slider under the chart to see the others, or choose Line or Area to see them all."
  - `ResultChartFit` re-measures the space around the chart with a ResizeObserver, so a late note no longer pushes content out of the resizable frame.
  - Help: Bars bullet in the Trends chart types.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - 20 periods × 5 groups in a 600 px plot: side by side; 40 periods: stacked; zoomed to half: side by side again.
  - A single series never stacks.
  - 250 periods in a 400 px plot: at most 100 shown, zoom capped at 40%, message shown; Line and Area are never capped.
  - The Shift-click hint stays visible when a bar note appears.
- Implementation: `lib/barFit.ts`; `features/views/common/components/EChartsView.tsx`, `MultiSeriesChart.tsx`, `ResultFrame.tsx`; `features/views/sequential-analysis/components/SequentialChart.tsx`.
- Tests: `lib/__tests__/barFit.test.ts`; `EChartsView.test.tsx` ("stacks bars that do not fit side by side and caps the zoom…"); manual Playwright WebKit runs on daily (stacked, then side by side when zoomed) and hourly (capped at 195 of 332) results.
- v0.8 notes: v0.8 stacks count bars always; decide whether to adopt width-based stacking and the zoom cap. The thresholds (6 px, 4 px) are tunable.

#### V07-236 Axis picker offers only date, date-time and number columns; the backend checks the type (#316)

The backend never checked the axis column's type and trusted the browser's `column_type`, so a mismatched request ("numeric" on a date-time column) was binned as raw numbers.

Otherwise InvalidInputError "Trends can't use '<column>' as its time axis. Choose a date, date-and-time or number column."
- Expected behaviour (testable):

  - A request whose column type does not match `column_type` is refused before it runs.

- Tests: `trendsAxisColumns.test.ts` (accepted and refused Arrow types); `test_trends_axis_types.py` (dates and numbers accepted; time of day, duration, text and mismatched modes refused).
- v0.8 notes: Check the axis column's type in the Rust handler, not only in the picker; offer DATE, TIMESTAMP and numeric DuckDB types only.
