- Implementation: Backend `services/analysis_preparation_registry.py`, `services/analysis_preparation.py`. Frontend `sequential-analysis/trendsGroups.ts`, `components/UniqueValueCount.tsx`, `SequentialAnalysisFeature.tsx`, `public/tutorials/sequential-analysis.md`.
- Tests: `test_trends_group_limit.py` (too many values, combinations, missing column, ordinary groups); `trendsGroups.test.tsx` (group count, badge); Playwright WebKit for all three guards.
- v0.8 notes: Keep a group limit (1,000) enforced before running and when drawing a stored result; a stored result must never be able to make a tab unopenable. Count combinations in DuckDB with COUNT(DISTINCT (a, b)).

### Topic Modelling

#### V07-011 Topic Modelling: paragraph-first segmentation, Max topic size, per-topic detach (#125, #126, #127)

- Commits: e37bb048, 713a30a6
- Area: Topic Modelling, Build/CI (dependency pin), Docs
- Type: feature + behaviour change
- Problem / reason: (#125) Junk fragments and merged paragraphs produced meaningless topics and made per-topic detach unreadable. (#127) One giant topic could swallow most of the corpus and hide the real structure. (#126) Researchers interested in particular topics need the text that belongs to each topic, not whole documents.
- What changed:
  - Segmentation (implemented in polars-text 0.6.1, Wordflow side is labelling and docs): Automatic is paragraph-first with no packing: a paragraph that fits the token budget is one segment; longer paragraphs split into sentences; an over-long unit (all modes) is split at the punctuation mark nearest its middle, repeated on each half until pieces fit (both sides keep at least 4 tokens); with no usable punctuation, cut at the token budget. Paragraph breaks are blank lines when the document has any, otherwise single newlines. Only the leftover of such a punctuation-free budget cut is dropped when it has fewer than 4 content tokens (`MIN_FRAGMENT_TOKENS`, capped at half the budget); a unit that fits the budget is always kept however short (a 2-word sentence or tweet stays a segment), and a punctuation split never creates a piece under 4 tokens, so it drops nothing. Segments with no letters or digits (a stray quote mark, a lone full stop, an emoji-only line) are dropped in every mode. v0.8's `ldaca-rs` segmentation (as of 2026-10-06) still packs units up to the budget and has neither rule: port polars-text 0.6.x `src/topic_modeling/segmentation.rs` (`split_unit`, `middle_punctuation_split`, `cut_by_budget`, `push`) and its tests. The segmentation option with API value `"line"` is now labelled "Paragraph" in the UI (was "Line"). Segmentation help text: "Automatic starts from paragraphs (blank-line blocks, or single lines when the text has no blank lines). Paragraph treats every non-empty line as a paragraph. Sentence starts from Unicode sentence boundaries. A unit that fits the token budget is one segment; an oversized unit is split into sentences, then at the clause punctuation nearest its middle."
  - Max topic size (#127): new request field `max_cluster_size` (int >= 3 or null = Auto), must be larger than `min_cluster_size` (backend error "Max topic size must be larger than Min topic size"). Unit is Topic Segments, not documents. Auto (null) is not passed to polars-text (works with older builds); Auto rule in polars-text: cluster uncapped; only if one topic holds more than half of all segments, re-run HDBSCAN with a cap just below that size, accepting the capped result only if it has more topics and keeps at least half of the dominant topic. The result's clustering block reports `max_topic_size` (applied cap or null); older stored results without it still load.
  - UI field "Max topic size" next to Min topic size: number input, placeholder "Auto", empty = Auto, values rounded and floored at 3, `min` = Min topic size + 1, committed on blur. Help: "The largest topic, in Topic Segments (not documents). Leave empty for Auto: it only steps in when one topic holds more than half of all segments, splitting it into its sub-topics, and keeps the result only if that topic is not lost to outliers. A fixed value must be larger than Min topic size. Changing it requires running a new analysis." Inline error "Must be larger than Min topic size" (red, `aria-invalid`). Otherwise a last-run note: "Last run: N segments, capped at N" (fixed), "Last run: N segments, Auto: no cap needed", or "Last run: N segments, Auto capped at N".
  - Per-topic detach (#126): Topic Modelling Data Block Creation gains `row_unit` ("documents" | "topics", default "documents"), recorded in provenance. The Add to Workspace dialog has a "Rows" tab choice: "Per document" (default) / "Per topic". Per document: unchanged (default name "<source> topics", `TOPIC_top1` always included, description "Creates topic-data and topic-meanings Data Blocks for each selected source, with one row per document and its topic coverage."). Per topic: default name "<source> topic segments", one row per (document, topic); the document text column holds only that topic's segments sliced from the same text the run segmented, joined by "\n" in source order, and is always included; always-included columns `TOPIC_topic`, `TOPIC_share` (topic's share of the document's segmented characters), `TOPIC_segment_count`; description "Creates topic-segment and topic-meanings Data Blocks for each selected source, with one row per document and topic. The document column holds only that topic's segments, joined by line breaks." Outlier segments (topic < 0) excluded; respects the displayed Number of topics (re-aggregation) and the topic selection. Results without segment spans (pre-0.6.1) error: "Per-topic detach needs a Topic Modeling run made with this version of Wordflow. Re-run the analysis, then detach again."
  - 713a30a6: polars-text submodule pointed at v0.6.1 (3f40977) and `backend/pyproject.toml` requires `polars-text>=0.6.1` (uv.lock updated), so published wheels and the desktop published-wheel install (`--no-sources`) get the segmentation, Max topic size and segment-span APIs.
  - Tutorial `frontend/public/tutorials/topic-modeling.md` and information page `frontend/public/information/topic-modeling.md` describe paragraph-first segmentation, punctuation-balanced splitting, Max topic size and the two detach modes.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - The segmentation dropdown shows "Paragraph" (request value still `line`).
  - Entering a Max topic size <= Min topic size shows "Must be larger than Min topic size"; the backend rejects such a request.
  - Leaving Max topic size empty sends null; after a run the note reports the segment count and the cap applied.
  - Detaching with "Per topic" creates rows only for (document, topic) pairs with non-outlier segments; per document the `TOPIC_share` values sum to at most 1; text slicing is character-accurate for non-ASCII text.
- Implementation: backend `domain/workspace/analysis.py` (request fields and validator), `domain/workspace/provenance.py` (`row_unit`), `models/analysis_results.py` (`max_topic_size`), `analysis/generated_columns.py` (`TOPIC_SHARE_COLUMN`, `TOPIC_SEGMENT_COUNT_COLUMN`), `services/analysis_preparation_registry.py` (passes `text_column` into source context), `workers/invocations.py`, `workers/entrypoints.py`, `workers/topic_pipeline.py` (`max_topic_size` kwarg only when fixed), `workers/topic_modeling.py` (`_topic_segment_rows`, uses `polars_text.project_topic_segments(projection_context, cluster_count)` returning `(document_index, start, end, topic_id)` tuples). Frontend `TopicModelingParameterPanel.tsx`, `hooks/topicModelingParameterState.ts` (`sanitizeMaxClusterSize`, `setMaxClusterSize`), `useTopicModelingParameters.ts`, `useTopicModelingTaskFlow.ts`, `TopicModelingAddToWorkspaceDialog.tsx` (`TopicModelingDetachRowUnit`, dialog remounted per mode via `key={rowUnit}`), `common/components/AddToWorkspaceDialog.tsx` (new `options` slot), `TopicModelingFeature.tsx`; OpenAPI + generated types.
- Tests: backend `test_topic_modeling_data_block_creation_worker.py` (per-topic rows incl. non-ASCII slicing), `test_topic_modeling_segmentation_contract.py`, `test_topic_modeling_worker.py`; frontend `TopicModelingAddToWorkspaceDialog.test.tsx`, `TopicModelingParameterPanel.test.tsx`, `topicModelingParameterState.test.ts`.
- v0.8 notes: Segmentation, HDBSCAN Auto cap and segment spans live in the Rust polars-text library; v0.8 must either keep calling it or re-implement the exact rules above. Per-topic slicing uses Polars `str.slice` on character offsets (not bytes) and `group_by(...).agg(str.join("\n"))`; in DuckDB use character-based `substring` and `string_agg` ordered by start offset.

#### V07-013 Topic Modelling: colour bubbles by a metadata column (#128)

- Commits: fd904ef6
- Area: Topic Modelling
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: Researchers want to see which topics each party, decade or annotation code talks about without re-running the model.
- What changed:
  - New endpoint `POST /api/workspaces/{workspace_id}/analyses/{analysis_id}/result/topic-color-groups/query` (body `TopicColorGroupsQuery {cluster_count, top_n_topics, column?}`; response `TopicColorGroups {columns, column, groups[{value,label,document_count,missing}], topic_counts[topic][group]}`). Single-corpus results only (error "Metadata colours are available for single-corpus results only").
  - Eligible columns: any non-text column of numeric, String, Boolean, Categorical or Enum type with 1 to 8 distinct non-missing values among the run's documents. Values are read live from the source Data Block via the run's `projection_context.source_row_indices`, so columns added after the run (e.g. annotations) qualify. Error if the block no longer has those rows: "The source Data Block no longer has the rows used by this run".
  - Counts use the same Top-N-with-ties rule as bubble sizes (`topic_activation_thresholds`). Groups ordered by document count descending, missing last; missing values form a grey "(missing)" group; booleans labelled "true"/"false"; integral floats shown without ".0".
  - Bubble fill: each value's count divided by that value's document count; the top two weighted values are blended with the two-corpus interpolation formula (`second/(first+second)`); one value uses its colour; none falls back to the Data Block colour. Palette `RANDOMIZABLE_FG` in group order, missing always `GREY`.
  - UI: "Colour by" select in Result settings (only when single-corpus and eligible columns exist), first option "Data Block colour", help tooltip "Colour bubbles by a column with up to 8 values. Each bubble blends the two values most over-represented in its documents, relative to how common each value is."; per-value count chips in the topic list and hover card (hover shows labels, "= total"); a legend under the graph ("<column>:" then swatches); downloaded charts include the legend and a "Colour by" header row. Not saved; resets on a new run.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - For a single-corpus result, "Colour by" lists only columns with at most 8 distinct values and excludes the text column.
  - Per-value chip counts for a topic add up exactly to that bubble's size.
  - Rows with a null value appear in a grey "(missing)" group.
  - Two-corpus results show no "Colour by" control.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/analysis/topic_metadata_colors.py` (`eligible_color_columns`, `group_topic_counts`, `MAX_TOPIC_COLOR_GROUPS = 8`), `api/workspaces/analyses.py` (`query_topic_color_groups`), `services/analysis_results.py` (`topic_color_groups`, `_topic_color_frame`, `_topic_color_groups`, reuses `_project_rust_topic_modeling`), `models/analysis_results.py`; frontend `topic-modeling/hooks/useTopicColorGroups.ts`, `results/topicModelingGraph.ts` (`buildTopicColorScheme`, `topicColorSchemeFill`), `TopicModelingBubbleChartSection.tsx` (`TopicColorLegend`), `TopicSizeComposition.tsx` (`TopicColorGroupChips`), `TopicSelectionPanel.tsx`, `TopicModelingFlowChart.tsx`, `panels/TopicModelingResultsPanel.tsx` (`ColorByControl`), `lib/queryKeys.ts`. No polars-text change.
- Tests: `backend/tests/unit/test_topic_metadata_colors.py`, `test_current_api_surface.py`; frontend `results/__tests__/topicModelingGraph.test.ts`, `panels/__tests__/TopicModelingResultsPanel.test.tsx`.
- v0.8 notes: Depends on #129 (row positions stable). The eligibility check uses Polars `n_unique` over `drop_nulls`; in DuckDB use `COUNT(DISTINCT col)` (which ignores NULLs) and map dtypes accordingly.

The "Topic structure" and "Representative words" subheadings are removed.

#### V07-072 Topic Modelling: bubble opacity shows how concentrated a topic is across Colour by values (#188)

- Commits: 3588b72f
- Area: Topic Modelling
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: With Colour by, the fused colour shows the top two over-represented values but not whether a topic is concentrated or spread across many values (Chao's decision).
- What changed:
  - With Colour by active (single-corpus runs only, `corpusCount <= 1 && colorScheme`), resting opacity = 0.25 + 0.55 × √C, where C = 1 − Pielou's evenness J, J = H / ln K, H = −Σ pᵢ ln pᵢ, pᵢ = (topic count for value i / document count of value i) normalised over all K values (zero shares add nothing). One value (or K < 2) gives C = 1, opacity 0.8; an even spread gives 0.25. If the weights total 0, opacity falls back to 0.6.
  - Without Colour by, all bubbles use 0.6.
  - Hover lifts a bubble to 0.92 (above the range; CSS group-hover also 0.92). Selection no longer raises opacity (was 0.78); it is shown by the green outline (`#16a34a`, width 2).
  - Exported SVG uses the same resting opacity.
  - Colour by tooltip adds: "A more solid bubble is concentrated in few values; a paler one is spread evenly."
  - Tutorial section "What bubble opacity shows" (`help-topic-modeling-color-by-opacity`) documents the formula with a worked example (counts 60, 58, 10 give C ≈ 0.17, opacity ≈ 0.48; 60, 58, 57 give ≈ 0.26).
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - A topic whose documents all have one value renders at fill-opacity 0.8.
  - A topic spread evenly (relative to value sizes) renders at 0.25.
  - Hover shows 0.92; a selected, not hovered bubble keeps its resting opacity with a green ring.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/topic-modeling/components/results/topicModelingGraph.ts` (`TOPIC_OPACITY_MIN/MAX/DEFAULT/HOVER`, `topicValueConcentration`, `topicColorSchemeOpacity`, `TopicBubbleModel.fillOpacity`), `TopicModelingFlowChart.tsx` (`TopicBubbleNode`, `TopicExportSvg`), `panels/TopicModelingResultsPanel.tsx`, `public/tutorials/topic-modeling.md`.
- Tests: `topicModelingGraph.test.ts` ("sets opacity from how evenly a topic spreads across the values"), `TopicModelingFlowChart.test.tsx`.
- v0.8 notes: Frontend-only maths from `scheme.topicCounts` and `group.documentCount`; keep the exact formula and constants so help text stays true. Checked in WebKit.

#### V07-073 Topic Modelling: no bubble fully hidden behind another (#189)

- Commits: 73005897
- Area: Topic Modelling
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Chao's screenshot showed T7 fully covering T6 and T9 covering T2, labels included. Positions were the 2D projection scaled to the canvas with no overlap handling, drawn in topic order. Decision: partial overlap is allowed and meaningful, a huge bubble should not push everything away, but no bubble may be completely hidden.
- What changed:
  - A deterministic collision relaxation runs on the projected positions. For every pair, required distance = max(r_large + 0.35 × r_small, 26 px), so the smaller bubble's centre (where its label is) stays outside the larger one and two labels are never closer than 26 px.
  - Moves are split by area weights (the smaller bubble moves most). 240 anchored rounds with a pull back to the projected spot of 0.04 that fades linearly to 0, then up to 3,000 separation-only passes until the worst shortfall < 0.1 px. Coincident bubbles split along a fixed golden-angle direction derived from their ids.
  - The result is cached (keyed by id, x, y, radius of every bubble) so hover and selection do not recompute it.
  - Draw order: larger bubbles first (by `total_size` desc, then id), so smaller bubbles are on top.
  - Chart, lasso and exported SVG share the relaxed positions. Radius is unchanged: 10 + 40 × √(size / max size).
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - For every pair of visible bubbles, centre distance ≥ max(r_large + 0.35 r_small, 26).
  - Two bubbles at the same projected point are separated identically on every run.
  - An isolated bubble keeps its projected position.
  - A 200-topic crowded layout finishes (about 0.1 s) with nothing hidden.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/topic-modeling/components/results/topicModelingGraph.ts` (`relaxTopicPositions`, `relaxedPositionsFor` module-level cache, `buildTopicBubbleModels` draw order). Tutorial paragraph in `topic-modeling.md`.
- Tests: `topicModelingGraph.test.ts` ("pulls a covered bubble out until its centre is visible, moving the smaller one more", "splits bubbles at exactly the same spot the same way every time", "leaves no bubble hidden in a crowded layout").
- v0.8 notes: The diff does not clamp relaxed positions to the canvas; unclear whether bubbles can be pushed outside `TOPIC_GRAPH_WIDTH/HEIGHT` (not verified). Keep determinism so lasso and export match the screen. Checked in WebKit.

#### V07-093 Topic coverage columns: left out of analysis metadata; refused where they break (#200)

- Commits: 9b22ca51
- Area: Concordance, Quotation, Trends, Topic Modelling, Annotation, Data Builder, Data Editor, Data View, Export
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Concordance Run All on a block detached from Topic Modelling with per-document coverage failed: `InvalidOperationError: cannot cast List type ... to Extension(org.ldaca.wordflow.topic_coverage.v1 ...)`. Run All workers carried other columns as metadata by copying them into Python lists and casting back, which the topic-coverage extension type (a fixed-size array of `{topic_id, coverage}` structs) cannot survive. Agreed approach (Chao): treat it as not supported yet; leave it out of metadata and stop it being selected where it would break.
- What changed:
  - One shared backend rule (`shared/unsupported_columns.py`): `is_unsupported_metadata_dtype` matches the extension and its physical storage; `supported_metadata_columns`, `without_unsupported_columns`, `require_supported_columns`, and the error `"<col>" holds topic coverage values, which cannot be used <use> yet. Choose another column.` (uses: "with a type change", "as text", "as a join key", "to group rows", "with this summary").
  - Analyses: Concordance and Quotation (Preview and Run All), Trends Results, and Topic Modelling colours leave the column out of carried/shown/grouped metadata; Trends refuses it as time axis or group before execution; frontend metadata pickers (Concordance, Annotation Manual/Preview/Review, Trends group-by) omit it.
  - Data Builder and Data Editor: tools reading a column as text, a key, a group, an ordered summary, or a type change refuse it; summaries `first`, `last`, `count_distinct` still accept it. Column pickers in Data Builder tools other than Filter no longer list it. Carrying it through works: Filter on another column, Filter by topic threshold, Sample, Stack, Join on another key, Deduplicate, Duplicate, Rename, Delete.
  - Data View: Sort disabled with tooltip "Topic coverage columns can't be sorted yet."; Change data type disabled with "Topic coverage columns can't change type yet."; the column tools menu offers only Duplicate.
  - Filter by topic threshold on a detached block works again (it read the extension instead of its stored values; failed with `expected Array datatype`).
  - CSV and Excel exports write it as JSON text of its topic/coverage pairs instead of crashing; Parquet keeps it.
  - Architecture docs updated (`docs/architecture/backend/analysis-data-flow.md`, `frontend/state-and-data-flow.md`).
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Concordance Run All on a detached per-document coverage block succeeds, and its results have no TOPIC_coverage column.
  - Trends with the coverage column as group is refused with the message above.
  - Join on the coverage column is refused "as a join key".
  - Data View Sort button on the coverage column is disabled with its tooltip.
  - Filter "topic ≥ threshold" on a detached block returns rows above the threshold.
  - CSV export shows readable JSON in that column.
- Implementation: backend `shared/unsupported_columns.py` (new), `analysis/concordance_core.py`, `analysis/quotation_core.py`, `analysis/sequential_core.py`, `services/analysis_preparation_registry.py`, `services/analysis_results.py`, `services/data_block_exports.py` (extension columns written from `.ext.storage()`), `services/node_operations.py` (checks in each plan builder; `_topic_coverage_expression(condition, dtype)` uses `.ext.storage()` before `.arr.eval`), `workers/concordance.py`, `workers/quotation.py`. Frontend `lib/arrow/semanticTypes.ts` (`isSupportedColumnField`), `workspace/data-view/components/WorkspaceColumnHeader.tsx` (`isTopicCoverage`), `WorkspaceTable.tsx`, `useWorkspaceDataTable.ts`, `preprocessing/DataPreprocessingFeature.tsx` (`fieldPredicate`), `join/hooks/useJoinSubTab.ts`, `sequential-analysis/SequentialAnalysisFeature.tsx`, `concordance/hooks/useConcordanceMetadataColumns.ts`, annotation panels and `RunAllReviewTable.tsx`, `analysisNavigation.ts` (formatting only).
- Tests: `backend/tests/unit/test_topic_coverage_columns.py` (43 tests with real Parquet data and the extension loaded; includes one reproducing the original error), `useConcordanceMetadataColumns.test.ts`, `WorkspaceTable.test.tsx` ("offers topic coverage only the column tools that can use it"), `arrowTable.test.ts`.
- v0.8 notes: The topic coverage type is a Polars extension type (`org.ldaca.wordflow.topic_coverage.v1`); `.ext.storage()` is Polars-specific. In DuckDB it would be a `STRUCT(topic_id BIGINT, coverage DOUBLE)[]` (LIST or ARRAY) column, which DuckDB handles natively; the v0.7 restrictions may not all be needed, but the Filter-by-threshold and readable CSV/Excel export behaviour must be preserved.

#### V07-121 Topic Modelling in plain words (#205 item 15)

- Commits: 32dac467, 09425c68 (test follow-up)
- Area: Topic Modelling parameters, bubble chart export, Add to Project dialog, topic coverage bar in the Data Editor, guidance
- Type: wording
- Problem / reason: Topic size help mentioned "HDBSCAN minimum", "outliers" and "Auto … capped"; the topic count had three names (Topics, Number of topics, Clusters); "Per document" meant different things; the Add dialog said "topic-data / topic-meanings Data Blocks" and its "Per topic" tab was wrong (rows are per document and topic); "outlier" and "Topic -1".
- What changed:
  - Topic size help: "The smallest and largest topic, counted in Topic Segments (not documents). A smaller Min finds more, smaller topics. Leave Max empty for Auto: Wordflow then splits a topic only when it holds more than half of all segments. A fixed Max must be larger than Min. Changing Topic size needs a new Run; Number of topics only merges the topics found."
  - Last-run note: "{segments}, capped at N" to "{segments}; topics larger than N were split"; "{segments}, Auto: no cap needed" to "{segments}; no topic needed splitting"; "{segments}, Auto capped at N" to "{segments}; topics larger than N were split".
  - Chart export summary: "Clusters" to "Number of topics"; "Topics" (shown count) to "Topics in the chart".
  - Topic coverage bar: "outlier" to "No topic"; tooltip "Topic -1: x%" to "No topic: x%" (topic_id < 0).
  - Add to Project tabs "Per document" / "Per topic" to "One row per document" / "One row per topic". Descriptions: "For each selected source, creates a Data Block with one row per document and its topic coverage, and a Data Block of topic meanings." and "For each selected source, creates a Data Block with one row per topic in each document, and a Data Block of topic meanings. The document column holds only that topic's segments, joined by line breaks."
  - Guidance: "…describe discovered clusters; topic −1 contains outliers…" to "…describe the topics found; segments that fit no topic are marked No topic…".
  - Help topic-modeling.md updated (the method description still names HDBSCAN).
- Expected behaviour (testable): the Add dialog shows tabs "One row per document" and "One row per topic"; a row with topic -1 in the coverage bar reads "No topic"; the export header reads "Number of topics".
- Implementation: `topic-modeling/components/panels/TopicModelingParameterPanel.tsx`, `results/TopicModelingBubbleChartSection.tsx`, `TopicModelingAddToWorkspaceDialog.tsx`, `workspace/data-view/components/TopicCoverageBar.tsx`, `guidance/registry.ts`.
- Tests: `TopicModelingAddToWorkspaceDialog.test.tsx` (09425c68 updates the expected text to /one row per topic in each document/i), `TopicModelingParameterPanel.test.tsx`, `TopicCoverageBar.test.tsx`.
- v0.8 notes: topic id -1 is BERTopic/HDBSCAN's outlier id; keep it internal and always show "No topic".

#### V07-136 Charts: plain scroll scrolls the pane; Cmd (Ctrl) + scroll zooms (#215)

- Commits: 914e6a80
- Area: Charts (shared `EChartsView`, Topic Modelling graph, Project Graph)
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: The Topic Modelling bubble chart zoomed on a plain scroll and swallowed it, so the pane could not scroll with the pointer over the large chart. Chao asked for one rule in every chart: plain scrolling never zooms, and Cmd + scroll zooms. Checking Trends in WebKit found a second bug: since #213 turned wheel zoom off, a plain scroll over the Trends chart did nothing at all, because ECharts cancels every wheel event over an inside dataZoom before it checks whether any wheel behaviour is on.
- What changed:
  - New `frontend/src/lib/chartZoom.ts`: `CHART_ZOOM_KEY` ('Meta' on Apple platforms, 'Control' elsewhere), `ECHARTS_WHEEL_ZOOM_MODIFIER` ('meta' or 'ctrl'), `isChartZoomWheel(event)`.
  - Topic Modelling graph: `zoomOnScroll={false}`, `panOnScroll={false}`, `zoomActivationKeyCode={CHART_ZOOM_KEY}`, pinch still zooms. React Flow drops the zoom key when page scrolling is allowed, so `preventScrolling` follows the key through `useKeyPress(CHART_ZOOM_KEY)`.
  - `EChartsView` (Trends and the Concordance dispersion chart): `zoomOnMouseWheel: ECHARTS_WHEEL_ZOOM_MODIFIER` (ECharts checks `event[modifier + 'Key']`, so 'meta' works although its types list only shift, ctrl and alt). A capture-phase wheel listener on the plot element stops plain wheel events before ECharts sees them, so the pane scrolls. Trackpad pinch does not zoom ECharts charts.
  - Project Graph: unchanged behaviour (scroll pans, pinch or Cmd + scroll zooms, issue 194; Shift + scroll pans sideways); the zoom key is now named from the same module.
  - Help: Trends, Topic Modelling and Concordance pages describe Cmd (Ctrl) + scroll.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Over the Topic Modelling graph, Trends chart or Concordance dispersion chart, a plain wheel or two-finger scroll scrolls the pane and leaves the chart unchanged.
  - With Cmd (macOS) or Ctrl (Windows, Linux) held, the same scroll zooms the chart and the pane does not move.
  - A trackpad pinch zooms the Topic Modelling graph and the Project Graph.
  - The Project Graph still pans on a plain scroll and zooms on Cmd + scroll.
- Implementation: `lib/chartZoom.ts`; `features/views/topic-modeling/components/results/TopicModelingFlowChart.tsx`; `features/views/common/components/EChartsView.tsx` (`keepPageScroll`); `features/workspace/graph-view/components/WorkspaceGraphFeature.tsx`; help `tutorials/sequential-analysis.md`, `topic-modeling.md`, `concordance.md`.
- Tests: `EChartsView.test.tsx` ("keeps plain wheel events away from ECharts so the pane scrolls", "zooms on the wheel only with Ctrl or Cmd held ..."); `TopicModelingFlowChart.test.tsx` ("lets the pane scroll and zooms only with the zoom key held"); `WorkspaceGraphFeature.test.tsx` (zoom key); manual Playwright WebKit run measuring pane scroll and chart state for plain and Cmd scrolls.
- v0.8 notes: an ECharts inside dataZoom cancels every wheel event over the chart, whatever its settings; filter wheel events before ECharts if the page must scroll. Keep one zoom key for all charts.

#### V07-154 Topic Modelling: every sample uses the Seed as given, not offset by position (#237)

- Commits: 2821cccd, 346bf63f
- Area: Topic Modelling
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: Each Data Block's sample was seeded with the Seed field plus the Data Block's position (`random_seed + index`), so the second Data Block used Seed + 1 and swapping the Data Blocks changed both samples. 2821cccd first used the whole-number percentage as the seed; 346bf63f replaced that, at Chao's request, with the Seed as given.
- What changed:
  - `_sample_corpora_for_topic_modeling` (`workers/topic_pipeline.py`) passes `random_seed` unchanged to `_sample_corpus` for every Data Block.
  - The Seed field (default 0) controls both the samples and the model. 100% is not sampled.
  - Help: `tutorials/topic-modeling.md`, Step 2 and Seed.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - With Seed 5 and two Data Blocks of the same documents at 20%, both samples are identical and equal to a Seed 5 sample of one Data Block; swapping the Data Blocks does not change either sample.
  - Re-running an existing Tab whose second Data Block is sampled below 100% picks a different sample for that Data Block than before.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/workers/topic_pipeline.py`, `workers/topic_modeling.py`.
- Tests: `test_topic_modeling_worker.py` (`test_each_sample_uses_the_seed_as_given_not_its_position`).
- v0.8 notes: random operations use only the seed the user set; never offset it by a Data Block's position or anything else.

#### V07-225 Topics slider keeps focus after applying; arrow keys apply after a pause (#306)

- Commits: 1930fd6f

#### V07-226 Size chips show each count with its share of its group (#307)

- Commits: 82c92eea
- Area: Topic Modelling
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-04: chips like `296 353 1 = 650` give counts (rows Add to Project yields) but not how much of each group the topic holds, while the bubble colour is weighed by exactly that share, so one document from a 15-document group can outweigh 20 from a large group without the chips showing why. Fractions such as 296/3729 were rejected as too many digits. Agreed: the percentage follows the count.
- What changed:
  - `topicSizeChips.ts`: `formatGroupShare(count, groupSize, precise?)` (whole % from 10%, one decimal below, `<0.1%`, `0%`, null without a size); chip text `"296 · 7.9%"` (labelled in hover cards: `"House 296 · 7.9%"`); title `"House: 296 of 3,729 documents (7.9%)"` (one decimal always, en thousands separators).
  - Denominators: colour-by group `documentCount` (documents in the run with that value); two Data Blocks: `corpusSizes` (documents each Data Block gave the run, after sampling), now part of the shared `TopicCorpusPresentation`. All three counts are documents.
  - `TopicSizeComposition` gives Data Block chips the title too; the HTML export prefixes the Data Block name to that title.
  - Help `topic-modeling.md`: new section Counts and percentages (anchor `help-topic-modeling-sizes`): percentages in one topic do not add to 100% (each is out of a different group; a widely shared topic can hold 70% of each); with Top topics per document above 1 a group's percentages across topics can exceed 100%; the bubble colour compares percentages, not counts.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Colour by chamber: a topic with House 138 of 713 and Senate 77 of 512 shows `138 · 19%` and `77 · 15%`; hovering the first chip shows "House: 138 of 713 documents (19.4%)".
  - Two Data Blocks of 400 and 1,200 documents, topic sizes 40 and 9: chips `40 · 10%` and `9 · 0.8%`.
  - The interactive HTML download's hover card shows the same chip text and titles.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/topic-modeling/components/results/topicSizeChips.ts`, `TopicSizeComposition.tsx`, `TopicModelingBubbleChartSection.tsx`, `topicBubbleHtmlExport.ts`; `frontend/public/tutorials/topic-modeling.md`.
- Tests: `topicSizeChips.test.ts` (formatting, colour-by, labels, two Data Blocks, unknown size); checked in Playwright WebKit (topic list, hover card, HTML payload).
- v0.8 notes: show the share next to every topic count; the share must use the same denominator as the colour weighting.

#### V07-227 Bubble map takes the canvas shape and clears the control strip (#308)

- Commits: 01d8b800
- Area: Topic Modelling
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-04, with screenshots: the map only filled a wide canvas; square and portrait canvases left wide empty bands above and below, and bubbles sat off centre to the left, under the zoom/lasso control strip. Chao classed it as a design flaw to fix in 0.7.10. Cause (frontend only; polars-text supplies PaCMAP coordinates): `normalizeTopicPositions` stretched x and y separately into a fixed 1000 x 550 plane, fit view scaled that evenly into the canvas, and the fit padding was 150 px right (unneeded: the hover card flips side) and 24 px left (under the controls).
- What changed:
  - `topicModelingGraph.ts`: `topicGraphPlaneFor(aspect)` returns a plane with the canvas aspect clamped to [1/2.5, 2.5] and the area of 1000 x 550 (bubble radii stay comparable); `normalizeTopicPositions(topics, plane)` and `buildTopicBubbleModels({..., plane})` use it; `DEFAULT_TOPIC_GRAPH_PLANE` before the first measurement.
  - `TopicModelingFlowChart.tsx`: a ResizeObserver reports the drawable aspect (pane minus fit padding) at once on first measurement and 200 ms after the last resize (`onCanvasAspectChange`); a plane change refits unless the user has panned or zoomed; hover-card side and alignment use the plane instead of the old constants; fit padding 64 px left (control strip ends about 50 px in), 24 px elsewhere.
  - `TopicModelingBubbleChartSection.tsx`: holds the measured aspect and builds the bubbles in that plane.
  - Downloads unchanged: the image and HTML exports already use the pane size and current view, so the downloaded shape is the canvas at download time.
  - Help: the map only shows closeness, is stretched to fill the chart area within 2.5:1 either way, and is laid out again on resize.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - WebKit, same result: canvases 988x450, 638x610 and 518x890 fill 83-92% of width and height with 24 px top and bottom; a 358x990 canvas (beyond the cap) keeps margins above and below.
  - No bubble overlaps the control strip; the left gap is about 64 px at every shape.
  - Resizing the chart lays the bubbles out again once the drag stops; a user zoom or pan is not undone.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/topic-modeling/components/results/topicModelingGraph.ts`, `TopicModelingFlowChart.tsx`, `TopicModelingBubbleChartSection.tsx`; `frontend/public/tutorials/topic-modeling.md`.
- Tests: `topicModelingGraph.test.ts` (plane shape, clamp, area, positions in a portrait plane); `TopicModelingFlowChart.test.tsx` (renders with a plane); Playwright WebKit measurements at four canvas shapes.
- v0.8 notes: lay topic maps out in the canvas shape (with a ratio cap), and keep fit padding clear of overlaid controls.

Reproduced in WebKit: faded bubbles disabled only their inner element while the React Flow node box kept `pointer-events: all`, and node boxes are squares with a 7 px ring margin, so faded or neighbouring boxes caught the pointer (search "pay": T8's centre hit T7).
- What changed:
  - `TopicModelingFlowChart.tsx`: node `style.pointerEvents: 'none'`, container `pointer-events-none`, rings `pointerEvents="none"`; the main circle `pointerEvents` is `visiblePainted` unless faded (`none`). Clicks bubble to React Flow's node handler, so selection is unchanged.

#### V07-239 Topic results land on the right rows of a Join Data Block; Join has one row order (#319)

- Commits: 014c5cb1
- Area: Topic Modelling
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-07 (seen since topic coverage reached Add to Project): one-line tweets showed several topics in TOPIC_coverage. The run was right (that tweet: one segment, 100% one topic); Add to Project put each document's coverage on another row. The source was a Join Data Block. Join had no fixed row order, and Polars' in-memory and streaming engines returned its rows in different orders. The run read documents in memory; Add to Project numbered the rows of a new snapshot and wrote them with sink_parquet (streaming). In Project "Test", 2,058 of 2,380 rows carried another tweet's coverage.
- What changed:
  - `ordered_join` in `services/node_operations.py`: both sides get a row number (`__wordflow_join_left_row`, `__wordflow_join_right_row`), the join runs, then rows are sorted by left then right number (right then left for a right join; unmatched full-join rows after the left rows) and the numbers are dropped. Used for every Join kind including cross. Plain `maintain_order` was not enough: a full join still differed between engines. The codebook edit's join uses `maintain_order="left"`.
  - New `shared/document_fingerprint.py`: `document_fingerprint` (sha256 over the documents with length prefixes), `require_same_documents` (InvalidInputError with `ROW_ORDER_CHANGED_MESSAGE`).
  - Topic Modelling run stores `projection_context.source_document_fingerprints` (one per source, over the full pre-sample corpus as read in memory). The registry passes it per source.
  - Add to Project (`run_topic_modeling_data_block_creation`): collects the source once in memory, checks the fingerprint when present, builds both row units from that frame, and writes with `collect().write_parquet` instead of `sink_parquet`.
  - Topic Map colours (`_topic_color_frame`): read the text column with the metadata in memory and check the fingerprint before indexing rows.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Ordered joins of a 60,000-row block return identical frames from `collect()` and `collect(engine="streaming")` for inner, left, right, full, semi, anti and cross; inner/left/semi/anti keep the left order. The plain Polars join differs on the same data.
  - Add to Project with a matching fingerprint gives each row its own document's topic; with the documents in another order it refuses: "This Data Block no longer lists its documents in the order Topic Modelling read them…". The Topic Map colours refuse the same way.
  - On the "Test" run (no fingerprint, made before the fix), every saved segment fits its row's text when the Join block is read in memory (0 misfits) and 1,179 documents misfit in streaming order, so Add to Project again from the same run gives correct rows.
- Implementation: `services/node_operations.py` (`ordered_join`), `shared/document_fingerprint.py`, `models/analysis_results.py` (`source_document_fingerprints`), `workers/topic_modeling.py`, `services/analysis_preparation_registry.py`, `services/analysis_results.py`.
- Tests: `test_join_row_order.py` (engines agree for every join kind; left order kept; cross order); `test_topic_row_order.py` (own topics per row; refusal on moved documents for Add to Project and colours); existing creation-worker tests.
- v0.8 notes: Any feature that maps run results back to rows by position must use one fixed row order (DuckDB: ORDER BY a row number; joins without ORDER BY have no order) or carry a row id through the run. Audit (2026-10-07): set_cell edits (Annotation saves) store a row position in the plan and are re-applied on every read, so they are only safe on blocks with a fixed order; Sample/Slice/Dedupe over a Join pick rows by position; Concordance, Quotation and Trends store their rows in the result and are safe.

#### V07-249 Topic Modelling embeds on all CPU cores and clusters in parallel (#329)

- Commits: polars-text 0.6.3 (`release/0.6` c984af6)
- Area: Topic Modelling
- Type: bug fix (performance)
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-07: Kelvin could not finish the Obesity corpus (26,163 articles) in 4 hours, and Chao's M5 Pro sat at 10% for over 100 minutes with the CPU at 12-13% and the GPU idle. On Mac the embedding ran through Core ML, which measured about 73 segments a second at 256 tokens against 364 on the CPU provider; HDBSCAN ran on one core.
- What changed:
  - polars-text `embedding.rs`: the `coreml` (macOS) and `directml` (Windows) ort features and execution providers are removed; every platform uses ONNX Runtime's CPU provider (`planned_provider_id()` returns `CPUExecutionProvider`). `POLARS_TEXT_EMBEDDING_THREADS` now really caps intra-op threads (inter-op 1).
  - polars-text `cluster.rs`: `hdbscan` 0.12 with the `parallel` feature and `cluster_par()`; `rayon` added to the `topic-modeling` feature.
  - Measurements (M5 Pro, all-MiniLM-L6-v2): CPU fp32 364 seg/s at 256 tokens, Core ML 73; int8 gave no gain; PyTorch MPS 727 but needs PyTorch. ORT scales to about 6 cores for MiniLM.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Activity Monitor shows several cores busy during embedding and clustering on Mac (40% or more of all cores instead of 12-13%).
  - `POLARS_TEXT_EMBEDDING_THREADS=2` keeps embedding to two cores.
- Implementation: polars-text `src/topic_modeling/embedding.rs`, `cluster.rs`, `Cargo.toml`, `README.md`; Wordflow requires `polars-text>=0.6.3`.
- Tests: polars-text Rust tests, clippy and Python tests; full Obesity run timed on the M5 Pro.
- v0.8 notes: Apply the same to `ldaca-rs`: CPU provider with all cores (Core ML is slower for MiniLM), parallel HDBSCAN, and a thread cap for servers. Stop in v0.7 kills the worker process, which works at any stage; v0.8's cooperative checkpoints cannot interrupt clustering (Chao keeps v0.7's behaviour).

#### V07-250 Automatic segments pack paragraphs up to Max tokens; Paragraph mode cuts long paragraphs at the middle sentence (#331)

- Commits: polars-text 0.6.3 (`release/0.6` c984af6); help and tooltips 2a35efac
- Area: Topic Modelling
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-07: Automatic made one segment per paragraph and never packed, so news articles with many one-sentence paragraphs gave about 534,000 segments for the Obesity corpus, 84% under 64 tokens. Sampling showed packing neighbours under 256 tokens gives about 105,000 (5.1x fewer). Agreed: pack in Automatic only; Paragraph keeps at most one paragraph per segment. The first timed run still made 238,095 segments: 6% of the articles store their body as one long line (median 503 tokens), and an oversized paragraph became one segment per sentence (about 34 for one body), 57% of all segments. Chao: one long paragraph into dozens of sentence segments is not the design; Automatic merges while the result fits the window, and Paragraph cuts a long paragraph in the middle at a sentence boundary.
- What changed:
  - polars-text `segmentation.rs`: `DocumentSplitter::pack_paragraphs` adds the next paragraph to the open segment while the span from its first to its last paragraph fits the budget, and starts a new segment otherwise. A paragraph is never cut to make room. A paragraph over the budget on its own closes the open segment, and its sentences are packed the same way within it (`pack_into`, `close_group`); a sentence still over the budget goes to `split_unit`. Units with no letters or digits never open or close a segment.
  - Line mode (Paragraph): `split_block` now cuts an oversized line at the sentence boundary nearest its middle (`middle_sentence_split`, both sides at least the tiny-fragment minimum), recursively, and falls back to `split_unit` without a usable boundary. Before, it made one segment per sentence.
  - Line (Paragraph in the UI) and Sentence never pack across paragraphs or sentences; Sentence is unchanged.
  - Help `topic-modeling.md` (Segments table and text, Max tokens) and the Segments and Max tokens tooltips describe packing.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - `a b.\n\nc d.\n\ne f.` at 4 tokens gives `a b.\n\nc d.` and `e f.`; `a b c.\n\nd e f.\n\ng.` gives `a b c.` and `d e f.\n\ng.` (no paragraph cut to fill).
  - An oversized paragraph between short ones closes the group and packs its own sentences (`C d. E f. G h.` at 4 tokens gives `C d. E f.` and `G h.`); `* * *` dividers alone give no segment.
  - Paragraph mode on `a b.\nc d.` still gives two segments; `A b. C d. E f. G h.` at 4 tokens gives `A b. C d.` and `E f. G h.`; `Mr.` is never split off alone.
- Implementation: polars-text `src/topic_modeling/segmentation.rs`; `frontend/public/tutorials/topic-modeling.md`; `TopicModelingParameterPanel.tsx` tooltips.
- Tests: polars-text `segmentation.rs` unit tests (packing, no cut to fill, oversized paragraph packs its sentences, content-free dividers, line mode middle sentence split and abbreviation guard, line mode never packs); Obesity segment count.
- v0.8 notes: `ldaca-rs` already packs units; check it packs only whole paragraphs (or whole sentences within one long paragraph) in Automatic, never cutting a fitting unit to fill a segment; Paragraph cuts a long paragraph at the middle sentence boundary; Sentence never packs.

#### V07-251 Topic sampling for very large corpora, with an estimated segment count and a first-run note (#330)

- Commits: polars-text 0.6.3 (`release/0.6` c984af6); Wordflow 2a35efac
- Area: Topic Modelling
- Type: new feature
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-07: large corpora never finished, and the run gave no idea of its cost. The first design (ticked above 10,000 documents or 20,000 segments, 20,000-segment sample) was dropped after measurement: with paragraph packing the Obesity corpus (26,163 articles, 107,551 segments) clusters every segment in 47 s, embedding takes most of a first run (about 12 minutes; sampling cannot shorten it), and a 20,000-segment sample merged 96% of segments into one topic. Agreed: off by default, suggested above a measured threshold, an editable sample with a grey suggestion, and plain notes on the time and quality trade-off.
- What changed:
  - polars-text `sample.rs`: `sample_indices(n, limit, seed)` (SplitMix64 partial Fisher-Yates, sorted, platform independent) and `assign_to_nearest_sampled` (cosine 1-NN in parallel; sampled segments keep their label; outliers keep the sample's share). `topic_modeling(..., cluster_sample_size=N)`: with more segments than N, PaCMAP and HDBSCAN run on the sample and every other segment takes its nearest sampled segment's label. Output field `clustered_segments` (UInt32, null when every segment was clustered). The projection context keeps every segment's span, label and full embedding, so Number of topics and per-topic detach cover the whole corpus.
  - Backend: `TopicModelingAnalysisRequest.cluster_sample_size: int | None = Field(None, ge=1000)`, threaded through the preparation registry, `TopicModelingInput`, `run_topic_modeling_analysis`, `_compute_topic_modeling`, `_compute_topic_payload` and `_run_rust_topic_modeling` (passed to polars-text only when set). `TopicClustering.clustered_segments` stores the sample size.
  - Frontend `topicSampling.ts`: `TOPIC_SAMPLE_SUGGESTION` 100,000, `TOPIC_SAMPLING_SUGGEST_ABOVE` 150,000 segments, `TOPIC_FIRST_RUN_NOTE_TOKENS` 5,000,000, `TOPIC_SAMPLE_MIN` 1,000, `sanitizeTopicSampleSize`, `smallestFindableTopic`, and one SQL estimate per Data Block (`segments`: Automatic `SUM(GREATEST(1, CEIL(LENGTH / (4 x Max tokens))))`, Paragraph non-empty lines, Sentence sentence marks; `characters`: `SUM(LENGTH)`), times the sampling fraction.
  - Panel: the unticked checkbox always shows "About N segments (estimated); every segment is clustered."; above 150,000 it warns that clustering time grows with the square of the segments and suggests sampling. Ticked, **Segments to sample** is empty with the suggestion in grey: Run uses it, Tab fills it (`acceptPlaceholderOnTab`), typing replaces it; the note names the sample, the estimated total, the seed and the smallest findable topic. Above 5 million estimated tokens a first-run note under the Data Blocks gives a rough time (36,000 tokens a second, a floor). The last-run summary says "topics found from a sample of S (seed K)". Parameter state `clusterSample` (false) and `clusterSampleSize` (`null` = suggestion); saved runs restore both.
  - Help: `help-topic-modeling-topic-sampling` rewritten (off by default, grey suggestion and Tab, why it helps, what it costs, what it does not change), Step 2 and the defaults table link to it; the Seed section no longer promises identical topics (PaCMAP is not bit-exact across runs, also in 0.6.2).
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Obesity (26,163 documents, Automatic, 256 tokens): the note reads about 110,000 segments, no sampling suggestion; the first-run note says about 25 million tokens and 12 minutes or more.
  - At Max tokens 64 (about 500,000 segments) the unticked note warns and suggests sampling.
  - Ticking shows Segments to sample empty with 100,000 in grey; Run sends 100,000; Tab fills it; typing 50000 sends 50,000; the note shows the smallest findable topic (10 x 400,000 / 100,000 = 40).
  - Segment integrity on the Obesity runs (sampled and full): every document has segments, 100% of letters and digits are in a segment, spans ordered, disjoint and trimmed, topic ids valid, and each document's coverage equals its span shares.
- Implementation: polars-text `src/topic_modeling/sample.rs`, `mod.rs`, `plugin.rs`, `projection.rs`, `reduce.rs` (comment), `polars_text/namespace.py`; backend `domain/workspace/analysis.py`, `services/analysis_preparation_registry.py`, `workers/invocations.py`, `workers/entrypoints.py`, `workers/topic_modeling.py`, `workers/topic_pipeline.py`, `models/analysis_results.py`; frontend `features/views/topic-modeling/topicSampling.ts`, `TopicModelingFeature.tsx`, `TopicModelingParameterPanel.tsx`, `hooks/topicModelingParameterState.ts`, `useTopicModelingParameters.ts`, `useTopicModelingTaskFlow.ts`, `lib/queryKeys.ts`, `tutorials/bundledRegistry.ts`, `public/tutorials/topic-modeling.md`.
- Tests: polars-text `sample.rs` and segmentation span-invariant tests, Python API test; backend `test_topic_modeling_worker.py`, `test_topic_modeling_segmentation_contract.py` (off by default, minimum 1,000); frontend `topicSampling.test.ts`, `topicModelingParameterState.test.ts`, `TopicModelingParameterPanel.test.tsx` (estimate line, suggestion warning, grey suggestion with Tab, trade-off note, first-run note, Learn more); full Obesity runs timed and checked for segment integrity.
- v0.8 notes: Port the mode with the same behaviour: off by default, estimate shown, suggestion above a measured threshold, editable sample with a grey suggestion, smallest-findable-topic hint, and a first-run note from the token count. Re-measure the thresholds on the v0.8 pipeline. v0.8 can count segments exactly if DuckDB stores them.

#### V07-256 Max topic size Auto judges by documents and explains its decision (#336)

- Commits: polars-text 0.6.4 (release/0.6 1590710, 6e7a875); Wordflow 0dcd271c
- Area: Topic Modelling
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-08: Auto often left one giant topic (5,000 of 6,000 documents) while a fixed Max of 2,000 split it well. Auto triggered only when one topic held more than half of the segments, which depends on the segmentation mode, and gave no reason when it did nothing. Decided: trigger on documents, explain the decision in the last-run line, and show in the Max field the size Auto worked with so the user knows which way to change it. Obesity sweep (cached embeddings): Min 10 gives 765 topics, no dominant topic (2% of documents); Min 30 to 200 give 6 to 14 topics with one topic the main topic of 96% of documents, which Auto keeps because splitting it leaves most of its segments as outliers. Chao also asked not to merge to a default of 100 topics: the run opens with every topic it found.
- What changed:
  - polars-text `cluster.rs`: `cluster(points, min, max, documents: Option<PointDocuments>)`; Auto loops while one topic is the main topic (most characters, as in the rollup) of more than half of the documents with segments; `AutoOutcome { decision: NotNeeded | Split | Kept, document_share }`; `largest_topic_size(labels)`. `mod.rs` passes each segment's document and characters (the sample's with topic sampling); output fields `auto_decision`, `auto_document_share`, `largest_topic_size` (of the clustered points).
  - Backend: `TopicClustering.auto_decision`, `auto_document_share`, `largest_topic_size` parsed in `topic_pipeline.py` and stored by `topic_modeling.py`.
  - Frontend `TopicModelingParameterPanel.tsx`: last-run line by decision (not needed with the largest topic's size; split with the share and cap; kept with a hint to try a fixed Max below the largest topic); the empty Max topic size field shows the applied cap or the largest topic size as its placeholder after an Auto run, Tab fills it (`acceptPlaceholderOnTab`).
  - Help `topic-modeling.md` Max topic size section.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Three blobs of 10 segments, one spread over 10 documents and the others in one document each: by documents Auto triggers (share 10/12); counted by segments it would not.
  - Obesity at Min 30: the line reads that one topic is the main topic of 96% of documents and was kept, try a fixed Max below the largest topic; the Max field shows that size in grey.
  - A fixed Max reports no Auto decision.
- Implementation: polars-text `src/topic_modeling/cluster.rs`, `mod.rs`, `plugin.rs`, `projection.rs`; backend `workers/topic_pipeline.py`, `workers/topic_modeling.py`, `models/analysis_results.py`; frontend `TopicModelingParameterPanel.tsx`, `TopicModelingFeature.tsx`; `public/tutorials/topic-modeling.md`.
- Tests: polars-text `cluster.rs` (documents trigger, main topic by characters, largest topic size, fixed Max no decision); backend `test_topic_modeling_worker.py::test_run_rust_topic_modeling_reports_the_auto_decision`; frontend `TopicModelingParameterPanel.test.tsx` (decision lines, grey size and Tab, Auto before a run); Obesity Min topic size sweep.
- v0.8 notes: Judge Auto by documents and explain its decision in the UI; show the size Auto used in the Max field. Do not merge the natural topics to a default count.

#### V07-257 Topic chart highlights match All Topics, and the chart never waits to measure its bubbles (#337)

- Commits: f3c00f82
- Area: Topic Modelling
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-08 (macOS desktop pre-release, Obesity 751 topics merged to 120): searching "Sta" listed 2 topics but about a dozen bubbles stayed lit; the chart often went blank, +/- did not help and Fit did nothing until a page zoom. Two causes: All Topics filters by lasso and search while the chart faded only by search; and React Flow hides unmeasured nodes and holds fitView until every node has a measured size, while the chart rebuilt its nodes without sizes, so a webview whose ResizeObserver did not report left the chart blank and Fit's promise unresolved.
- What changed:
  - `topicModelingGraph.ts`: `filteredOut` is true outside an active lasso or when not matching the search, the same rule as All Topics.
  - `TopicModelingFlowChart.tsx`: each node carries `width`, `height` and `measured` (its known diameter); `fitBubbles()` sets React Flow's pane `width`/`height` from the pane's `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight` (via `useStoreApi`) before every fit (load, relayout, resize, Fit button).
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Playwright WebKit with ResizeObserver disabled, production build: before 0 of 121 nodes visible and Fit a no-op; after, all bubbles visible, fitted on load, Fit changes the view.
  - Normal WebKit: lasso 33 lit and 33 listed; lasso plus "Sta" 3 and 3; merge to 120 and "Sta" 9 and 9.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/topic-modeling/components/results/topicModelingGraph.ts`, `TopicModelingFlowChart.tsx`.
- Tests: `topicModelingGraph.test.ts` (lit = lasso and search), `TopicModelingFlowChart.test.tsx` (store mock); WebKit imitation of a webview without ResizeObserver; full frontend suite (1,696).
- v0.8 notes: Give chart nodes their known size so nothing depends on ResizeObserver timing in the desktop webview; keep chart highlighting identical to the topic list's filter (lasso and search).

#### V07-260 Topic Modelling embeds on every processor it may use, also on small VMs and in containers (#341)

- Commits: 440b827b
- Area: Topic Modelling
- Type: bug fix (performance)
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-08: on a 2-vCPU Windows VM (0.7.11 pre-release, CPU provider) Topic Modelling rarely used more than 50% of the CPU. ONNX Runtime starts one intra-op thread per physical core by default, and Wordflow set POLARS_TEXT_EMBEDDING_THREADS only as an opt-in cap; a VM presenting one core with two hyperthreads embedded on one thread. The default was only measured on an M5 Pro (physical = logical cores). In a container with a CPU quota (BinderHub) ONNX Runtime may count the host's cores instead.
- What changed:
  - New `workers/cpu.py`: `available_cpus()` = `os.process_cpu_count()` capped by a Linux cgroup v2 (`cpu.max`) or v1 (`cpu.cfs_quota_us`/`cpu.cfs_period_us`) quota; `default_embedding_threads()` sets `POLARS_TEXT_EMBEDDING_THREADS` to it unless already set.
  - `run_topic_modeling_analysis` calls it first; each run is a fresh worker process, and polars-text reads the variable whenever it builds an embedding session.
  - Hosted runbook: the unset default now means every processor the worker may use, capped by a container quota.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - 2-vCPU VM: embedding uses both processors (about 2x).
  - cgroup quota 2 on a larger host: 2 threads.
  - An explicit POLARS_TEXT_EMBEDDING_THREADS (hosted server: 8) is kept.
  - Macs unchanged; laptops with hyperthreading roughly neutral.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/workers/cpu.py`, `workers/topic_modeling.py`; `docs/runbooks/hosted-deployment.md`.
- Tests: `test_worker_cpu.py` (cgroup v2 and v1 quotas, no quota, explicit setting kept).
- v0.8 notes: Size the embedding thread pool from the processors the process may use (affinity and container quota), not ONNX Runtime's physical-core default; still parallelise segmentation and topic words.

#### V07-261 Find topics shows the matched words bold orange in the topic lists and bubble word cloud (#342)

- Commits: 6cdf710f
- Area: Topic Modelling
- Type: interface change
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-08: Find topics filters the lists and lights the matching bubbles, but in a cluster of words it is hard to see the actual hit, for example which word matched `famil*` (family, familiarisation), or that a topic has several hits. Chao asked for bold plus a different font colour, with no change to word order or font size, and for the full highlighted list in a tooltip because the list line is truncated. Chao: bold orange everywhere is fine (no bubble colour).
- What changed:
  - `matchedTopicWords(words, query, matchOption)` in `topicModelingAdapters.ts`: the words that match the Find topics text on their own, using the same `matchChecklistOption` rule (wildcards included).
  - New `TopicWords.tsx`: `TopicWordsLine` renders the comma-separated words with matches as `<strong class="font-semibold text-chart-4">` (theme chart orange) and a Radix tooltip with the same highlighted list; it replaces the plain `title` on both list lines in `TopicSelectionPanel`.
  - `TopicBubbleModel.matchedWords` is computed in `buildTopicBubbleModels`; the hover word cloud passes `{color, bold: true}` for those words, with the colour resolved from `--vscode-charts-orange` at hover time (`topicMatchColor()`), because the ECharts SVG text cannot read CSS variables.
  - `ResponsiveWordCloud` accepts a per-word `bold` flag (per-word `fontWeight`).
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Search `famil*`: family and familiarisation are bold orange in both lists and in the hover cloud; other words unchanged; order and sizes unchanged.
  - Hovering a list line shows all of the topic's words with the same highlighting.
  - No search: no highlighting, and no colour lookup per bubble.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/topic-modeling/topicModelingAdapters.ts`, `components/results/TopicWords.tsx`, `TopicSelectionPanel.tsx`, `topicModelingGraph.ts`, `TopicModelingFlowChart.tsx`; `common/components/ResponsiveWordCloud.tsx`.
- Tests: `topicModelingAdapters.test.ts` (wildcard matches, empty search), `TopicSelectionPanel.test.tsx` (bold orange matches, tooltip), `TopicModelingFlowChart.test.tsx` (hover cloud bold orange), `ResponsiveWordCloud.test.tsx` (per-word fontWeight).
- v0.8 notes: Highlight the words matching the topic search in the topic lists (with the full list on hover) and in the bubble word cloud: bold, theme orange, same order and size.

#### V07-272 Example texts of a topic beside a one-column topic list (#353)

- Commits: dd9de3d7, 40d3b289, 362d916c, 2eedd431, 8de3728a, d9d131b3, aeec808 (polars-text 0.6.6)
- Area: Topic Modelling
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: Monika (Qualtrics and email, 2026-10-08): "Is it possible to see some of the texts (not just the word clusters) for each topic?" Chao: the two-column topic list wasted width; one list in the left third with selected topics pinned in their own scrolling group, the right two thirds for the shown topic's segments, chosen with an eye at the card's tail or by right-clicking a bubble, with typical or random pages and a document viewer. Segment embeddings were not stored, so typicality needed compact embeddings in the run's context (Chao accepted they stay invisible, not in Data Blocks).
- What changed:
  - polars-text 0.6.6: projection context v4 stores 64 seeded PCA components per segment as i8 codes plus a per-segment f32 scale (about 74 bytes a segment); `topic_segment_similarities(context, topic_count, topic_id)` gives each segment of a (merged) topic its cosine similarity to the topic centre. v3 contexts list segments with similarity None.
  - Backend: POST /analyses/{id}/result/topic-segments/query (cluster_count, topic_id, order typical|random with the run's seed, one_per_document default true, corpus_index, group_column/group_value matching the Colour by labels, page, page_size) returns segment and document counts, items with code-point offsets, text, similarity, typicality 0-100 (share of the topic's segments less similar) and string metadata; POST .../topic-document/query returns a document's text, every segment's span and topic, and metadata. Both check the source documents still match the run.
  - Topic list: one column with Selected (up to 40% of the height, Clear) and Others groups that scroll separately; heading "Topics n" or "Topics m / n" while Find topics or the lasso filters; selected topics stay listed, faded when filtered out; compact cards with the size chips next to the title and a full-height eye strip (aria "Show examples of Topic N" / "Stop showing Topic N"); a draggable divider sets the list's width (20% to 70%, default a third, remembered per browser, double-click resets).
  - Bubbles: right-click shows or stops showing a topic (not on faded bubbles), an eye badge marks the shown bubble, the hover card says "Right-click to show examples". Left click still selects. Filters never cancel the shown topic; the pane notes when it is filtered out, with Clear filters, and × stops showing it. The shown topic resets when the Topics slider changes (topic ids change).
  - Examples pane: Most typical / Random, One per document, Colour by value and Data Block filters, Label column, pages of 5; each card shows about 3 lines (Show more), the topic's words bold in the bubble's base colour, a "Top N%" badge (green top quarter, amber middle half, grey bottom quarter; similarity in the tooltip), and Open document. Runs without embeddings note "Run Topic Modelling again to rank them" and show no badge.
  - Document window: the shared Row Details dialog (new title, anchor and Previous/Next labels) with the topic's words under the title, the opened segment tinted with the topic colour and the topic's other segments more lightly (coloured text was hard to read in some colours), the topic's words bold and italic inside its segments, other topics grey, Previous/Next segment.
  - Find topics moved into the Result settings row; stop words became an unlabelled switch plus a dropdown resting on "Use stop words" (showing the saved list while on, "Don't use stop words" turns it off, the list is kept). Opt-in props, so Frequency is unchanged.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Right-clicking an active bubble shows its examples beside the list, ranked most typical first with Top N% badges; right-clicking again, the card's eye or × stops showing them.
  - Typing a search that hides the shown topic keeps its examples, with a note and Clear filters.
  - Open document highlights every segment and steps through the shown topic's segments.
- Implementation: polars-text `src/topic_modeling/projection.rs`, `polars_text/topic_projection.py`; backend `analysis/topic_examples.py`, `services/analysis_results.py` (`topic_segments`, `topic_document`), `models/analysis_results.py`, `api/workspaces/analyses.py`, `analysis/topic_metadata_colors.py` (`group_label`); frontend `TopicSelectionPanel.tsx`, `TopicExamplesPane.tsx`, `TopicDocumentDialog.tsx`, `topicExamplesModel.ts`, `TopicModelingFlowChart.tsx`, `TopicModelingBubbleChartSection.tsx`, `TopicModelingResultsPanel.tsx`, `TopicModelingStopWordsControl.tsx`, `StopWordsLanguageSelect.tsx`, `StopWordsEnabledSwitch.tsx`, `RowDetailPanel.tsx`.
- Tests: polars-text Rust tests (ranking, binary round trip, v3 contexts) and `test_progress.py`; backend `test_topic_examples.py`, API surface; frontend `TopicSelectionPanel`, `TopicExamplesPane`, `TopicDocumentDialog`, `topicExamplesModel`, `TopicModelingFlowChart`, `TopicModelingStopWordsControl`, `TopicModelingResultsPanel` tests; live API (0.16 s per page on 32,266 segments) and WebKit checks on the test instance.
- v0.8 notes: Keep the one-column list with Selected/Others, eye strip and bubble right-click, the examples pane (typical/random with the run seed, one per document, typicality rank badge, never called relevance or confidence) and the document window. Store per-segment embeddings (or compact codes) with the run so typicality survives; DuckDB can hold them as a hidden table.

### Quotation

#### V07-001 Quotation Run All fails validation, no "Add to Workspace" (#110)

- Commits: aa22e185
- Area: Quotation
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Running Quotation "Run All" failed in the Tasks centre with `ValidationError ... QuotationRunAllWorkerResult source.source_document_count Field required`. The vNext contract cutover (3693d82a, first shipped in v0.7.7) made `source_document_count` required on `RunAllSourceDescriptor`; the Concordance worker was updated but the Quotation worker was not. With no successful Run All Result, `runAllSource` stays null and the tab falls back to the Preview "Search Results" panel, which has no "Add to Workspace" button.
- What changed: The Quotation Run All worker now reports `source_document_count` (number of all source documents, `len(node_corpus)`) next to `document_count` (documents with at least one quotation, `quote_df.height`) and `match_count`.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Quotation Run All completes; the Review panel shows with an "Add to Workspace" button.
  - The worker result validates against `QuotationRunAllWorkerResult`.
  - For a corpus with a document containing no quotations, `source_document_count` > `document_count`.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/workers/quotation.py` `run_quotation_run_all` (source descriptor dict).
- Tests: `backend/tests/unit/test_quotation_run_all_worker.py` now validates the output dict against the worker result model and includes a document with no quotations.
- v0.8 notes: Any Run All style worker contract must carry both the total source document count and the matched document count; validate worker outputs against the result model in tests (the old test only checked selected keys).

#### V07-002 Quote Extraction cells bounded, clamped, with full-text hover tooltip (#111, #112)

- Commits: fcad731d, 844b9750, c67c040e
- Area: Quotation
- Type: bug fix (layout) + feature (tooltip)
- Problem / reason: The result table frame used `min-w-max`, so long quotation context made the Quote Extraction column extremely wide (#111). Users needed a way to read the full context of a clamped cell (#112). Review feedback: 32rem too narrow; tooltip blended into the table (same surface colour); tooltip opened only intermittently.
- What changed:
  - Quote Extraction (document pseudo-column) cells wrap inside a fixed width of 42rem (first 32rem in fcad731d, widened about 30% in 844b9750) and are clamped to 3 lines with an ellipsis (`line-clamp-3`, `whitespace-normal break-words`). Metadata columns unchanged.
  - Hovering a cell shows a tooltip with the complete highlighted context (same speaker/verb/quote badges as the cell), only when the clamp actually hides content (`scrollHeight > clientHeight + 1`). Short cells show no tooltip, by design. Tooltip open delay 300ms.
  - Tooltip styling: side bottom, align start, `sideOffset` 8px, `alignOffset` 48px (shifted right so it does not read as the next row), info-tinted background (`bg-info-background`), 2px focus-coloured border (`border-2 border-focus`), large shadow (`--vscode-shadow-lg`), rounded, max width `max-w-2xl`, an arrow (14x7, `fill-focus`) pointing back at the cell. Content scrolls inside an inner wrapper capped at 60vh so the bubble does not clip its arrow. All colours are theme tokens (light and dark).
  - Intermittent tooltip fix: hovering a highlighted segment lifts hover state into `QuotationFeature`, which re-renders the node block and rebuilds column defs. TanStack `flexRender` mounts a function `cell` as a React component, so each rebuilt closure was a new component type, remounting every body cell and resetting the Radix tooltip's open state and delay timer. Body cells now call a local `renderCell(columnName, row, rowId)` directly (headers still use `flexRender`). Side effect: highlight spans stop flickering on hover.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - A long Quote Extraction cell is 42rem wide and shows at most 3 lines.
  - Hovering a clamped cell for about 300ms opens a tinted, outlined tooltip with the full context and an arrow; a cell whose content fits shows no tooltip.
  - Moving the pointer across highlighted words inside the cell does not close or restart the tooltip.
  - Re-rendering the block with new hover state keeps the same cell DOM node (no remount).
- Implementation: new `frontend/src/features/views/quotation/components/QuotationClampedCell.tsx` (Radix tooltip from `@/components/ui/tooltip` plus `TooltipPrimitive.Arrow` from `radix-ui`, controlled `open` gated by `isClamped()`); `QuotationNodeBlock.tsx` (`renderCell`, body cells no longer through `flexRender`).
- Tests: `quotation/components/__tests__/QuotationClampedCell.test.tsx` (width/clamp classes, tooltip only when clamped, none when content fits); `__tests__/QuotationNodeBlock.test.tsx` (regression: cell DOM node survives hover-state re-render).
- v0.8 notes: Keep the fixed-width 3-line clamp and clamp-gated tooltip. In any TanStack table whose column defs capture changing state, do not render stateful cells through `flexRender` with inline closures (remount hazard); call a render function directly or memoise the column defs.

#### V07-003 Row details opens scrolled to the extracted quote (#113)

- Commits: 75a950f8
- Area: Quotation (shared Row details panel)
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: The Row details "Document" box always opened at the top of the full text, so users had to scroll and search for the quotation.
- What changed: Detail renderers can mark one element with `data-row-detail-anchor`. The shared `RowDetailPanel` scrolls only the Document box (not the whole dialog) so the anchor sits near the top with a 48px context margin above it, on open and on previous/next row navigation. Without an anchor the box scrolls to the top. Summary fields above stay in view. Quotation marks its first highlighted segment, so in "Documents" paging mode a document with several quotes opens at the first one. Other users of the panel (Concordance) are unaffected until they add an anchor.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Opening Row details for a quotation row shows the Document box scrolled so the first highlighted quote is about 48px below the box top.
  - Navigating to the next/previous row re-applies the scroll.
  - The dialog's summary fields remain at the top of the dialog.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/common/components/RowDetailPanel.tsx` (`scrollDocumentToAnchor`, `DOCUMENT_ANCHOR_MARGIN_PX = 48`, `data-testid="row-detail-document"`); the Document box is tracked with a state-backed callback ref (`useState` + `ref={setDocumentBox}`) because Radix mounts dialog content one render after `open` flips, so a plain `useRef` is still null when the effect first runs. `quotation/components/quotationDetailText.tsx` `renderQuotationDetailText` adds `data-row-detail-anchor` to the first segment with types.
- Tests: `common/components/__tests__/RowDetailPanel.test.tsx` (scroll offset).
- v0.8 notes: Keep the generic anchor contract so other analyses (Concordance) can opt in. Radix dialog content mount timing: use a callback/state ref, not a plain ref, for post-open effects.

#### V07-046 Quotation Review: controls in one compact row (#160)

- Commits: 209990d7
- Area: Quotation
- Type: behaviour change (layout)
- Problem / reason: Label sizes were mixed ("Context length (words per side)" larger), the "Enter a whole number between 0 and 2000." hint was always shown, and controls sat on uneven rows.
- What changed: One row: Page by (Documents / Matches, removed next commit), "Context" [n] "words per side" with a help icon (tooltip "Words shown on each side of a quotation, from 0 to 2000." using `MAX_CONTEXT_LENGTH`), then the Show metadata selector. Labels are small secondary text; the number input is `h-8 w-20`. The range message appears only when invalid (`aria-invalid`, `aria-describedby`). The per-block "Text column: ..." note becomes a small caption without the divider.

- Tests: none added.

#### V07-047 Quotation Review: page by matches only; shorter document box in Row Details (#161)

- Commits: 6f263cfd
- Area: Quotation, Row Details
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: Paging by documents could make a page unexpectedly long. In Row Details the tall document box hid the metadata, which tells users when several extractions come from the same document.
- What changed: The Page by choice is removed; Review always pages by matches. `RowDetailPanel` accepts `documentMaxHeightClassName` (default `max-h-96`); Quotation uses `max-h-48` (12rem, about seven lines), so metadata (file_path, base_name, ...) shows below. The box still scrolls to the quotation and can be scrolled for more context.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - No "Page by" control in Quotation Review; each page has at most page-size matches.
  - Quotation Row Details shows metadata below the document without scrolling the panel.

#### V07-060 Quotation: explain the quote types (#174)

- Commits: 9ff1074c (follow-up efea30af puts the explanation on its own smaller line, outside this range)
- Area: Quotation, Row Details, Docs
- Type: feature / docs
- Problem / reason: User feedback: Quote Type values (QCQSV, CSV, SVC, AccordingTo, Heuristic) were shown raw and unexplained.
- What changed: Row Details "Quote Type" shows "<code>: <Description>". Letter codes list parts in sentence order: Q quotation mark, C content, V verb, S speaker; C becomes "the quote in quotation marks" when the code contains Q, else "the quote (reported speech)", joined with ", then ". Specials: QCQ "a quote in quotation marks that continues the previous quote, whose speaker it takes"; AccordingTo 'attributed with "according to"'; Heuristic "text between quotation marks found by a fallback rule; the speaker and verb are the nearest ones and may be missing". Unknown codes show raw. Tutorial adds a quote-type legend with examples and notes that exported speaker, verb and quote columns carry start and end offsets.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - "QCQVS" shows "QCQVS: The quote in quotation marks, then the verb, then the speaker".
  - "SVC" shows "SVC: The speaker, then the verb, then the quote (reported speech)".
  - An unknown code such as "XYZ" shows "XYZ".
- Implementation: new `frontend/src/features/views/quotation/quoteTypes.ts` (`describeQuoteType`, `formatQuoteType`); `quotationRowDetail.ts`; `tutorials/quotation.md`.
- Tests: `frontend/src/features/views/quotation/__tests__/quoteTypes.test.ts`.
- v0.8 notes: Frontend-only mapping; keep it in sync with the quotation extractor's codes.

#### V07-063 Quotation: Date and Datetime columns shown as dates (#177)

- Commits: e09712a4
- Area: Quotation, Row Details
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Quotation decodes Arrow rows with its own native decoder (`nativeRows` in `quotationArrowPage.ts`) so Int64 character offsets stay exact (bigint). That decoder skipped the shared Date/Timestamp formatting from #165, so a source Date showed as epoch milliseconds (2020-01-31 as `1580428800000`) in Row Details and in the results table metadata columns, in both Preview and Run All Review. Also a List<Int64> or Struct metadata column threw in Row Details because `JSON.stringify` cannot write bigint. Display only: Add to Project and Export were already correct.
- What changed: Top-level Date columns show as `YYYY-MM-DD`; Timestamp columns show as a UTC ISO string (`new Date(ms).toISOString()`). Int64 offsets stay native bigint. Row Details writes nested bigint values as decimal strings.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - A Quotation source Date column value 2020-01-31 shows "2020-01-31" in the results table and Row Details (Preview and Run All).
  - A Datetime shows as an ISO timestamp.
  - Opening Row Details for a row with a List<Int64> metadata column does not throw and shows the numbers.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/lib/arrow/arrowTable.ts` new exported `formatArrowTemporalValue(value, type)` (shared with `normalizeArrowValue`); `features/views/quotation/quotationArrowPage.ts` `nativeRows` applies it to schema fields where `DataType.isDate || DataType.isTimestamp`; `RowDetailPanel.tsx` `formatMetadataValue` uses a `JSON.stringify` replacer converting bigint to string.
- Tests: `RowDetailPanel.test.tsx` ("shows nested big integers and leaves missing values empty"), `quotationArrowPage.test.ts` ("shows source Date and Timestamp columns as dates, keeping Int64 offsets exact").
- v0.8 notes: Any decoder that keeps Int64 as bigint for exact offsets must still format temporal columns. Timestamps display in UTC ISO form here (not local time). If v0.8 still uses Arrow from DuckDB, DATE arrives as Date32 (days) and TIMESTAMP may arrive with a different unit; format both.
