#### V07-051 Dates: show Date columns as dates; convert them to datetime directly (#165)

- Commits: 86246a5d
- Area: Data Editor (table display, type conversion)
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: User feedback (Excel metadata with a DD/MM/YYYY column). Arrow Date values reached the table as epoch milliseconds (31 Jan 2020 showed as 1580428800000). Converting a Date column to datetime failed ("Sample validation failed ... expected String, got date") because the cast always used `str.strptime`.
- What changed: Arrow Date values render as "YYYY-MM-DD". Date to Datetime casts directly (`cast(pl.Datetime("us"))`); Date to text with a format uses strftime like datetimes. The plan reads back for Project save and load.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - A Date cell for 2020-01-31 displays "2020-01-31".
  - Converting a Date column to datetime succeeds and yields midnight datetimes.
  - Converting a Date column to text with "%d/%m/%Y" gives "31/01/2020".
- Implementation: `normalizeArrowValue` in `frontend/src/lib/arrow/arrowTable.ts` (`DataType.isDate` then `new Date(ms).toISOString().slice(0, 10)`); `_datetime_cast_expr` and `_cast_expr` in `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/services/node_casting.py`.
- Tests: `backend/tests/unit/test_node_casting.py::test_date_columns_convert_to_datetime_without_text_parsing`; `frontend/src/lib/arrow/__tests__/arrowTable.test.ts`.
- v0.8 notes: The frontend conversion uses UTC, which is correct for epoch-day values; do not use local time. DuckDB DATE to TIMESTAMP is a direct cast.

#### V07-062 Missing values shown as empty, not "null" (#176)

- Commits: a7fc7002
- Area: Tables, Data Builder (Filter, previews), Data Editor, Row Details, Docs
- Type: wording / behaviour change
- Problem / reason: "Null" is computer-science jargon for HASS researchers. #166 had already made Filter treat missing, NaN and blank text as one "empty" idea, but other places still said `(null)`, `(undefined)`, `(empty string)`, `NaN` or `null`.
- What changed:
  - Preview tables (`formatPreviewValue`, used by Filter, Join, Stack and other Data Builder previews) show missing (null/undefined), NaN and blank/whitespace-only text as an empty cell.
  - Row Details metadata shows missing and NaN as empty (was `null`).
  - Data Editor "First row on this page:" sample shows italic "empty" for null, NaN and blank text (NaN used to show as "null" via `JSON.stringify`).
  - Filter value checklist: the missing-value entry is "(empty)" (was "Null (no value)"); a blank or whitespace-only text value is "(blank text)" (was "(empty string)").
  - Filter missing-values toast description: "Rows with missing values won’t match ordinary filter conditions. Choose “is empty” to target them." (was "is null").
  - Auto-generated block names use `empty` for a null condition value (was `null`).
  - Concordance tutorial: "Sorting is case-sensitive, and empty values come first in either direction." (replaces "Polars' default null ordering").
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - A preview cell holding null, undefined, NaN, "" or "   " renders as an empty string.
  - `formatPreviewValue(0)` is "0", `false` is "false", `{a:1}` is `{"a":1}`.
  - Filter checklist for values `['', '  ', 'a']` with nulls present lists "(empty)", "(blank text)", "(blank text)", "a" (the two blank strings remain separate entries).
  - The Data Editor sample says "empty" for NaN.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/features/views/preprocessing/utils/typeUtils.ts` (new exported `isEmptyValue`, `formatPreviewValue`), `filter/utils/categoricalOptions.ts` (`buildCategoricalOptionEntries`), `filter/components/FilterValueChecklist.tsx`, `filter/hooks/useFilterSubTabSections.tsx`, `utils/autoNodeNames.ts` (`formatScalar`), `views/common/components/RowDetailPanel.tsx` (`formatMetadataValue`), `workspace/data-view/hooks/useWorkspaceDataTable.ts` (`previewSample`), rendered in `DataEditorToolPanel.tsx`.
- Tests: new `preprocessing/utils/__tests__/formatPreviewValue.test.ts`; `categoricalOptions.test.ts` (blank text case); `DataPreprocessingFeature.test.tsx` (hint text).
- v0.8 notes: Keep one user-facing notion of "empty" = null, NaN, or blank/whitespace text everywhere (display, filter labels, generated names). DuckDB distinguishes NULL and NaN (and NaN only exists for float/double); the "is empty" predicate must cover NULL, `isnan()` for floats, and `trim(x) = ''` for text. Superseded by V07-237 (#317): empty values now sort last in both directions in every table.

#### V07-065 Data types: "text" and "decimal" instead of "string" and "float" (#178)

- Commits: 8de81d9c
- Area: Data Editor, Tables, Trends (docs), Docs

- Problem / reason: ANU testers found "float" unfamiliar; HASS users do not say "string".
- What changed: The type picker (`DATA_TYPES`), column header type labels, column selectors (via `arrowTypeDisplayName`), the cast error toast, and docs say "text" for String (Arrow `Utf8View`) and "decimal" for Float64. The values sent to the backend are unchanged (`string`, `float`). The error toast is `Failed to convert column "<col>" to <label>: <message>`.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - The type menu lists: text, categorical, integer, decimal, datetime (date was added later by #187).
  - A Utf8View column header shows "text"; a Float64 column shows "decimal".
  - Converting to decimal still sends `target_type: "float"`.

Docs: `information/sequential-analysis.md`, `tutorials/sequential-analysis.md`, `tutorials/ui.md`.
- Tests: label updates in `WorkspaceTable.test.tsx`, `arrowTable.test.ts`, `SequentialAnalysisParameterPanel.test.tsx`, `DataPreprocessingFeature.test.tsx`.

#### V07-070 Data type change: report every unconvertible value, point to the first, with Undo (#183)

- Commits: 688f3d3b, a1eb6f4d
- Area: Data Editor (type change)
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: ANU testers found a type change could silently destroy data (text to integer gave only empty values), because casts use `strict=False`. First version (688f3d3b) warned only when at least 10% of the column was emptied. Chao's follow-up (a1eb6f4d): messy data should not block a conversion, and a few typos in 10,000 rows should not go unnoticed, so the warning now appears whenever any value could not be converted and names the first one.
- What changed:
  - The cast edit endpoint (`POST .../nodes/{node_id}/edits` with `kind: "cast"`) returns response headers: `X-Wordflow-Emptied-Values` (count of values that were not empty before and are empty after), `X-Wordflow-Total-Rows`, and when any were emptied `X-Wordflow-First-Emptied-Row` (1-based) and `X-Wordflow-First-Emptied-Value` (original value cast to text, first 200 characters, URL-encoded). "Empty" uses the same test as Filter's "is empty", so a value already blank is not counted. Non-cast edits send none of these headers. All are added to CORS `expose_headers`.
  - The Data Editor shows a `toast.warning` (15 s) with an "Undo" action that calls `undoNode(nodeId)`:
    - Title, one value: `1 value in "<col>" could not be converted to <type label> and is now empty.`
    - Title, several: `<n> of <rows> values in "<col>" (<pct>%) could not be converted to <type label> and are now empty.` (percentage rounded, minimum 1%, numbers with thousands separators).
    - Description: `The first is row <row> (page <page>): "<value>". Undo restores them.` ("it" for one value). The "(page N)" part is included only when the table is unsorted; page = ceil(row / page_size).
  - No toast when nothing was emptied.
  - Docs (`tutorials/ui.md`): "The conversion never stops because of messy data ..." with the `5OO` example.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - CSV `label` column with values a,b,c,d cast to integer: headers Emptied 4, Total 4, First row 1, First value "a".
  - `year` values `1990`, `about 2000`, blank, `2010` cast to integer: Emptied 1 (blank not counted), First row 2, First value `about%202000`.
  - A rename edit has no `X-Wordflow-Emptied-Values` header.
  - 1,000 counts with `5OO` at row 500 and `9oo` at row 900 cast to integer: Emptied 2, First row 500, value "5OO"; the edit succeeds (status 200) for both integer and float.
  - UI: casting "count" with 2 of 10,000 emptied shows exactly `2 of 10,000 values in "count" (1%) could not be converted to integer and are now empty.`; clicking Undo calls undo on the block.
- Implementation: backend `api/workspaces/nodes.py` `edit_node` (headers), `services/nodes.py` `NodeService.edit_with_report` (new; `edit` now delegates), `EmptiedValuesReport` dataclass, `_emptied_values_report(before, after, column)` (horizontal concat of before/after column with a 1-based row index, then `lost = ~empty(before) & empty(after)`), `services/node_operations.py` `empty_value_expression` (public wrapper of `_empty_value_expression`), `shared/table_transport.py` header constants, `main.py` CORS. Frontend `workspace/common/hooks/useWorkspaceTransformMutations.ts` (`headerCount`, `headerText` with `decodeURIComponent`; cast mutation returns `{node, emptied, rows, firstRow, firstValue}`), `workspace/data-view/hooks/useWorkspaceDataTable.ts` `handleCast`. OpenAPI and generated SDK regenerated.
- Tests: `backend/tests/integration/test_cast_emptied_values.py` (`test_cast_reports_emptied_values`, `test_cast_with_a_few_typos_converts_the_rest`); `useWorkspaceDataTable.test.tsx` ("reports values a type change could not convert, with the first one, and offers Undo").
- v0.8 notes: The report relies on the before and after plans having the same row order so a horizontal concat lines rows up (true for a column cast in Polars). In DuckDB compute it in one query over the source, e.g. `count(*) FILTER (WHERE NOT is_empty(col) AND is_empty(TRY_CAST(col AS T)))` plus the first row number via `row_number()`; use `TRY_CAST` for non-strict casts. Undo depends on v0.7's per-block session plan history; v0.8 needs an equivalent undo for the toast action.

#### V07-074 Data types: Date type added (#187)

- Commits: f701afb4
- Area: Data Editor, Filter, Trends, Export, saved Projects
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: Many HASS columns are plain dates (publication or sitting dates), but the type menu offered only datetime, which shows a meaningless 00:00 time. Excel dates already arrive as Date (#165).
- What changed:
  - Type menu adds "date" (value `date`). Arrow `Date32<DAY>` columns are labelled "date".
  - Text (String or Categorical) to date opens the same format panel as datetime, titled `Convert “<col>” to date`; parsed with `str.to_date(format=..., strict=...)` or an inferred format. Datetime to date keeps the date (`dt.date()`). Date to date is a no-op. Any other source type is refused: `Column '<col>' is <type>; only text and datetime columns can be converted to date.`
  - Saved Projects: `CastDerivation.target_type` literal adds `"date"`; saved-plan tests cover every type change, including the three date conversions.
  - Trends: accepts Date time columns; hides "second", "minute", "hourly" frequencies and "seconds", "minutes", "hours" custom units when the time column is a Date. If a sub-day period is still requested, the backend casts the Date to `Datetime("us")` (midnight).
  - Filter: for Date columns the frontend sends the local calendar day (`YYYY-MM-DD`) instead of the date picker's UTC timestamp, so midnight in Sydney no longer compares as the previous day; the backend `_parse_temporal` accepts plain `YYYY-MM-DD` values (parsed to a naive datetime at midnight).
  - Export: CSV, Excel, JSON and Parquet write dates as dates (tested).
  - Text to decimal now uses `strict=False`, so unconvertible values become empty (and are reported by #183) instead of refusing the whole change.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Converting "2020-01-31" text to date gives a Date column labelled "date", showing 2020-01-31 with no time.
  - Converting a datetime column to date drops the time.
  - Converting an integer column to date gives the error above.
  - With a Date time column, Trends frequency options exclude second, minute and hourly.
  - Filtering a Date column by a picked day in Australia/Sydney matches rows of that day.
  - Text "1.5, abc" to decimal succeeds with "abc" empty.
  - A Project with a date cast reopens with the cast applied.
- Implementation: backend `services/node_casting.py` (`_date_cast_expr`, `_cast_expr`, `SUPPORTED_CAST_TARGETS`), `domain/workspace/provenance.py` (`CastDerivation`), `services/node_operations.py` (`_cast_is_no_op`, `_DATE_PATTERN`, `_parse_temporal`), `analysis/sequential_core.py` (`_build_sequential_result_frames` sub-day cast). Frontend `workspace/data-view/services/schemaMutations.ts`, `hooks/useColumnMutations.ts` (text to date opens the format panel), `components/WorkspaceTable.tsx` (`targetLabel`), `views/common/components/DatetimeFormatPanel.tsx` (`targetLabel` prop), `lib/arrow/arrowTable.ts` (`isArrowDateField`, display map), `preprocessing/filter/utils/serializers.ts` (`toLocalCalendarDay`, `toCalendarDays`), `sequential-analysis/SequentialAnalysisFeature.tsx` and `panels/SequentialAnalysisParameterPanel.tsx` (`dateOnly`, `SUB_DAY_FREQUENCIES`, `SUB_DAY_UNITS`). OpenAPI regenerated.
- Tests: `backend/tests/unit/test_data_editor_saved_plans.py::test_type_change_plan_can_be_read_back`, `test_date_columns_in_tools.py` (`test_trends_accepts_a_date_time_column`, `test_trends_custom_interval_accepts_a_date_column`, `test_date_columns_export_as_dates`), `test_filter_text_values.py::test_filter_compares_date_columns`; frontend `filter/utils/__tests__/serializers.test.ts` ("sends the local calendar day for Date columns", "keeps full timestamps for datetime columns").
- v0.8 notes: Date must be a first-class type (DuckDB DATE). Saved-plan round-trip was a polars-source-utils concern; in v0.8 make sure stored plans or SQL can express date casts (`strptime(...)::DATE`, `TRY_STRPTIME`). The Filter timezone fix is frontend logic (local calendar day for Date columns) and must be kept. Trends with DuckDB: `date_trunc` on DATE for sub-day units needs a cast to TIMESTAMP.

#### V07-080 Column names are exact: whitespace around a name is kept (#108)

- Commits: 9d70ce34
- Area: Analyses (all request models), Data Editor, Frequency (document column), metadata selectors
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: A CSV header "ID, text" names the second column " text" (leading space). The frontend trimmed it before saving the document column (the backend's exact check then rejected it with 400 and a generic toast "Could not save the document column for this data block."), and analysis request models stripped whitespace via `NonEmptyText`, so workers selected a non-existent column (`ColumnNotFoundError: unable to find column "text"`).
- What changed:
  - Backend: new `ColumnName = Annotated[str, StringConstraints(min_length=1)]` (no strip) for every field naming an existing column: `node_columns` (Token Frequency, Topic Modelling, Concordance), Quotation `column`, Trends `time_column` and `group_by_columns`, annotation `text_column`, `annotation_column`, `correction_column`, `class_column`, `description_column`, `example_text_column`, `example_annotation_column`, Data Block creation `selected_columns` and `selected_metadata_columns`, Topic Modelling creation `selected_columns`. `NonEmptyColumnName` in `models/node_resources.py` (used for set_cell and annotation class columns, among others) no longer strips. Free-text fields (names, instructions, search words) still strip.
  - Frontend: `usePersistNodeDocumentColumn` sends the exact column (empty string becomes null); `normalizeMetadataColumns` de-duplicates without trimming.
  - Headers are not normalised on load, so exports keep the source header.
  - Not done (optional polish from the issue): showing leading/trailing spaces visibly in column pickers.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Loading `ID, text` CSV and choosing " text" as the document column saves without error.
  - Running Frequency on it succeeds.
  - Request models keep " text" and " date " exactly.
  - `normalizeMetadataColumns([' text', 'text'])` keeps both.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/domain/workspace/analysis.py`, `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/models/node_resources.py`, `frontend/src/features/views/common/hooks/usePersistNodeDocumentColumn.ts`, `views/common/components/metadataColumnSelection.ts`.
- Tests: `backend/tests/integration/test_exact_column_names.py` (`test_analysis_requests_keep_whitespace_in_column_names`, `test_document_column_with_a_leading_space_can_be_saved`); `metadataColumnSelection.test.ts`.
- v0.8 notes: In DuckDB, always quote identifiers (`"  text"`), never trim schema names, and do not let CSV readers normalise headers (DuckDB `read_csv` keeps whitespace by default but check `normalize_names` is off). Consider visible whitespace markers in pickers.

#### V07-102 Data Editor layout: title in the tab strip, double-click rename, tab list, type symbols, Switch Project (#206)

- Commits: 3fda1f28
- Area: Data Editor (right column), shared `EditorTabs` (also analysis tabs), Project Graph title bar
- Type: behaviour change / feature
- Problem / reason: requested by Chao: the Data Editor header had a separate title, the Data Block name and a "Rename" button, taking space; type buttons with full type names made columns wide; "integer" is jargon.
- What changed:
  - The "Data Editor" title and its help icon (target `ui.data-viewer`) sit at the left end of the Data Block tab strip. The header's Data Block name label (with RTL clipping fade) and its "Rename" button (aria "Rename node") are removed.
  - The tab strip now shows from one selected Data Block up (before: only with two or more), because it carries the title and renaming. Accessible name of the strip: "Data Block tabs" (old "Selected node tabs").
  - Renaming (shared `EditorTabs`, so both Data Editor and analysis tabs): a single click only activates a tab; renaming starts on double-click of the active tab. Before, a single click on the already active tab started renaming. Keys typed in the rename input no longer reach the tab strip's key handler (`event.target !== event.currentTarget` guard), so Space, arrows, Home and End edit the text.
  - Tab list: a chevron button at the right end of the strip (aria "All Data Block tabs", title "All tabs") opens a dropdown listing all tabs alphabetically (`localeCompare`, base sensitivity, numeric), the active one with a tick; choosing one activates it.
  - Column type button: shows only a symbol plus the chevron. Symbols (`ColumnTypeSymbol`): text "Aa", categorical = tag icon, whole number "123", decimal "1.2", date = calendar icon, date and time = clock icon, true/false = square-check icon, list = list icon, topic coverage = pie chart icon, unknown = circle-help icon. Tooltip (300 ms delay) shows the capitalised type name; the button keeps `aria-label="Change data type for column {column}"` and gets `aria-description` with the type name. The menu lists types with capitalised names. Topic coverage columns keep the disabled-reason tooltip.
  - Type vocabulary: cast option labels "integer" to "whole number", "datetime" to "date and time" (`schemaMutations.ts`); `arrowTypeDisplayName` map `Int64` to "whole number", `Timestamp<MICROSECOND, UTC>` to "date and time", new `Bool` to "true / false". The datetime modal's fallback label "datetime" to "date and time". (Item 4 later renames "categorical" to "category" and maps all remaining types.)
  - Project Graph: "Change Project" to "Switch Project" (busy "Switching…"); help registry label "Switch Project".
  - Docs: ui.md Data Editor section (tabs, double-click rename, tab list, a table of type symbols), Trends tutorial uses "date and time" and "whole number"; screenshots retaken, new `data_editor_tab_list.png`.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - With one Data Block selected, the strip shows "Data Editor", a help icon, one tab and the tab-list arrow.
  - Single click on the active tab does nothing; double-click opens the rename box; typing a space inserts a space.
  - The tab list is alphabetical and ticks the current tab.
  - A text column's type button shows "Aa"; hovering shows "Text"; an Int64 column shows "123" and "Whole number".
  - The Project Graph button reads "Switch Project".
- Implementation: `frontend/src/components/tabs/EditorTabs.tsx` (click/double-click handlers, key guard), `features/workspace/data-view/components/WorkspaceSelectionTabs.tsx`, `ColumnTypeSymbol.tsx` (new), `WorkspaceColumnHeader.tsx` (`field` prop, `typeName`), `WorkspaceDataHeader.tsx` (rename removed), `hooks/useWorkspaceDataTable.ts` (`onTabRename`, `shouldShowTabs = selectedNodeIds.length > 0`), `services/schemaMutations.ts`, `lib/arrow/arrowTable.ts` (`isArrowTimestampField`, `isArrowListField`), `components/layout/ChangeProjectMenu.tsx`.
- Tests: `EditorTabs.test.tsx`, `WorkspaceSelectionTabs.test.tsx` (tab list, rename), `WorkspaceDataHeader.test.tsx` (rename removed), `WorkspaceTable.test.tsx`, `ChangeProjectMenu.test.tsx`, `WorkspaceControls.test.tsx`, `arrowTable.test.ts`, label updates in Trends and Data Builder tests.
- v0.8 notes: symbols come from Arrow field types in v0.7; in v0.8 map DuckDB types (VARCHAR, ENUM, BIGINT, DOUBLE, DATE, TIMESTAMP/TIMESTAMPTZ, BOOLEAN, LIST, STRUCT) to the same user vocabulary and symbols. Keep "activate, then double-click to rename" for all tabs. The rename behaviour itself was later unified by #210.

#### V07-104 Data Editor follow-ups: scrolling tab strip, tools left and Undo/Redo right, wider tab list, steady scroll, double-click column rename (#208)

- Commits: d8e7790d
- Area: Data Editor, shared `EditorTabs`
- Type: bug fix + feature
- Problem / reason: follow-ups from testing #206: the tab strip lost its edge buttons and no longer scrolled; the tab list button was pushed off-screen; the tab list was too narrow; changing a column's type scrolled the table to the top-left; no double-click column rename.
- What changed:
  - `EditorTabs` applies its `className` to the outer box instead of the inner scroller, so a flex parent (`min-w-0 flex-1`) can shrink it; the strip scrolls with its edge buttons again, and the tab list button stays visible at the right end.
  - Toolbar: editing tools (Add column, Find & replace, Clean text, help, Delete columns, and "(empty table)" note) on the left; Undo/Redo pushed to the right end (`ml-auto`); on a narrow panel Undo/Redo wrap to a second line.
  - Tab list width: `w-max min-w-64 max-w-[min(36rem,calc(100vw-2rem))]`, so it sizes to the names up to about 36 characters' width; long names use `MiddleFadeLabel` (keep start and end, fade the middle), full name on hover.
  - Scroll position: `WorkspaceTable` stops using TanStack `flexRender` and calls renderers directly (local `renderColumnPart`), so headers and cells stay mounted when the table re-renders. Before, a type change remounted every cell (1,680 cells and 84 headers in one test case) and WebKit reset the scroll to (0, 0).
  - Double-click a column name to rename it (when renaming is allowed and the column is not busy). Title tooltip "{column} (double-click to rename)"; the rename input has accessible name "Rename column {column}"; Enter submits, Esc cancels.
  - Docs: ui.md Data Editor section and two screenshots updated.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - With 7 tabs in a narrow panel, the strip scrolls with arrow buttons and the tab-list arrow stays visible.
  - Undo/Redo are at the right end of the toolbar.
  - After scrolling the table to (300, 150) and changing a column type, the position is still (300, 150) (checked in WebKit).
  - Double-clicking "speaker", typing "member" and Enter calls rename with ("speaker", "member").
- Implementation: `frontend/src/components/tabs/EditorTabs.tsx`, `WorkspaceDataHeader.tsx`, `WorkspaceSelectionTabs.tsx`, `WorkspaceColumnHeader.tsx`, `WorkspaceTable.tsx`. The local `renderColumnPart` was moved to `src/lib/table/renderColumnPart.ts` in #209.
- Tests: `WorkspaceTable.test.tsx`: "renames a column when its name is double-clicked (issue 208)", "keeps cells mounted when the table re-renders, so the scroll position holds (issue 208)" (asserts the same DOM node before and after rerender); `WorkspaceSelectionTabs.test.tsx` updated.
- v0.8 notes: see the #209 entry: column renderers must be plain functions called directly, not mounted as components. WebKit/Safari-specific: remounting a table's cells resets its scroll position.

#### V07-113 Type-change errors in plain words (#205 item 5)

- Commits: d5ec6213
- Area: Data Editor type change (backend `node_casting.py`)
- Type: wording
- Problem / reason: messages were written for programmers ("…Note your notebook example used .head() (sampling)… keep strict=False (default) to set them null.") and pasted raw Polars text.
- What changed (all casting failures end with "Nothing was changed." and carry the Polars text in `details.diagnostic`, shown under Details):
  - Date parse failure: 'Some values in "{column}" don't match the date format {format}. Check the format, or clean those values first. Nothing was changed.' (without a format: "…don't match a date format Wordflow recognises…"). Same wording for sample-validation failures on date/datetime targets.
  - Other sample-validation failure: 'Some values in "{column}" can't be read as {target label}. Clean them first, or choose another type. Nothing was changed.'
  - General cast failure: '"{column}" can't be converted to {target label}. Clean its values first, or choose another type. Nothing was changed.'
  - Wrong source for date: 'Only text and date and time columns can become dates, and "{column}" is neither.' (old "Column 'x' is Int64; only text and datetime columns can be converted to date.")
  - Unsupported target: "Wordflow can't convert columns to {target} yet. Choose text, category, whole number, decimal, date, or date and time."
  - Target labels: string to text, categorical to category, integer to whole number, float to decimal, datetime to date and time, date to date.
- Expected behaviour (testable): converting a column with "31/31/2020" using `%d/%m/%Y` fails with the date sentence and a Details link; the column is unchanged.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/services/node_casting.py` (`_TARGET_LABELS`, `_cast_failure`).
- Tests: `backend/tests/unit/test_node_casting.py`.
- v0.8 notes: the agreed decision asked to "remind the user that the change can be undone in the Data Editor"; the implementation says "Nothing was changed." instead (the failed cast changes nothing, so there is nothing to undo). Confirm with Chao which wording v0.8 should use. DuckDB `TRY_CAST`/`strptime` errors differ from Polars; keep the sentences, map DuckDB's errors to them.

#### V07-114 Dates: plain date format panel, year-first display, simple entry (#205 item 6)

- Commits: a49163ab
- Area: date/datetime conversion panel (Data Editor and Builder), all table cells and Row Details, date entry fields (Filter etc.)
- Type: wording + display behaviour change
- Problem / reason: the panel said "Provide a strftime format or let Auto Fill guess it", "Use Python strftime codes.", "Could not infer format", "Inferred."; tables showed "2020-01-30T00:00:00.000Z"; the placeholder was "YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS+00:00".
- What changed:
  - `DatetimeFormatPanel`: description "How are the dates written?"; body "How are the dates written? Wordflow can work it out from the data, or you can enter the format." plus "In this column: {up to 3 distinct sample values}". Field label "Date format" (old "Custom format"), placeholder "for example %d/%m/%Y" (old "e.g., %Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S"), monospace. Button "Detect from the data" (old "Auto Fill"). Status (aria-live): failure "Couldn't work out the date format. Enter it above, or choose an example below."; success "Worked out from the data.". Examples list with text "Examples (choose one to use it). %d is the day, %m the month number, %b the short month name, %Y the year, %H the hour and %M the minute."; clicking an example fills the format: "30/01/2020" %d/%m/%Y; "2020-01-30" %Y-%m-%d; "30 Jan 2020" %d %b %Y; "January 30, 2020" %B %d, %Y; "30/01/2020 14:05" %d/%m/%Y %H:%M; "2020-01-30 14:05:00" %Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S (aria "Use {format} for dates like {example}"). Default target label "date and time".
  - Display: `displayDateTime(text)` turns the decoder's exact ISO output (`YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS.sssZ`) into "YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM", adding seconds only when not :00 and milliseconds only when not .000; no time zone shown (Wordflow stores UTC). Other text unchanged. Applied in Data Editor cells (`formatCellValue`), Row Details, Concordance table, Builder preview table, Annotation results. Data values keep the ISO text that charts and filters parse.
  - Entry: placeholder "YYYY-MM-DD or YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM"; `normalizeIsoDraft` accepts "2020-01-30 14:05" (space converted to T).
  - Help ui.md updated.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - A timestamp 2020-01-30T00:00:00.000Z shows as "2020-01-30 00:00"; 2020-01-30T14:05:07.000Z shows "2020-01-30 14:05:07".
  - Typing "2020-01-30 14:05" in a date filter is accepted.
  - With samples that cannot be inferred, the panel shows the "Couldn't work out the date format…" line; clicking "30/01/2020" fills %d/%m/%Y.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/lib/displayDateTime.ts` (new), `common/components/DatetimeFormatPanel.tsx` (`DATE_FORMAT_EXAMPLES`, `handleDetect`), `preprocessing/utils/dateTimeHelpers.ts` (`ISO_PLACEHOLDER`, `normalizeIsoDraft`), `formatCellValue.ts`, `RowDetailPanel.tsx`, `concordanceTableModel.ts`, `PreviewTable.tsx`, `AnnotationResultsPanel.tsx`.
- Tests: `lib/__tests__/displayDateTime.test.ts`, `preprocessing/utils/__tests__/dateTimeHelpers.test.ts`.
- v0.8 notes: formats are Python/Polars strftime codes; DuckDB `strptime` uses almost the same `%` codes, but check `%b`/`%B` and fractional seconds. The display rewrite depends on the Arrow JS decoder producing `toISOString()` text; v0.8 should format dates at display time only, never in stored data.

#### V07-137 Data Editor tools stay open after Apply; Close button (#217)

- Commits: ddc50f2a
- Area: Data Editor
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: Every Data Editor tool closed on Apply, so chaining edits (for example Clean text "Remove digits" and then "Remove punctuation" on the same column) meant reopening the tool from the column menu each time, and closing it needed a trip to the × in the panel's top corner. Chao asked for the tool to stay open on the same column and for a Close button beside Cancel and Apply. Chao also once saw the second Clean text edit start from the original column, but could not reproduce it in the Tauri app; the backend chains both edits correctly through the API (checked), so nothing was changed for that.
- What changed:
  - `dataEditorToolStore` gains `formKey`, `resetForm(column)` and `setColumns(columns)`. `WorkspaceView` keys the panel by tool and `formKey`, so `resetForm` remounts a fresh form on the same column; `open` also bumps the key.
  - Apply success: toast, then `resetForm(column)` instead of `close()`. Settings other than the column return to their starting values (Clean text returns to the operation chosen from the column menu, or Trim), so the applied edit is not previewed a second time.
  - `useWorkspaceDataTable` keeps the open tool's columns equal to the active Data Block's committed columns (never preview or placeholder data), using the same filter as opening (`toolColumns`: every column for Copy column, text-readable columns otherwise).
  - Footer: Cancel (discards unapplied settings, keeps the tool open; disabled until something changes), Close (closes the tool), Apply. The header × stays.
  - Help: `tutorials/ui.md` Data Editor paragraph.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - After Apply in any Data Editor tool, the tool is still open on the same Data Block and column, with other settings reset, and the Data Block is not marked as having unfinished settings.
  - A second Apply on the same column builds on the first (Remove digits, then Remove punctuation, leaves neither).
  - A column added by the first Apply can be chosen in the tool, and its name is treated as taken.
  - Cancel is disabled on an unchanged form; after a change it restores the starting settings and keeps the tool open.
  - Close closes the tool.
- Implementation: `features/workspace/data-view/dataEditorToolStore.ts`, `components/DataEditorToolPanel.tsx`, `hooks/useWorkspaceDataTable.ts`, `components/layout/WorkspaceView.tsx`.
- Tests: `DataEditorToolPanel.test.tsx` ("stays open after Apply on the same column; Cancel starts over and Close closes", updated duplicate test); `useWorkspaceDataTable.test.tsx` ("keeps an open tool's columns in step with the Data Block after Apply"); manual Playwright WebKit run of the two-step Clean text flow.
- v0.8 notes: keep the tool open after Apply and refresh its column list from committed data, not the preview. Reset the form after Apply so a non-idempotent edit (Find & replace that adds text) is not previewed twice.

#### V07-148 Data Editor Clean text keeps the applied operation after Apply (#230)

- Commits: 848d449f
- Area: Data Editor
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Since #217 the tool restarts its form after Apply from its starting settings. For Clean text the starting operation is the one chosen from the column menu, so after applying lowercase from a tool opened as Title Case, the Cleaning list jumped back to Title Case.
- What changed: `resetForm(column, operation?)` sets the store's `initialOperation`; the panel passes the applied operation for Clean text only. Cancel returns to that operation too. Other settings and other tools still reset (#217).
- Expected behaviour (testable): open Clean text as Title Case, choose lowercase, Apply: the list still reads lowercase and the tool is not marked unfinished.
- Implementation: `features/workspace/data-view/dataEditorToolStore.ts`, `components/DataEditorToolPanel.tsx`.
- Tests: `DataEditorToolPanel.test.tsx` ("keeps the Clean text operation just applied rather than the starting one"); manual WebKit run.
- v0.8 notes: after Apply, keep a choice whose re-preview is harmless (idempotent operations); reset fields that would re-apply a non-idempotent edit.

#### V07-192 Combine columns: a fixed value with no column (#273)

- Commits: 14081469
- Area: Data Editor
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: Chao asked for a quick way to add a column holding one value for every row (for example a corpus label before stacking Data Blocks). A template with no `{column}` was refused: Apply was disabled and the backend returned 422.
- What changed:

  - `buildCombine` accepts any non-empty template; the hint says "No column: every row gets this text."; **When a value is missing** is disabled until the template uses a column.

  - The Data Block reloads from its saved plan with the column intact.

- Tests: `test_data_editor_edits.py::test_combine_columns_template_handles_types_and_missing_values`; `test_data_editor_saved_plans.py` (text-only combine round-trips); `dataEditorRequests.test.ts`.
- v0.8 notes: a literal-only template is a constant column; the missing-value choice does not apply to it.

#### V07-205 Edits and Data Builder creations are checked by reading a row, and the Data Editor waits for a good preview (#285)

- Commits: 3bca90fa, 02f4e293
- Area: Data Editor
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Audit of 2026-10-04, reproduced live: Find & replace with regex `(`: preview 400, Apply 200, the Data Block then failed on every read with the raw Polars regex text until Undo; the panel showed "Previewing…" with Apply enabled. Segment with an invalid pattern created an unreadable block the same way.
- What changed:
  - `validate_plan_executes` (`services/node_operations.py`) resolves the schema and reads one row; a regex failure becomes "The pattern is not a valid regular expression. Check it, or turn off Use regular expression to match it as text." with the diagnostic under Details. Used by edit validation (`nodes.py`) and `build_derived_node`.
  - Data Editor store carries `previewError`; the panel shows it in the status line and Apply is disabled until a preview succeeds (also after Apply resets the form).
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Applying a regex edit with `(` is refused in plain words and nothing changes; Segment with `(` creates no Data Block; the panel's Apply stays disabled while the preview fails or is pending.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/services/node_operations.py`, `services/nodes.py`; `frontend/src/features/workspace/data-view/dataEditorToolStore.ts`, `hooks/useWorkspaceDataTable.ts`, `components/DataEditorToolPanel.tsx`.
- Tests: `test_plan_execution_validation.py`; `DataEditorToolPanel.test.tsx`.
- v0.8 notes: validate plans by executing a row, not only by schema; gate Apply on the preview.

#### V07-211 Replace and Extract output names used as typed; never overwrite another column (#291)

- Commits: 3bca90fa
- Area: Data Editor
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Audit of 2026-10-04: the backend collapsed whitespace and cut the name to 120 characters while the panel only trimmed, so highlight and scroll could miss; a normalised name equal to an existing column replaced it in place silently.
- What changed:
  - `_replace_expression` trims only and refuses a name that belongs to another column ("New column name already exists on the Data Block").

#### V07-238 Ordered category columns: order window, order everywhere, Trends category axis (#318)

- Commits: 885a5ca0, 615bd4fb, 50158911 (row-count orders, dragging), 0be41e03 (warnings and sampling), 7c421820 (list at most 300), e60ecbe6 (CI: unused export, shadow)
- Area: Data Editor
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-06, for 0.7.11 ahead of the November seminar (v0.8 may not be stable by then): a category column needs a user-defined order (Likert scales, ordinal codes) used by every table, list, legend and chart, and Trends should accept a category X axis shown as bars. Decisions: default A to Z (text, natural so Q9 before Q10) or value ascending (numbers, dates), with descending; Most rows first and Fewest rows first; values can always be dragged (no Custom button); warn above 150 values, read the first 10,000 rows first for a long or document column so a mistaken click can be cancelled, list at most 300 values, ceiling 10,000 (Chao, 2026-10-07, replacing the first design: refuse above 50 values, Custom only up to 12); empty is never a category and is always last; blank text becomes empty; choosing Category again reopens the window; legends, Filter and Group use the order; Trends' missing group moves last; a Trends category axis shows (empty) as the last bar while number and date axes keep dropping empty values; editing tools warn that the column becomes text and Undo restores it; Stack merges orders; a Parquet order Wordflow cannot read is reported.
- What changed:
  - Backend `services/category_order.py`: labels (`category_label_expression`: blank text and NaN become null; Datetime as `%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S`, Date as `%Y-%m-%d`, others cast to String; lists, structs, times of day and durations refused), `category_values` (default order: an Enum's own categories, text by `natural_key`, numbers/dates/booleans by value; counts; empty count; `read_all` / `sample_only`; ceiling `MAX_CATEGORY_VALUES` 10,000, warning at `WARN_CATEGORY_VALUES` 150, sample `PROBE_ROWS` 10,000), `ordered_category_expression` (checks every non-empty value is in the order, then `cast(pl.Enum(order))`), `merge_orders` / `stacked_category_order` (keep agreeing orders, else natural A to Z; values of unordered categories follow), `unreadable_order_notes` (pandas `metadata.ordered` in the Parquet footer).
  - `CastDerivation.categories: list[str] | None` (max 10,000). `_cast_is_no_op` treats a matching Enum order as a no-op. New `GET /api/workspaces/{id}/nodes/{node_id}/category-values?column=` with `read_all`, returning `CategoryValuesResource` (kind, labels, counts, empty_count, is_ordered, warn_values 150, max_values 10,000, complete, sample_rows, sample_distinct, is_document).
  - Stack (`_aligned_concat_frames` + `_with_stacked_category_orders`): two category dtypes never count as a mismatch; differing ones are cast via String to `pl.Enum(merged order)`. Category against text is still refused.
  - Trends (`sequential_core`): `column_type` "category"; `time_period` is the category with empty as null, period index from `unique().sort(nulls_last=True)` joined with `nulls_equal=True`, label `(empty)`; group index sorts `nulls_last=True` for every column type (missing group last). `_require_trends_axis` accepts category for "category".
  - Topic colour legend (`group_topic_counts(category_order=...)`): an Enum's order, or natural A to Z for an unordered Categorical; text columns keep largest first.
  - Converting a category column to date and time casts to String before parsing (it called `.str` on a Categorical).
  - Frontend: `CategoryOrderPanel` (dialog; presets Current order / A to Z / Z to A (Smallest first / Largest first for numbers and dates) / Most rows first / Fewest rows first (ties keep A to Z or value order; follow-up 50158911, Chao 2026-10-07); no Custom button: the chips (dnd-kit `useSortable`, PointerSensor + KeyboardSensor) can always be dragged, and a dragged order selects no preset. Limits (Chao, 2026-10-07, replacing 50 values / Custom up to 12): the first request (`read_all=false`) reads only the first 10,000 rows when they hold more than 150 values or the column is the Data Block's document column, and returns `complete: false` with `sample_rows`, `sample_distinct`, `is_document`; the window warns and reads everything on Continue (`read_all=true`, request aborted by Cancel). A complete list above 150 values asks before showing it. The window lists at most 300 values (`SHOWN_CATEGORY_VALUES`, for responsiveness): a note says "Showing the first 300 of N values", presets order every value, drags reorder only the listed ones, and Convert sends the full order. Ceiling 10,000 values (`MAX_CATEGORY_VALUES`; `CastDerivation.categories` max 10,000); fixed last chip "empty (always last)"; Convert / Apply order). `categoryOrder.ts` (`naturalCompare`, `orderedLabels`, `moveLabel`, `stackedCategoryOrder`). The type menu uses `onSelect` per item so choosing Category on a category column reopens the window; `useColumnMutations` keeps `categoryColumn` state and passes `categories` through `onCast` to the cast request.
  - Filter value list SQL `ORDER BY value ASC NULLS LAST` and `(empty)` appended last; Group `groupCountSql(..., isCategory)` lists a category in its order. Trends: `isTrendsAxisField` accepts Dictionary, `trendsAxisRank` (dates, numbers, categories) via a new `NodeInputConstraints.columnRank`, chart model places category periods by period index, forces Bars and even spacing, chart menu offers Bars and Stacked bars only, Spacing hidden. Data Editor: store keeps `columnTypes`; Find & replace and Clean text writing to the same non-text column show a note and an Undo toast. Stack: `columnTypesMatch` matches any two dictionaries with the same value type; `useStackCategoryNotes` reads each block's order and shows a note and toast description.
  - Help: ui.md (Category window, editing note), sequential-analysis.md (category axis, group order), preprocessing.md (Filter list, Group, Stack), data-loader.md (Parquet order).
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Likert column answer converted with the dragged order Strongly disagree, Disagree, Neutral, Agree, Strongly agree (also possible with the keyboard): Data View sort ascending gives that order then the two empty cells; descending reverses it, empties last (Playwright WebKit).
  - Choosing Category again shows "Order of answer" in Current order; closing and reopening the Project keeps the order.
  - Trends with X = answer and Group = group: bars in the custom order, (empty) last; legend A, B, then the missing group; Chart offers Bars and Stacked bars only; Spacing hidden. A Data Block with a number and a category column defaults to the number.
  - Find & replace on answer saving to the same column shows: "answer" is a category column. Saving the result to the same column makes it a text column. Undo restores the category column and its values.
  - Stack of the custom-ordered block and an A to Z copy: note and toast say the orders differ so the stacked column lists its values A to Z; the result's window shows A to Z.
  - Group on answer lists the values in the custom order with (empty) last.
  - A column with more than 150 different values asks before listing them; the document column, or a column whose first 10,000 rows hold more than 150 values, reads only those rows first and offers Continue or Cancel. With more than 300 values the window says "Showing the first 300 of N values" and the order buttons still order every value. More than 10,000 values is refused.
  - Most rows first and Fewest rows first order by the counts shown beside each value; ties keep A to Z or value order.
- Implementation: Backend: `services/category_order.py`, `services/node_casting.py`, `services/node_operations.py`, `services/nodes.py`, `api/workspaces/nodes.py`, `models/node_resources.py`, `domain/workspace/provenance.py`, `domain/workspace/analysis.py`, `analysis/sequential_core.py`, `analysis/topic_metadata_colors.py`, `services/analysis_results.py`, `services/analysis_preparation_registry.py`. Frontend: `CategoryOrderPanel.tsx`, `categoryOrder.ts`, `WorkspaceTable.tsx`, `WorkspaceColumnHeader.tsx`, `useColumnMutations.ts`, `useWorkspaceDataTable.ts`, `useWorkspaceTransformMutations.ts`, `DataEditorToolPanel.tsx`, `dataEditorToolStore.ts`, `nodeInputsCore.ts`, Trends files, Filter and Group SQL, Stack hook and panel. Dependency: @dnd-kit/core, @dnd-kit/sortable, @dnd-kit/utilities.
- Tests: `test_category_order.py` (labels, default orders, warning, sample and ceiling, cast with order, refusal of a missing value, category to date and time, merges, Stack, Trends category axis, Parquet note); `test_topic_metadata_colors.py` (category order); `test_sequential_worker.py` (missing group last); `categoryOrder.test.ts`; `CategoryOrderPanel.test.tsx`; `sequentialChartModel.test.ts` (category axis); `builderModels.test.ts` (Group SQL); updated Filter, Stack and cast tests; Playwright WebKit run of the whole flow on an isolated instance.
- v0.8 notes: Use DuckDB ENUM for an ordered category (ORDER BY follows the declared order). Keep the conversion window, the warnings (sample of the first 10,000 rows; ask above 150 values or for the document column; list at most 300; ceiling 10,000), dragging at any time, the empty-never-a-category rule, natural A to Z default, Stack merge rule and the Trends category axis. See rule R9. Converting must read the whole column once: an ENUM lists every value up front, a value left out becomes empty, and Parquet statistics hold no distinct values, so never sample. The same pass gives the row counts. In v0.7 this is a local Parquet scan of one column (10 million rows: 0.19 s to list, 0.1 s to check). Over remote views it is a full read of that column over the network (only its compressed chunks); show progress and allow cancelling, or convert after the Data Block is materialised locally.

#### V07-241 Conversion window: date formats that fit, parts menus, two-digit centuries, Unix and Excel numbers, text to number, date to text, whole-column check (#322)

- Commits: c51f7b50 (backend), ac99f31d, fcdee084 (dates inside text)
- Area: Data Editor
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-07, for 0.7.11: date conversion failed on many real formats, numeric timestamps could not be converted, format codes were hard to type, and nothing showed the result before converting. Decisions: day first by default with a one-click swap when both readings fit (OS locale is a later version); Excel day numbers supported but never preselected; two-digit years always ask for the century (historians' data spans centuries, so never assume 1969/1970 pivots); include text to number and date to text; no text to true/false (no boolean type in Wordflow); decimals to whole numbers round to the nearest (halves away from zero), not truncate; time zones stay UTC.
- What changed:
  - Backend `services/conversion.py`: `ConversionOptions` (datetime_format, epoch_unit s/ms/us/ns, excel_serial, two_digit_year_start, decimal_mark, thousands_separator, ignore_symbols); `candidate_formats()` (date part x time part x zone, plus Twitter `%a %b %d %H:%M:%S %z %Y` and RFC 2822); `detect_datetime_formats` (first 1,000 non-empty values, quick pass on 20; `%Y` rejected when the parsed years are below 100; candidates with identical results deduplicated; `swap_format` when day/month and month/day both read every value; day first wins ties; numbers offer the coarsest Unix unit whose values are not bunched at 1969 to 1970, and Excel when every value is 1 to 73,050); `datetime_from_text` (strict, then `_shift_two_digit_years` from `two_digit_year_start`); `datetime_from_numbers` (Unix in microseconds; Excel from 1899-12-30); `number_from_text` (symbols stripped except digits, signs, marks and exponent; thousands separator only in strict groups of three, else null; comma decimal mark); `round_to_whole` (half away from zero); `format_tokens` (digits, letters, zones `[+-]HH:?MM`, single characters).
  - `node_casting.py`: date and datetime casts take the options; text and decimals to whole number round; text to decimal via `number_from_text`; new `check_cast` (one pass: total rows, non-empty, converted, failed, 5 sample rows with value and result, first 5 failures; dates shown as `%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S`). `CastDerivation` gains epoch_unit, excel_serial, two_digit_year_start, decimal_mark, thousands_separator, ignore_symbols, so saved plans replay the same conversion.
  - API: `GET /api/workspaces/{id}/nodes/{node_id}/datetime-formats?column=` returning `DatetimeFormatsResource` (candidates with kind format/unix/excel, format, epoch_unit, parsed, sample_size, examples, swap_format, two_digit_year; sample_value; sample_parts); `POST .../conversion-check` (body: column, target_type and the options) returning `ConversionCheckResource`.
  - Frontend `ConversionPanel` (replaces `DatetimeFormatPanel` and `datetimeFormatInfer`), modes date / number / date-text, title "Convert "column" to type". Date: "Formats that fit" radios ("reads N of M values", one example; two-digit candidates say "choose the century below"); the best non-Excel candidate is preselected; note "These dates could be day first or month first … Wordflow reads them day first." with button "Read month first"; "Unix time in" seconds/milliseconds/microseconds/nanoseconds; parts builder "What each part of "value" is" with one menu per part (Day, Month, Year, Year (two digits), Hour, Hour (12-hour clock), Minute, Second, Month name, Month name short, Weekday, Weekday short, AM/PM, Time zone, Fraction of a second); century fieldset "The years have two digits. Which century are they in?" with 1900s / 2000s / Split at N (start 1900+N); "Format code (for advanced use)". Number: "Decimal mark" Point (1,234.5) / Comma (1.234,5); "Thousands separator" Comma / Point / Space / Apostrophe / None; "Ignore currency signs, % and other symbols"; note on rounding. Date to text: "Written like" presets (2020-01-30, 30/01/2020, 30 Jan 2020, 30 January 2020, Thursday 30 January 2020, January 2020, 2020, plus time presets when the source has a time), "+ part" buttons and a format field. Check section (300 ms after the last change): "N of M values convert; K would become empty. E empty values stay empty.", a Row / Value / Becomes table (dates readable, "doesn't convert" in red), first failures. **Convert** is enabled only when the choices are complete and the check matches them.
  - `dateFormatBuilder.ts` (`segmentsFromFormat`, `segmentsFromValue`, `formatFromSegments`, `describeFormat`, `rolesFor`, presets). `useColumnMutations` opens the window for text/category to date or number and date to text (`conversionMode`), and passes the extras through `onCast` to the cast request; decimal to whole number converts directly.
  - Help: ui.md conversion section rewritten.
  - Follow-up (Chao, 2026-10-07, file names such as "2021_01_17_LaurenLancaster"): the parts menus kept no choice until every part was named, and text after the date could not be skipped. Now `DateSection` keeps the named parts in local state (re-seeded when the format changes from a candidate, the swap or the code field); every part menu ends with "Ignore (not part of the date)" (`IGNORE_PART`); `buildFormat` returns the format of the kept range, `inText`, and `ignoredInside` (an ignored part between date parts: alert "Only text before or after the date can be ignored, not a part in the middle of it.", Convert disabled). With ignored parts the note reads "Wordflow finds the date inside each value and ignores the text around it."; checkbox "Find the date inside longer text (ignore the rest)" under the format code. Backend: `ConversionOptions.date_in_text` / `CastDerivation.date_in_text` parse with Polars `exact=False` (the date may sit anywhere in the value; text before and after is ignored; survives plan serialisation); a Date target with it goes through the date-and-time parser. Detection adds `_` separators (`%Y_%m_%d`, `%d_%m_%Y`, `%m_%d_%Y`, swap pair `%d_%m`) and, when no format reads whole values, tries date and date-time formats inside the text (`in_text` on the candidate, labelled "…, inside longer text"); inside text a two-digit-year candidate is dropped when a four-digit-year one reads as many values ("21-01-17" inside "2021-01-17"). Builder alignment reads `%Y` as four digits and other numeric parts as one or two, so "20210117" splits into 2021, 01, 17.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - "03/01/2020", "15/02/2021": Day/Month/Year preselected; Read month first not shown (15 cannot be a month). "03/01/2020", "04/02/2021": the note and Read month first appear; after the swap, 03/01/2020 becomes 1 Mar 2020 (Playwright WebKit).
  - "30/01/20": Convert disabled until a century is chosen; 1900s gives 30 Jan 1920; Split at 50 gives 2020 for 20 and 1969 for 69.
  - 1601596799 (seconds) is read as Unix time (seconds) and becomes 1 Oct 2020 23:59:59; 43831 is offered as an Excel day number (1 Jan 2020) but not selected.
  - "Thu Oct 01 23:59:59 +0000 2020" reads as a Twitter date.
  - Text to decimal with Comma decimal mark and Point thousands: "$1.234,5" becomes 1234.5; "1,23" with Comma thousands does not convert.
  - Decimal to whole number: 2.5 becomes 3, -2.5 becomes -3; no window.
  - base_name "2021_01_17_AlexJones", "2021_02_03_SamLee": Year_Month_Day, inside longer text is preselected; 3 of 3 convert; cells 2021-01-17 (Playwright WebKit).
  - "17~01~2021~notes" (no candidate): Day, Month, Year chosen one at a time each stay selected; Ignore on "notes" gives format %d~%m~%Y with the checkbox ticked; 3 of 3 convert. Ignoring a middle part shows the alert.
  - Date to text with "Thursday 30 January 2020": 2020-10-01 23:59:59 becomes "Thursday 01 October 2020"; the check's Value column shows "2020-10-01 23:59:59".
- Implementation: Backend `services/conversion.py`, `services/node_casting.py`, `services/node_operations.py`, `services/nodes.py`, `api/workspaces/nodes.py`, `models/node_resources.py`, `domain/workspace/provenance.py`. Frontend `ConversionPanel.tsx`, `dateFormatBuilder.ts`, `WorkspaceTable.tsx`, `columnMutationState.ts`, `useColumnMutations.ts`, `useWorkspaceDataTable.ts`, `useWorkspaceTransformMutations.ts`.
- Tests: `test_conversion.py` (detection of ISO, Twitter, names, swap, two-digit years, Unix units, Excel; centuries; Excel and Unix casts; numbers with comma decimal and strict thousands; rounding; check counts and failures); API surface test; `dateFormatBuilder.test.ts`; `ConversionPanel.test.tsx` (day first and swap, century required, number defaults); updated `useColumnMutations`, `columnMutationState`, `useWorkspaceNodeMutations` tests; Playwright WebKit runs on an isolated instance.
- v0.8 notes: keep one window and the whole-column check before every interpreting conversion (rule R10). DuckDB: `strptime` with a list of formats can try candidates, `try_strptime` gives null on failure (the check counts), `to_timestamp` for Unix seconds (`epoch_ms` etc. for other units), Excel as `DATE '1899-12-30' + INTERVAL (n) DAY`. For dates inside text, DuckDB `strptime` needs the whole string, so extract the date first with `regexp_extract` built from the format (each code as a digit or letter class), then parse. Note that DuckDB `%y` uses a fixed pivot, so shift years from the user's century choice yourself. Keep the format detection on a sample of the first values, but run the check on every row. Store the options in the plan so a saved Project replays them.

#### V07-243 Elapsed time data type for transcript timestamps: load, convert, display, Filter, Group, Aggregate, Trends, export (#324)

- Commits: f23bff49
- Area: Data Editor
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-07, for 0.7.11: transcription is an important use case that was overlooked. A transcript's 07:58.5 (7 min 58.5 s into a recording) in an Excel cell formatted mm:ss.0 loaded as a date and time on 31 Dec 1899; converting to text with %M:%S%.3f kept the value but could not be binned or used in Trends. Decisions: a type named "Elapsed time" (Duration), not time of day (clock times can wait and do not conflict with date and time); conversions to and from text and decimal numbers of seconds, minutes, hours; Trends binning; included in 0.7.11, which is no longer a hotfix release.
- What changed:
  - Backend `shared/elapsed_time.py`: `ELAPSED_DTYPE = pl.Duration("us")`; `elapsed_from_text(expr, two_part)` (regex groups for h:mm:ss and two-part m:ss or h:mm, comma before the fraction accepted, minutes/seconds parts 0 to 59, else null); `elapsed_from_numbers(expr, unit)` (ms, s, min, h, d; days rounded to the millisecond); `elapsed_from_datetime` (time of day); `elapsed_to_number(expr, unit)`; `elapsed_to_text(expr, style)` (auto: m:ss under an hour, h:mm:ss from an hour, trimmed fraction only when not zero; h:mm:ss, h:mm:ss.fff, mm:ss, mm:ss.fff with minutes past 59); `parse_elapsed` (Python, for Filter values); `as_text(expr, dtype)` (Polars cannot cast Duration to String); `is_excel_day_zero` (every non-empty value on 30 or 31 Dec 1899). Every public expression keeps the column name (`.name.keep()`).
  - Loading: `normalize_dtypes` turns an Excel day-zero Datetime column into elapsed time (change note "the values are times without a date (Excel keeps them on 30 or 31 December 1899), so Wordflow reads them as elapsed time", shown by `notifyTypeAdjustments`) and Duration of other units into microseconds.
  - Casts: target `duration` ("elapsed time") from text/category, numbers (`elapsed_unit`), dates and times; from Duration only to string (`elapsed_text`), float or integer (`elapsed_unit`, rounding half away from zero); other targets refused in plain words. `CastDerivation` / `ConversionOptions` gain `elapsed_unit`, `two_part_times`, `elapsed_text`; `_cast_is_no_op` knows duration; `check_cast` shows both sides as elapsed text; the emptied-values report and the Data Editor's changed-rows count read Duration through `as_text` (a Polars String cast failed: Chao's report).
  - Data Editor tools (Clean text, Split, Count, Combine, Find & replace, Segment) read an elapsed column as its text. Group summary: sum and mean accepted for Duration; join_text and distinct_values use `as_text`. Join type label "elapsed time".
  - Filter: a Duration column parses typed values with `parse_elapsed` ("'soon' is not an elapsed time. Type it like 7:58, 7:58.5 or 1:23:20."); ranges use Duration literals. Frontend operators "at or after", "at or before", "between", "is empty"; text inputs ("e.g. 7:58"); prefill with the column's smallest and largest values like numbers.
  - Trends: `column_type` "elapsed" with frequency second, minute, hourly, or custom seconds/minutes/hours; bins from 0 by integer microseconds; `time_period`, `period_start`, `period_end` in seconds; `time_period_formatted` like 7:00. `_require_trends_axis` accepts Duration. Frontend: `isTrendsAxisField` and rank 0 for Duration; `elapsedFrequency` / `elapsedUnit` map calendar choices to minutes; the panel lists only sub-day periods and units; chart ticks and the HTML download use portable formatter kind `elapsed`.
  - Export: CSV writes elapsed text; Excel writes day fractions with format `[h]:mm:ss.000` (reads back as elapsed time); Parquet and JSON unchanged.
  - Frontend display: `lib/elapsedTime.ts` (`formatElapsed`, `parseElapsed`, `arrowDurationMicros`); `arrowTable` formats Duration values (including the digit strings a whole-row decode leaves), type name "elapsed time" with Timer symbol; Quotation native rows too. Type menu "Elapsed time"; an elapsed column offers only Text, Whole number, Decimal. ConversionPanel modes `elapsed` ("A time with two parts, such as 7:58, is" minutes and seconds / hours and minutes), `elapsed-number` ("The numbers count" milliseconds, seconds, minutes, hours, days (as Excel stores times)), `elapsed-text` ("Written like" 7:58.542, 0:07:58, 0:07:58.542, 07:58, 07:58.542), `elapsed-out` (unit). Builder `ColumnKind` "elapsed": Group "One Data Block per N minutes of elapsed time, from 0:00" (SQL `FLOOR(CAST(col AS BIGINT) / step)`, conditions with elapsed text); Aggregate offers sum, mean, min, max.
  - Help: ui.md (type table, Elapsed time section, conversions), sequential-analysis.md, preprocessing.md (Filter, Group, Aggregate).
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - transcript.xlsx with StartTime formatted mm:ss.0 loads with StartTime as elapsed time (Timer symbol), cells 9:06.154 (Playwright WebKit).
  - StartText "09:06.2" to Elapsed time: 60 of 60 values convert, 9:06.2. StartTime to Decimal in minutes: 9.1025… .
  - Trends on StartTime, Customised every 10 minutes: axis 0:00, 10:00 … 50:00; only Per second, Per minute, Hourly, Customised offered; units seconds, minutes, hours.
  - Filter between 1:00 and 7:58.5 keeps those rows; "soon" is refused in plain words. Exports: CSV 7:58.5, Excel reads back as elapsed time.
  - Elapsed column to Text, Whole number, Decimal converts without the earlier Duration-to-String error.
- Implementation: Backend `shared/elapsed_time.py`, `infrastructure/storage/data_loading.py`, `services/conversion.py`, `services/node_casting.py`, `services/node_operations.py`, `services/nodes.py`, `services/data_block_exports.py`, `analysis/sequential_core.py`, `services/analysis_preparation_registry.py`, `domain/workspace/analysis.py`, `domain/workspace/provenance.py`. Frontend `lib/elapsedTime.ts`, `lib/arrow/arrowTable.ts`, `lib/chartHtml/portableFormatter.ts`, Data View type menu and symbol, `ConversionPanel.tsx`, `useColumnMutations.ts`, Filter, Group, Aggregate, Trends files, `notifyTypeAdjustments.tsx`, `quotationArrowPage.ts`.
- Tests: `test_elapsed_time.py` (parsing, display, units, conversions both ways, refusal, check, Excel day-zero load, Trends bins, Filter, exports); `test_data_editor_saved_plans.py::test_elapsed_time_plans_can_be_read_back` (15 edits read back through polars-source-utils); `elapsedTime.test.ts`; `arrowTable.test.ts` (decode); `trendsAxisColumns.test.ts`; `builderModels.test.ts` (Group); `ConversionPanel.test.tsx`; `useColumnMutations.test.tsx`; Playwright WebKit on an isolated instance.
- v0.8 notes: DuckDB INTERVAL for elapsed time (or BIGINT microseconds with a logical type); keep the type name, display rule, conversions and window, Excel day-zero detection on load (DuckDB's Excel reader gives TIME or TIMESTAMP 1899-12-30/31), Filter typed values, Group by minutes, sum/mean, Trends bins from 0 with m:ss labels, CSV text and Excel [h]:mm:ss export. Clock times (time of day) remain separate and later.

### Data Builder

#### V07-012 Data Block Edits never change rows (#129)

- Commits: bb8fb01b
- Area: Data Builder (preprocessing), Data Blocks backend, Annotation, Docs
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: In-place edits replace a Data Block's plan, while annotations, analysis results and earlier descendants refer to its rows by position. A filter, sort, select or aggregation edit silently misaligned them. It also makes metadata colouring (#128) safe.
- What changed:
  - Each Data Builder tool has one fixed result destination, shown as a static line instead of the old "Apply result as" select (options were "New Data Block" / "Selected Data Block"): "Result: New Data Block" for Filter, Sample, Join, Stack (and analysis Add to Workspace, Workspace SQL Query); "Result: Updates the selected Data Block" for Find (Replace, Extract) and Create. Filter always shows the "New data block name" input and "Create Data Block" / "Creating Data Block…"; Create always shows "Updating Data Block…".
  - The "Expression" tool tab (CodeMirror typed Polars expression editor) is removed, with its hint, help anchors, tutorial entry and the `@codemirror/view` and `@uiw/react-codemirror` dependencies. Data Builder tabs are now Filter, Sample, Join, Stack, Find, Create.
  - Create's count/mean/sum column operations (the operation popover on column tokens and `.op()` chips) are removed; Create only builds new per-row columns.
  - Backend enforces: `FilterNodeEditRequest` is removed from `NodeEditRequest` (no `filter` edit kind); an `expression` edit accepts only `context: "with_columns"`; annotation Run All publication raises "Annotation Run All output does not keep the Data Block's rows" when the published row count differs. Kept in place: cast, rename_column, delete_column, replace, expression with_columns, set_cell. Documented exception: `annotation_classes` (codebook Data Block rows are the class list).
  - Older workspaces are not checked or migrated.
  - ADR 0031 `docs/adr/0031-data-block-edits-preserve-rows.md` supersedes the create-or-update clause of ADR 0007; `CONTEXT.md`, `docs/domain/workspaces-and-data-blocks.md`, `docs/reference/backend-api.md`, and tutorials `preprocessing.md`, `ui.md`, information `preprocessing.md` updated (screenshot `create_expression.png` deleted).
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - No "Apply result as" select appears in any Data Builder tool; Filter shows "Result: New Data Block"; Find and Create show "Result: Updates the selected Data Block".
  - Applying a Filter always creates a new Data Block and leaves the source unchanged.
  - There is no "Expression" tab.
  - `POST /nodes/{id}/edits` with kind `filter`, or `expression` with context `filter`/`select`/`sort`/`group_by_agg`, returns a validation error (422).
  - Annotation Run All output with a different row count fails to publish.
- Implementation: backend `models/node_resources.py`, `services/node_operations.py`, `services/analysis_artifacts.py`; frontend `preprocessing/DataPreprocessingFeature.tsx`, `components/PreprocessingApplyBar.tsx` (prop `mode`), `preprocessingApplyMode.ts` (`UPDATE_DATA_BLOCK_MODE`), `filter/FilterSubTab.tsx`, `replace/ReplaceSubTab.tsx`, `aggregate/AggregateSubTab.tsx`, `aggregate/hooks/aggregateExpressionModel.ts`, `useAggregateSubTab.ts`, `common/hooks/useWorkspaceTransformMutations.ts`, `utils/autoNodeNames.ts`, `guidance/registry.ts`, `tutorials/bundledRegistry.ts`; deleted `expression/*` and `aggregate/components/OperationPopover.tsx`.
- Tests: new `backend/tests/unit/test_row_preserving_node_edits.py`; `test_canonical_node_api.py` (filter edit case removed); frontend `DataPreprocessingFeature.test.tsx`, `aggregateExpressionModel.test.ts`, `useWorkspaceNodeMutations.test.tsx`, `guidance/__tests__/registry.test.tsx` updated; expression and autoNodeNames tests removed.
- v0.8 notes: This is a core invariant: in-place edits never change row count or order, because results reference rows by position. In DuckDB, preserve a stable row identity (explicit row id column) and enforce the rule server-side.
