### Data folder, storage and runtime

#### V07-015 Startup snapshot cleanup is best effort, never blocks the Data Root (#131)

- Commits: c7b15a76 (test typing follow-up in 1a9e6ad2)
- Area: Storage / Data Root (backend runtime), desktop (Windows)
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: A Windows desktop user was stuck on the first-start Data Root screen ("Choose another Data Root") with `PermissionError: [WinError 5] Access is denied: '...\.response-snapshots\resources'`. The doubled backslashes are only Python's repr. Startup ran `shutil.rmtree` on abandoned response snapshots (`ResponseSnapshotService.reconcile()`); Windows refuses to delete files another process holds open (a second backend on the same Data Root, antivirus/Defender, the indexer). Any error aborted Runtime initialization and the UI reported "Data Root required".
- What changed: Startup cleanup of `.response-snapshots` and of abandoned query snapshots is best effort: locked paths are logged (warning) and left for the next start instead of aborting Data Root initialization. This reverses the earlier "propagate cleanup failure" rule. Trade-off: a locked orphan may use some disk until a later start removes it.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - If a file under `.response-snapshots` cannot be deleted at startup, the Runtime still opens and a warning "Left N abandoned response snapshot path(s) that could not be removed, first: <path>" is logged.
  - If the query snapshot root cannot be fully removed, a warning "Abandoned query snapshots could not be fully removed from <path>" is logged and startup continues.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/services/response_snapshots.py` (`shutil.rmtree(root, onexc=skip_locked)`, collects OSError paths; `onexc` needs Python 3.12+); `services/analysis_results.py` (try/except OSError around `_remove_query_root`).
- Tests: new `backend/tests/unit/test_startup_snapshot_cleanup.py`; `test_storage_admission.py` now asserts skip-and-log. 1a9e6ad2 uses `monkeypatch` for the run helper to satisfy `ty`.
- v0.8 notes: Treat all snapshot/cache cleanup as best effort on Windows. Follow-up suggested on the issue (not done): the Tauri shell has no single-instance guard (`tauri-plugin-single-instance`), so launching twice starts a second backend on the same Data Root.

#### V07-016 Open the default Data Root on first start (#130)

- Commits: 1ebdb6b4
- Area: Data Root / Settings (backend runtime), Docs
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: First start asked users to choose between the recommended location and another folder, a decision they had no basis for.
- What changed: Without `DATA_ROOT` or saved configuration, a single-user backend opens the platform's suggested Data Root (e.g. `%LOCALAPPDATA%\au.edu.ldaca.wordflow\data` on Windows) without asking, and saves it to `settings.json` once it opens (if saving fails, it is logged and repeated next start). Users can still move it under Settings. The chooser appears only if the default cannot open, showing the real error. Multi-user and environment-managed deployments are unchanged. UI tutorial text: "On first launch without `DATA_ROOT` or saved configuration, Wordflow uses the recommended location for your operating system and remembers it. There is nothing to choose." and "If that location cannot be used (for example, it is not writable), a setup screen shows the error and lets you choose another folder."
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Fresh single-user start with no config: no Data Root prompt; `settings.json` afterwards contains the suggested root; the runtime source becomes "config".
  - If the default root cannot be opened, the setup screen shows the actual error and lets the user pick another folder.
  - Multi-user mode still requires configuration as before.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/runtime.py` (`_RuntimeOwnerRequest.persist`, `_initialize_owned(..., persist=True)` writes via `self._config_store.write`, uses `self._config_store.paths.suggested_data_root`).
- Tests: `backend/tests/unit/test_data_root_bootstrap.py` (default opens, default fails).
- Docs: `docs/architecture/backend/runtime.md`, `docs/architecture/frontend/desktop.md`, `docs/reference/backend-settings.md`, `frontend/public/tutorials/ui.md`.
- v0.8 notes: Keep zero-question first start for the desktop/single-user app.

#### V07-149 Windows: staging paths kept under 260 characters (#232)

- Commits: 365c548a
- Area: Data folder, storage and runtime
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: On a fresh Windows install, adding a Parquet file (or a folder of them) failed with FileNotFoundError (workspace_corrupt, HTTP 500). A Project snapshot staged under the default Data Root reached 261 characters (`…\workspaces\.staging\.snapshot-<Project id>-<32 hex>\data\.<Data Block id>.r2-<32 hex>.plbin.<random>.tmp`); Windows refuses paths over 259 characters unless long paths are enabled, which is off by default. The naming predates v0.7, so 0.7.7 had the same bug.
- What changed: generated names are short and keep their leading dot and suffix (cleanup and quota code rely on both): staging folder `.snapshot-<12 hex>`; `atomic_output_path` temporaries `.t-*.tmp` (no longer repeating the target's name); input snapshot staging `.s-*.tmp`; uploads `.u-*.upload`; plan validation copies `.v-*.validate` (not `.plbin`, which the data-folder scan deletes); sample downloads `.d-*.download`.
- Expected behaviour (testable): the reported path becomes 127 characters; temporary names are at most 16 characters whatever the target; adding files works on Windows under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\au.edu.ldaca.wordflow\data` with a long user name.
- Implementation: `infrastructure/storage/{durable_fs,input_snapshots,safe_paths,workspace_store}.py`, `services/{sample_data,workspace_coordination}.py`.
- Tests: `tests/unit/test_windows_path_budget.py`; full backend suite.
- v0.8 notes: budget every generated path for Windows' 260-character limit; never build temporary names from the target's full name.

#### V07-150 Desktop: a slow first start is allowed; plain startup messages (#231)

- Commits: af5f19ee
- Area: Data folder, storage and runtime (desktop shell)
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: A fresh install on a 2-CPU Windows VM showed "Backend did not complete startup before the deadline"; the second start worked. The desktop bundle has no precompiled bytecode for its libraries (7,870 `.py`, bytecode only for Python itself) and runs with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, so every start compiles every imported module, and a fresh install also waits for antivirus scans. Measured on an M-series Mac: cold import 7.7 s as shipped, 0.7 s with bytecode.
- What changed: `STARTUP_TIMEOUT` 30 s → 180 s (a crashed backend still fails at once through the exit check). Timeout detail: "Wordflow took too long to start. This can happen the first time on a slow computer."; dialog title "Wordflow couldn't start", ending "Close this message and open Wordflow again. If it keeps happening, please send feedback."
- Expected behaviour (testable): a first start that takes up to three minutes succeeds; a backend crash still shows the dialog at once.
- Implementation: `frontend/src-tauri/src/backend_process.rs`, `supervisor.rs`; runbook `docs/runbooks/desktop-runtime.md`.
- Tests: `cargo test` (27 passed); manual check on the Windows VM with the next build.
- v0.8 notes: precompile bytecode (or use a native server) so starts are fast; keep a generous first-start allowance.

#### V07-151 Windows: short names for sources an Analysis stages (#233)

- Commits: db26554b
- Area: Data folder, storage and runtime
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: After #232, Topic Modelling still failed on Windows with FileNotFoundError [WinError 3]: an Analysis staged each source as `<64-hex SHA-256 of its path>.parquet` under `analyses\<id>\.execution\<tmp>\sources`, a 265-character path under the default data folder. The Python backend is not long-path aware on Windows unless `LongPathsEnabled` is set machine-wide. The hash names each staged source uniquely within one Analysis; it is not a content fingerprint.
- What changed: 16 hex characters instead of 64 (`input_snapshots.py`). The reported path becomes 217 characters (222 with a 20-character user name). Shipped by rebuilding only the v0.7.8 Windows installer (Chao's decision): MSI, signature and the Windows entry of `latest.json` replaced, and a one-line notice added to the release notes; macOS, PyPI and the tag unchanged.
- Expected behaviour (testable): Topic Modelling and other Analyses run on Windows under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\au.edu.ldaca.wordflow\data`; staged source names are at most 16 hex characters plus the extension.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/infrastructure/storage/input_snapshots.py`.
- Tests: `test_worker_input_snapshots.py` (real staged names), `test_windows_path_budget.py` (path budget).
- v0.8 notes: Rust's standard library uses extended-length paths on Windows automatically, so v0.8 should not hit this, as long as file access goes through `std::fs`. For any remaining Python in v0.8, budget paths or use `\\?\` paths.

#### V07-166 Server upgrade from 0.7.6: user database 6 to 7, and Project migration (#250)

- Commits: 570ffcd9
- Area: Data folder, storage and runtime
- Type: feature
- Problem / reason: Upgrading wordflow.ldaca.edu.au from 0.7.6 hit two blockers. The service would not start: `deployment.sqlite3` was at `user_version` 6, and since 3693d82a the backend expects 7, with no upgrade step (the tables are identical; 7 only validates indexes more strictly). And 0.7.6 Projects did not open: they stored Data Blocks as serialized Polars plans tied to 0.7.6's Polars build, without `data_schema_version`.
- What changed:
  - The backend upgrades a version-6 user database to 7 on startup when it passes every version-7 check, in the same transaction; a different version-6 structure is still rejected.
  - `backend/scripts/migrate_076_server_data.py`: two phases. Export runs with the 0.7.6 install and writes each Project's Data Blocks as Parquet with a current archive manifest (read-only on the old folder). Import runs with the new install, creates a new data folder, copies users, files and preferences, and imports each Project through the archive importer. Tabs and analyses are not migrated.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - A version-6 database that matches version 7's structure opens and is labelled 7; any other version-6 database is refused and left at 6.
  - Used on production on 2026-10-01: 3 Projects and 10 Data Blocks imported, 5 users, database version 7.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/infrastructure/database.py`, `backend/scripts/migrate_076_server_data.py`.
- Tests: `tests/unit/test_database_schema.py` (version 6 upgraded; mismatched version 6 refused).
- v0.8 notes: v0.8 has its own storage; keep the principle that a server upgrade never rewrites the old data folder and has a tested rollback.

#### V07-167 Hosted server: WebSocket requests refused cleanly in the host check (#251)

- Commits: 00af2de9
- Area: Hosted server runtime
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Internet scanners send WebSocket upgrades. `ExactHostMiddleware` let `websocket` scopes through and then built an HTTP-only `Request` from them, so each one logged an uncaught `AssertionError` and returned 500 (seen in production on 15 and 27 September 2026).
- What changed:
  - The host check reads the host with `HTTPConnection`, which supports both scope types, and closes an untrusted-host WebSocket with code 1008.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - A WebSocket request with a trusted host gets the normal refusal; with an untrusted host it is closed with 1008; neither logs a traceback.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/api/security.py`.
- Tests: `tests/integration/test_session_cookie_contract.py::test_websocket_request_is_refused_without_a_server_error`.
- v0.8 notes: any ASGI middleware must handle every scope type it lets through.

#### V07-168 Hosted server: a stop no longer waits 90 s for open browser tabs (#252)

- Commits: 71af094d, 3dbfa83c
- Area: Hosted server runtime
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Every open tab holds a Server-Sent Events stream (`/api/events`). Uvicorn ran without `timeout_graceful_shutdown`, so on SIGTERM it waited forever for those connections before the lifespan shutdown, which is what would close them. A stop therefore waited for systemd's 90 s timeout and ended in SIGKILL, and Wordflow's own shutdown never ran. Production showed it during the 0.7.9.1 cutover.
- What changed:
  - The server closes the event hub as soon as a stop begins (a `uvicorn.Server` subclass overrides `shutdown`), so every stream returns normally.
  - `CONNECTION_DRAIN_SECONDS = 5` is passed as `timeout_graceful_shutdown`, as a backstop for other long requests.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - With open event streams, a stop finishes in well under 5 s, the streams end cleanly, and nothing is logged at ERROR. Dev server: 0.28 s with 3 open streams (was 90 s).
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/server_launcher.py`.
- Tests: `tests/unit/test_server_launcher.py::test_open_event_stream_ends_cleanly_when_the_server_stops` (real server; fails before the fix).
- v0.8 notes: long-lived streams must end on shutdown; bound the connection drain.

#### V07-169 Hosted server: another user's refused request no longer closes your Project (#253)

- Commits: 0c37c223
- Area: Hosted server runtime
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: `WorkspaceService._require_open` cleared the residency slot whenever the requester had no path to the Project. That also happens when the requester is not the owner, and slots are shared by Project ID, so another user's PATCH (refused with 404) unloaded the owner's open Project; the owner's next action failed with "Project is not open".
- What changed:
  - The slot is cleared only when the Project's folder is really gone.
  - New HTTP-level multi-user isolation test: a second user tries every route naming the first user's Project, Data Block, tab, analysis and files.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Another user gets 404 on every route naming your resources, and your Project stays open and unchanged. A Project removed on disk is still unloaded.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/services/workspace.py`.
- Tests: `tests/integration/test_multi_user_isolation.py`.
- v0.8 notes: an authorisation failure must have no side effect on the resource's owner.

#### V07-170 Hosted runbook: KillMode=mixed in the systemd unit (#258)

- Commits: ea389573
- Area: Hosted server runtime
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: With systemd's default `KillMode=control-group`, a restart sent SIGTERM to the analysis worker processes at the same moment as the backend. Running analyses were recorded as generic failures (`analysis_execution_failed`, `EOFError`) instead of "Analysis was interrupted", and Python reported leaked semaphores.
- What changed:
  - The runbook's example unit sets `KillMode=mixed` and `TimeoutStopSec=30`. Applied on production and the dev server on 2026-10-02.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - A restart during a run ends running analyses as "Analysis was interrupted"; restart took 0.4 s on the dev server.
- Implementation: `docs/runbooks/hosted-deployment.md`.
- Tests: manual restart under load on the dev server.
- v0.8 notes: a server that supervises worker processes should be the one to stop them.

#### V07-248 Analysis lanes: quick tools never wait behind topic models (#328)

- Commits: 121a0af4
- Area: Data folder, storage and runtime
- Type: behaviour change
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-07, before opening the hosted server to the public for the October workshop and eResearch: two shared analysis slots meant two long Topic Modelling or Quotation runs blocked every Concordance and Token Frequency run on the server until they finished.
- What changed:
  - `services/analysis_scheduler.py`: `analysis_lane(kind)` maps kinds to three lanes, slow (`topic_modeling`, `topic_modeling_data_block_creation`, `quotation`, `quotation_run_all`), medium (`annotation`, `annotation_run_all`) and fast (everything else). `AnalysisScheduler` takes `capacity: int | Mapping[AnalysisLane, int]` and runs one `FairSchedulerKernel` per lane; start, enqueue, cancel, workspace cancel, stop, active keys, has_work and wait_idle go through every lane; an unknown lane falls back to fast.
  - `AnalysisLane` exported from `services/analysis_execution_types.py`; `enqueue(..., lane)` in the protocol and `services/analysis_execution.py`; `services/analyses.py` passes `analysis_lane(request.kind)`.
  - `settings.py`: `analysis_execution_capacity` (fast, default 4, env `ANALYSIS_EXECUTION_CAPACITY`), `analysis_medium_capacity` (2, `ANALYSIS_MEDIUM_CAPACITY`), `analysis_slow_capacity` (2, `ANALYSIS_SLOW_CAPACITY`); `runtime.py` passes the three.
  - Docs: `docs/architecture/backend/background-work.md` (lanes), `docs/runbooks/hosted-deployment.md` (new Analysis capacity section; suggests `POLARS_TEXT_EMBEDDING_THREADS=8` on the 32-core server so one topic model cannot take every core).
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - With the slow lane full (two Topic Modelling runs), a Concordance run starts at once (`test_a_fast_analysis_never_waits_behind_full_slow_lane`).
  - Each kind maps to its lane (`test_analysis_kinds_map_to_lanes`); cancel and Stop still reach a run in any lane.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/services/analysis_scheduler.py`, `analysis_execution.py`, `analysis_execution_types.py`, `analyses.py`, `settings.py`, `runtime.py`.
- Tests: `test_analysis_scheduler.py` (lane mapping, fast run beside a full slow lane), `test_analysis_service.py` (execution double with lanes); full backend suite.
- v0.8 notes: Keep long and quick analyses in separate pools on the server (static lanes are enough); a per-job core cap (`POLARS_TEXT_EMBEDDING_THREADS` or the v0.8 equivalent) stops one topic model taking the machine. An active-users indicator for the server UI is still to be designed.

#### V07-253 The per-file limit on analysis processes no longer caps the shared caches (#257)

- Commits: a3e0540a
- Area: Data folder, storage and runtime
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Found in server stress testing (2026-10-02) and fixed for 0.7.11 (Chao, 2026-10-07): every analysis process ran with RLIMIT_FSIZE equal to the 1 GiB analysis budget, which also applied to the per-user tokens.duckdb and embeddings.duckdb. Once a cache passed 1 GiB, every later analysis writing to it failed permanently with the generic message. With large corpora now finishing, one Obesity run adds about 160 MB to the embedding cache and a Max tokens 64 run about 750 MB.
- What changed:
  - `services/supervised_process.py`: `PROCESS_FILE_SIZE_CEILING_BYTES` (64 GiB) and `process_file_size_limit(max_storage_bytes)`, the larger of the two, used for `RLIMIT_FSIZE`. The analysis budget is still enforced on its own storage tree every 0.1 s by `_receive_result`.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - An analysis can write a cache past 1 GiB; an analysis whose own output passes the budget is still stopped.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/services/supervised_process.py`.
- Tests: `test_supervised_process.py::test_process_file_limit_does_not_cap_shared_caches_at_the_analysis_budget`.
- v0.8 notes: v0.8's DuckDB caches must not share a per-analysis write cap either; caches need their own limit and clean-up (#259).

#### V07-254 Settings: see and clear the Topic Modelling and Tokeniser caches (#334)

- Commits: d928fa22
- Area: Data folder, storage and runtime
- Type: new feature
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-07: v0.5.4 had a Clear cache control, lost in the refactoring; v0.7 had no way to see or clear the per-user caches, which grow with every corpus. The full design (limit, weekly clean-up, clear by tool and last use) stays in v0.8 (#259); 0.7.11 gets a minimal manual clear.
- What changed:
  - Backend `services/tool_caches.py`: `tool_cache_size` (database plus `.wal`) and `clear_tool_cache`, which takes the same exclusive `<cache>.lock` polars-text holds (`cache.rs` `with_file_lock`; flock on POSIX, msvcrt on Windows), waits up to 5 s, then raises `ToolCacheBusyError` (409 `tool_cache_busy`). API `GET /api/tool-caches`, `DELETE /api/tool-caches/{kind}` (`topic_modeling`, `tokeniser`), current user's `.cache/users/<id>/` only.
  - Frontend `components/dialogs/ToolCacheSettingsPanel.tsx` in Settings > Project under Data folder: a row per cache with its size (or Empty, Clear disabled), an inline confirmation naming the size and the slower next run, and a toast with the space freed.
  - Help `ui.md` `help-ui-tool-caches`.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Settings > Project > Tool caches shows Topic Modelling 48 MB and Tokeniser Empty (Playwright WebKit against the running app).
  - Clear asks first; confirming removes the file and reports the space freed; the other cache is untouched.
  - While polars-text holds the cache lock, clearing answers 409 and keeps the file.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/services/tool_caches.py`, `models/tool_caches.py`, `api/tool_caches.py`, `shared/errors.py`, `main.py`; `frontend/src/components/dialogs/ToolCacheSettingsPanel.tsx`, `SettingsDialog.tsx`; `frontend/public/tutorials/ui.md`.
- Tests: `test_tool_caches.py` (sizes, clear one keeps the other, busy under the lock, HTTP list and clear with CSRF, unknown kind 422); `ToolCacheSettingsPanel.test.tsx` (sizes, Empty disabled, confirm then clear and toast); API surface test.
- v0.8 notes: #259 replaces this with the planned limit, weekly clean-up and clear by tool and last use; keep clearing under the cache's own lock.

#### V07-258 Download closed Projects safely; Open, Delete and Download respect a Project open in another Wordflow (#338)

- Commits: 8118cf86
- Area: Data folder, storage and runtime
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-08: the desktop app and a local server shared a data folder; the second showed the Project as open elsewhere but offered Delete and Download. Tested with two backends on one data folder: Open and Delete from the second were refused (409 workspace_in_use; deletion_context already takes the cross-process lock), but Download was refused with workspace_not_open, and in fact failed for every closed Project because export read only Projects open in this backend while every Project card offers Download. Chao chose to download closed Projects under the same lock.
- What changed:
  - `WorkspaceService.closed_read_context(user_id, workspace_id)`: holds the Project's slot and cross-process lock (raises `WorkspaceInUseError` if another backend has it open) while the caller reads it; the archive export uses it for a Project open in no backend, then builds the ZIP from the private copy (`_export_snapshot`, shared with the open path).
  - Messages: `WorkspaceInUseError` reads "This Project is open in another Wordflow window or app. Close it there, then try again."; lock-unavailable, schema-version, revision-conflict, lock-entry, Data Block path, archive and Topic Modelling messages say Project instead of Workspace.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Two backends on one data folder: while A has a Project open, B's Open, Delete and Download each answer 409 workspace_in_use and the Project stays; after A closes, B opens it.
  - Download of a closed Project (open nowhere) returns the ZIP.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/services/workspace.py`, `services/workspace_archives.py`, `shared/errors.py`, `infrastructure/storage/workspace_store.py`, `workspace_lock.py`, `node_store.py`, `workers/topic_modeling.py`.
- Tests: `test_workspace_resource_api.py` (closed Project downloads; Download refused while another backend has it open; new message); `test_workspace_service.py`; two-backend manual check.
- v0.8 notes: Every operation on a stored Project (open, delete, export) must respect the cross-process lock; closed Projects must be exportable under it. Say Project in user-visible text.

#### V07-126 Help: one plain "How Preview, Run and Clear work" section; fix help that contradicts the UI (#205 items 23 and 24)

- Commits: 35fc5dbb
- Area: help pages (tutorials, information), help registry
- Type: docs
- Problem / reason: item 23: the Preview/Run/Clear lifecycle was explained in developer terms ("immutable snapshot", "projection", "Analysis forest", "root", "Supporting Analysis", "mutable Data Block") across several pages, and draft notes were visible (a stray "Token Frequency" line, "[anything else you can change?]", "link to LDACA site?"). Item 24: help contradicted the UI.
- What changed:
  - New ui.md section `#help-ui-preview-run-clear` "How Preview, Run and Clear work" (registry key `ui.preview-run-clear`), linked from every analysis tool page. Content: Preview works out a first few pages; Run works through all data (needed for sorting every column, whole-result charts, and before Add to Project); Clear removes this tab's Preview and Run results but not Data Blocks already added nor changes written to a Data Block (use Undo in the Data Editor). What to expect: results keep the data they were made from (edits later do not change them; Preview/Run again); buttons turn on only when a relevant setting changes (and off again if changed back); settings are locked while a task runs, use Stop; after a failure or a stop, settings can be edited but Preview and Run stay off until Clear; results stay with the tab across tool switches and Project reopen.
  - Developer terms removed from concordance.md, quotation.md, annotation.md, token-frequency.md, sequential-analysis.md, topic-modeling.md (tutorials and information).
  - Draft notes removed from information/token-frequency.md.
  - Contradictions fixed: "Head / Tail Rows (N)" (gone; now "Words per page"); Trends custom interval default is 1 minute (not 1 day); empty periods explained per Spacing option (Even hides them, To scale shows gaps); Concordance options written in the UI's case ("Use regular expression / Whole word / Case sensitive"); "Concordance Match Data Block Creation" removed; panel names "Tool Choice / Data Selection / Task Centre" to "Views / Data Blocks / Tasks" (ui.md headings "1. Views", "2. Data Blocks", "3. Tasks"; anchors unchanged `help-ui-tool-choice`, `help-ui-data-selection`, `help-ui-task-centre`; registry labels updated).
- Expected behaviour (testable): every analysis tool help page links to "How Preview, Run and Clear work"; searching help for "snapshot", "projection", "Analysis forest" or "Supporting Analysis" finds nothing.
- Implementation: `frontend/public/tutorials/*.md`, `frontend/public/information/*.md`, `frontend/src/tutorials/bundledRegistry.ts`.
- Tests: `frontend/src/tutorials/__tests__/documentationRegistry.test.ts` (label update).
- v0.8 notes: the lifecycle rules described here (results are snapshots, buttons enable on relevant change only, Clear required after failure/stop, results persist per tab) are the v0.7 behaviour contract; if v0.8 changes any of them, update this section.

#### V07-181 Help window: pinned header, theme-following text, gamma curve for screenshots, no "tutorial" (#262)

- Commits: 6181e016, eed59d4b, 9ea73f17, 591cdcd8 (guard tests), e4075bb2
- Area: Help pages
- Type: interface change
- Problem / reason: Reviewed in both themes for 0.7.10. The header scrolled away and its title read "LDaCA References"; in the dark theme Help text was dark on dark (Tailwind `prose-slate` is a fixed light palette); screenshots glared in the dark theme; and thin light-grey details in screenshots (Project Graph edges in `ui/graph_controls.png`, a 1x capture) nearly vanished in both themes. Pages still said "tutorial" although the UI calls them Help. Issue #262 records why each choice was made; keep it, because these are easy to undo by "simplifying".
- What changed:
  - Header: `sticky top-0` inside the dialog scroll area; grid `minmax(max-content,1fr) auto minmax(max-content,1fr)` so the two side columns are equal and the logo strip (LDaCA, ARDC, NCRIS | Sydney Corpus Lab, SIH, each linked) is exactly centred; title (Help, Information, References) left, Close and zoom right; stacks on narrow windows. `1fr auto 1fr` is not enough: the wider controls push the strip off centre.
  - Anchor offset: the header's measured height (ResizeObserver) sets `--doc-header-offset` (height + 16 px), used as `scroll-margin-top` on every `.doc-prose [id]` and on `.tutorial-highlight`. Without it, a help link scrolls its section under the pinned header.
  - Text: `prose doc-prose` replaces `prose prose-slate`; `.doc-prose` in `index.css` maps every `--tw-prose-*` colour to theme variables (`--vscode-foreground`, `--vscode-descriptionForeground`, `--vscode-textLink-foreground`, `--vscode-surface-border`, `--vscode-panel-background`). Do not use `dark:prose-invert`: the dark theme is `data-theme="dark-2026"`.
  - Screenshots: `.doc-prose img { filter: url(#doc-image-gamma) brightness(0.9) }`, an inline SVG `feComponentTransfer` gamma, exponent 1.4 on R, G and B, `color-interpolation-filters="sRGB"`, rendered once by DocumentView; the dark theme uses `brightness(0.8)`; no hover state. Light brightness 0.9 ("option C", chosen after comparing 23 screenshots): the graph edges measure 239 to 246 against a 252 to 255 background, and a 10-level difference stays invisible on full-brightness white (they appeared only under a selection tint), so white has to be softened as well. Measured reasons: `contrast()` pivots on mid-grey and pushes light greys to white (`contrast(1.25)` erased the graph edges and grey node boxes); the first dark filter `brightness(0.8) contrast(1.1)` only looked clearer because brightness moved greys off white first, and full brightness on hover was the least readable; gamma above 1 darkens light and mid greys while white and black stay put; exponent 2 over-saturated pastel highlights such as the Concordance pink.
  - Wording: page titles are the tool name; "← Back to Help home"; About pages say "the Concordance page in Help"; the dialog's accessible title is Help. Internal names (`tutorials/`, the `tutorial` doc kind, `help-tutorial-index`) unchanged.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - Scrolling a long Help page keeps the header visible; the logo strip's centre equals the dialog's centre at the default window size; a help link's section starts just below the header.
  - In the dark theme Help text is light on dark; screenshots are dimmed but Project Graph edges in the User Interface page stay visible; in the light theme those edges are visible and white stays white.
  - No reader-visible "tutorial" in Help.
- Implementation: `frontend/src/components/DocumentView.tsx` (header, `useElementHeight`, `DOC_IMAGE_GAMMA`, SVG filter), `components/help/PartnerLogos.tsx`, `frontend/src/index.css` (`.doc-prose` rules), `components/dialogs/DocumentModalHost.tsx`, `public/tutorials/*.md`, `public/information/*.md`.
- Tests: `src/__tests__/docProseStyles.test.ts` (theme-token prose colours, gamma filter and no `contrast()` on doc images, anchor offset) and `components/__tests__/DocumentView.test.tsx` ("keeps the Help window layout recorded in issue 262"); both fail if a rule is removed.
- v0.8 notes: port the same four behaviours with the same reasons; if screenshots are retaken at 2x with thicker edges the gamma can be reconsidered, but not replaced by `contrast()`.

#### V07-183 Desktop app Help loads the published docs channel (#264)

- Commits: 809be306
- Area: Help pages
- Type: bug fix
- Problem / reason: Help fetches Markdown and the registry from the `VITE_DOCS_ORIGIN` docs channel (GitHub Pages) and falls back to the built-in docs on failure. The Tauri CSP allowed `connect-src` and `img-src` only to the app and the local backend, so in every desktop release the fetch failed silently and published docs fixes never reached desktop users (0.7.9 desktop still showed "Back to tutorial index" after the docs tag moved). Web builds were unaffected: no CSP, and the docs site sends `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`.
- What changed:
  - `frontend/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`: `https://australian-text-analytics-platform.github.io` added to `connect-src` and `img-src` in `csp` and `devCsp`.
  - Offline or with the site down, Help still falls back to the built-in docs.
  - Note the split this exposed: the docs channel updates only the page Markdown and images; the Help window itself (header, logos, theme, screenshot filters) is app code and changes only with an app update.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - In the 0.7.10 desktop app, after a docs tag move, Help shows the published text; with networking off it shows the built-in text.
- Implementation: `frontend/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`.
- Tests: `frontend/scripts/desktop-contract.test.mjs` ("lets desktop Help load the published docs channel (#264)") checks both CSPs allow the origin of `VITE_DOCS_ORIGIN` in `frontend/.env`.
- v0.8 notes: `main` has the same bug, logged as #265; port this fix and the test.

- Area: Build/CI

#### V07-064 CI: xlsxwriter allowed again; macOS desktop build fix (no issue; follows #169)

- Commits: 9b47a1bb
- Area: Build/CI
- Type: build/CI
- Problem / reason: `scripts/verify_backend_distribution.py` listed `xlsxwriter` as a removed dependency (the old Excel export was dropped in 299d00b4), but #169 brought Excel export back, so every backend CI job failed. In `desktop-build.yml`, expanding an empty bash array under `set -u` fails on macOS bash 3.2 ("unbound variable"), so a macOS build from checked-out sources (without `--no-sources`) failed in "Prepare backend runtime".
- What changed: `xlsxwriter` removed from the banned list; the pnpm call uses `${args[@]+"${args[@]}"}`.
- Expected behaviour (testable): Backend CI passes with xlsxwriter declared; macOS desktop build with `NO_SOURCES` false passes the "Prepare backend runtime" step.
- Implementation: `.github/workflows/desktop-build.yml`, `scripts/verify_backend_distribution.py`.
- Tests: none (CI itself).
- v0.8 notes: If v0.8 keeps Excel export via xlsxwriter, keep it out of any removed-dependency guard. The bash 3.2 empty-array idiom applies to any macOS runner script.

#### V07-081 CI: six Windows-only backend test failures (#142)

- Commits: 5e55b49a
- Area: Build/CI (backend storage)
- Type: build/CI (with two small production fixes)
- Problem / reason: Six backend tests failed only on the Windows runner (POSIX mode bits, reading a held lock file, path separators).
- What changed:
  - Tests: mode bits (0o600) checked only where they exist; the lock file is read after release and the secret scan skips lock files (Windows byte-range locks are mandatory); plan source paths compared as paths.
  - Production: on Windows a link or reparse point at a workspace lock entry now raises `OSError(errno.ELOOP, "Workspace lock entry is a link or reparse point", path)` (wrapped in `WorkspaceLockStorageError`), matching POSIX `O_NOFOLLOW`; plan relocation compares `Path` objects instead of strings, because Polars stores `C:/...` forward-slash paths on Windows, so Windows no longer rewrites every plan on load or rebase.
  - Not changed: Windows lock files stay unreadable to other tools while a Project is open; private TOML files on Windows rely on the Data Root folder's ACL rather than mode bits.
- Expected behaviour (testable): CI run 36221984547 green on Windows, Linux, macOS; opening a Project on Windows does not rewrite unchanged plans.
- Implementation: `backend/src/ldaca_wordflow/infrastructure/storage/workspace_lock.py` (`acquire_workspace_lock`), `workspace_store.py` (`_rebase_plan_copy`, `_read_workspace`), `input_snapshots.py` (`rebase_worker_input_snapshot_sources`).
- Tests: `test_analysis_service.py`, `test_data_root_bootstrap.py`, `test_private_toml.py`, `test_workspace_lock.py`, `test_workspace_store.py` made platform-aware.
- v0.8 notes: Polars plan path storage is Polars-specific; any stored file paths in DuckDB-era projects should also be compared as paths, not strings, on Windows.

## Planned for v0.8 (new features, not in v0.7)

Designed with the product owner and written up as issues; build them on v0.8 from the specs in the issues.

- **Code designer (#53).** AI-assisted codebook design in the Annotator: a description plus a seeded sample of distinct texts (default 30, at most 200; optional stratification by up to 2 columns, at most 6 groups of at least 20) produce a new Codebook table, a linked examples Data Block (`text`, `code`, `reason`, `correction`) and a summary; redesign rounds from the researcher's corrections; design rows left out of reliability; provenance and a copyable methods note; structured, validated AI output. The agreed spec is in the issue.
- **ZIP and text document loading (#315).** v0.8's local import reads only CSV, TSV, Parquet and JSON, and its sample import only Parquet. Restore v0.7's loading of a ZIP or folder of UTF-8 text files as one Data Block with a row per document (`file_path`, `base_name`, `extension`, `document`, plus the skip report), single text files and Excel, and let sample import download and load non-Parquet catalogue files locally. Product decision (2026-10-06): ZIP and folder loading is a critical Data Loader requirement and sample data should show varied formats, so samples stay in the format they were provided in: Honi Soit is back to `SCL/Honi_Soit.zip` (sample-data d9984da) and Queensland election to its CSV files (a135fc0), with the Parquet copies removed. Until this lands, v0.8's sample import has no file to offer for either collection.
- **Annotator deduplication (#234).** On by default: each distinct text (trimmed) is coded once and its label joined back to every copy; review and reliability count distinct texts, with the basis stated. One open question (copies that already disagree) is noted in the issue.

## Points the recorders could not confirm

- e37bb048: the segmentation and HDBSCAN Auto-cap rules live in polars-text 0.6.1; the entry follows the issue text, not the Rust code.
- 12970c61: about 330 strings were renamed from Workspace to Project; the entry quotes a sample.
- 54bf549d / ad6148fd: LDaCA (Oni) provider internals such as paging and the concurrency limit come from commit messages and issue comments.
- 0230a1e0: Group's date bounds were not checked on time-zone-aware datetime columns other than UTC.
- 73005897 (#189): whether relaxed bubble positions can end up outside the graph canvas.
- 6101fb8f (#197): how %DIFF sorts rows set to N/A, and whether the keyness download also replaces the no-Reference value.
- 919cb08c (#181): sheet names for files ticked inside a folder rely on each file being created as its own request (inferred from docs).
- 2e72095d, 35fc5dbb, 4d85698e: help-page wording edits were skimmed, not read line by line; screenshots in 57c47fc5 were not inspected by the recorder.
- 7ab3eb64: what a Join does when the suffixed name (`col_<right name>`) already exists in the left Data Block.

#### V07-235 pnpm dev picks free ports for both halves and tells each (decision for Alex: apply to main?) (none (Chao, 2026-10-06))

- Commits: 5626bc73
- Area: Build, CI and tests
- Type: build / CI
- Problem / reason: Chao, 2026-10-06: with another backend holding 8001, `pnpm dev`'s backend failed with "[Errno 48] Address already in use" and the page showed "Can't connect to Wordflow" (Safari "Load failed"). The backend never moved ports, and Vite (strictPort off) could drift from 3000 to 3001 while the backend's CORS list still allowed only 3000. The desktop app was unaffected (its backend binds port 0).
- What changed:
  - `scripts/dev.mjs`: `resolveDevPorts(mode, env, isFree)` runs before anything starts. In `web` mode (both halves) each default port (8001, 3000) moves to the first free port up to 19 above, printed as `backend: port 8001 is in use, using 8002.`; both ports go to both halves (backend `--port` and CORS origins; frontend `FRONTEND_PORT` and `VITE_BACKEND_PORT`). A port set by environment, or a half started alone (`--backend`/`--frontend`), never moves and stops with a plain message naming the variable.
  - `portIsFree`: connection test on 127.0.0.1 and ::1 plus bind test on 127.0.0.1 and ::. The connection test is required: on macOS a bind to 127.0.0.1 succeeds beside Vite's 0.0.0.0 listener.
  - `frontend/vite.config.ts`: `strictPort` also when `FRONTEND_PORT` is set.
  - `docs/runbooks/development.md`: port section.
- Expected behaviour (testable):
  - With a `pnpm dev` already on 3000/8001, a second `pnpm dev` prints both moves and serves on 3001 with the backend on 8002 (checked on macOS).
  - `VITE_BACKEND_PORT=8001 pnpm dev` with 8001 busy stops at once with a message.
- Implementation: `scripts/dev.mjs`, `scripts/dev.test.mjs`, `frontend/vite.config.ts`, `docs/runbooks/development.md`.
- Tests: `scripts/dev.test.mjs` (moves, explicit ports, single half, search limit) with an injected port checker; manual run beside a live `pnpm dev`.
- v0.8 notes: DECISION FOR ALEX: main's dev launcher (Rust `wordflow-api-dev` host plus Vite) may have the same fixed-port and CORS-origin coupling. If so, the same approach applies: resolve both ports before starting, pass them to both halves, keep explicit ports fixed, and make Vite strict. Not ported; Alex to decide.
